(The mirror, the bundle and the push verify each file's SHA-256 before using it, so a planted link cannot make them copy something else.)
- **Early quota check, upload cap.** Before streaming, an upload is refused (413 `quota_exceeded`) if its declared length, or one byte when none is declared, does not fit the owner's, the person's or the instance's remaining quota; the check in the transaction still decides the rest. Because a repeat of a file cannot be told from a new one before it is read, a repeat is also refused at the limit; the existing attachment is there to link to. A person may have `SCRIOBH_UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY` (3) uploads in flight; the next is a 429.
- **Images by structure.** PNG needs the signature and an IHDR first chunk of length 13; JPEG the start marker and another marker; GIF the header and a non-zero logical screen; WebP `RIFF`, a size field equal to the file less 8 bytes, `WEBP` and a `VP8 `, `VP8L` or `VP8X` first chunk that fits; PDF `%PDF-` and a version. Forged and truncated files are refused (tests).
- **Restoring a batch.** A restore reparents every restored note whose folder is still in the trash, in one statement over the batch, so no live note is left under a trashed folder (F > G > K, trash G, trash F, restore K).
- **The event hub restarts safely.** `Subscribe` waits for a loop the last unsubscribe stopped, and `deliver` never delivers at or before the cursor, so a stop and a start racing cannot run two loops, deliver twice or move the cursor back (a churn stress test under the race detector).
- **Scratchpad and keys.** Restricting `scratch.*` to browser sessions is being done elsewhere and is not in this tree yet; once it lands, `internal/server/research_test.go` should assert a key is refused.

## Phase 3.5 alternatives decisions

The design of manual alternatives, ghosting and the stash, as built (backend only; the screens are the UI agent's, see EDITOR-SPIKE). It extends the Markdown decision (§25) and the ghost decision (§26) and replaces ARCHITECTURE's first sketch of the comment, which had no ids and no way to find an anchor again.

**One source of truth, and what that means.** `documents.content` is the body followed by at most one trailing line, `<!-- scriobh:alts {json} -->`. The tables `alternatives` and `alternative_variants` are the canonical store; the comment is their serialisation, written by the server. The invariant, stated so a test can check it: *after every committed write to a document, `Serialize(tables of the document)` is byte-equal to the comment that ends `documents.content`; the body before it holds the chosen wording of every `active` alternative at its anchor (`body[start:end]` is the chosen variant's text); no two anchors overlap; each alternative has exactly one original; and a document with no alternatives has no such comment.* A save that would break it is rewritten, not refused: the server canonicalises the comment line and stores that, so a client treats the response as authoritative for the last line (EDITOR-SPIKE has the contract). `TestInvariantAfterEveryOp` drives every op and save path at random, seeded, and checks it from the database after each step; `alt_save_test.go` pins the cases by hand.

Every path that writes a document's text goes through the same reconciliation (`altstore.Reconcile` and its pure half `alts`): `doc.update`, `revision.restore`, `doc.split`, `doc.create`, the importer (`docs.Prepare` and `InsertTx`), and the alternatives, ghost and drag-back ops. There is no other `UPDATE documents SET content` or `INSERT INTO documents` (checked by grep).

**What a save does with the comment** (`doc.update`, in the transaction):

1. The text is split into body and comment (`alts.Split`: the comment is the last non-blank line, begins `<!-- scriobh:alts ` and ends ` -->`; the exact whitespace around it is kept, so an unchanged comment is byte-stable). Line endings become LF when a document carries alternatives (the only text this changes).
2. **No comment, but the document has alternatives:** they are kept, re-anchored to the new body, and the comment is added back. A client that knows nothing of alternatives (a script, the CLI, an agent) cannot lose them by saving plain Markdown. Removing them is `alternative.delete`.
3. **A comment that says the same as the tables** (same items, scope, and wordings with their ids, text and times; anchors, contexts, the authors and the chosen wording are not compared): the tables stand and are re-anchored. A stale copy held by an editor is harmless, including one that predates an in-place edit the server adopted (the chosen wording is the server's to keep).
   **An older copy is not an instruction.** The comment carries `sig`, a fingerprint of what it says (items, scope, each wording's id, text, kind and time). A comment that differs from the tables but whose sig agrees with its own content has not been touched by hand: it is a copy the server wrote earlier (a cached client, "keep mine" re-sending what it loaded). It is not applied, so it cannot remove a wording added since; `doc.update` answers with a notice saying so, and the comment that now ends the text is the server's. A comment whose sig does not agree (edited in the Source view, which does not update it) or has none (hand-written) is applied as an edit (rule 4).
4. **A comment that differs** (hand-edited in the Source view): it is applied as a whole. Items are matched by id; an unknown id is a new item (it gets ids of its own, since an id in a comment could be another document's, and is placed by its text); an item missing from the comment is resolved (deleted); wordings are matched by id and a new one is added. Refused with a 422: changing an item's scope, changing or removing an original that is not the chosen wording, a duplicate wording, a breach of the limits. The chosen wording is the tables' to keep: a comment that rewords or drops it is not applied for that wording, because *the body is the truth about which wording is chosen and what it says* (so an original that is chosen cannot be changed through the comment either).
5. **A trailing alternatives line that cannot be used** (not valid JSON, or valid but refused by the checks: a wording with a blank line, a duplicate, a limit) is a 422 on `doc.update`, naming the problem and the way out (fix or delete the last line), unless it is byte-identical to the last line already stored and there are no alternatives behind it (then it is an ordinary comment, as it was, and the document keeps saving). A last line with no alternatives behind it is never touched by an op: `ghost.create`, `alternative.create`, drag-back and `doc.split` leave it in the text, and a new comment goes after it. On every other path (a new document, an import, a restore) it is kept as an ordinary comment: such a document is never unsaveable and nothing is guessed. A missing `scope` (the first sketch had none) is inferred from the quote; a comment with no items is dropped.
6. Concurrency is the existing one: `base_revision` must match, so a stale editor cannot overwrite alternatives that changed after it loaded; the single-writer lease applies when a `tab_id` is sent.

Every alternatives op rewrites the comment, so it changes `content`, `content_hash`, `revision` and `updated_at` like any save. It returns the new revision, hash, word count and comment line, and, for a choice, the range it replaced.

**The destroyed-text rule.** Anything that removes or rewords a wording (`alternative.delete`, `remove_variant`, `edit_variant`, an item dropped or a wording rewritten through the comment, a revision restore) first records the text that held it as a revision, ignoring the autosave throttle. `TestRemovedVariantTextIsKeptInHistoryInsideTheThrottleWindow` proves it inside the five-minute window.

**The anchor.** An alternative's anchor is a range of the *body* (the text before the comment, LF line endings) plus the evidence to find it again: `anchor_start` and `anchor_end` in UTF-16 code units (JavaScript's string index, the measure `doc.split` and `mention.list` use; an emoji is two, a CRLF in the input is one), `prefix` and `suffix` (up to 24 characters of the Markdown before and after) and a hash. The quote is the chosen wording's text, stored once. Ranges are over the Markdown *source*, not the rendered text: a selection that spans emphasis includes its asterisks. A client that cannot compute offsets (the editor) names the text by its source plus context (`quote`, `before`, `after`, `occurrence`), and the server resolves it by exact search; an offset is another way to say the same. Anchors do not overlap and do not nest: a word alternative inside a sentence alternative would be destroyed by cycling the sentence, so that is refused (409) rather than invented. Alternatives are not available over a range that holds a ghost tag.

**Re-anchoring** runs on every save that changes the body of a document with alternatives (`alts.Rebase`). The comment holds no offsets (so typing away from an alternative leaves the line byte-identical and nothing churns in history, the mirror or Git); the tables do.

1. The common prefix and suffix of the old and new body bound **one edit window**. An anchor wholly before it is unchanged and one wholly after it shifts by the length difference: exact, no guessing. An insertion exactly at an anchor's edge is outside it (typing after a word does not extend the alternative). An op that knows its edits (choose, ghost, drag-back, split) passes the edit list and nothing is searched.
2. An anchor the window touches is looked for: `prefix + quote + suffix` exactly (nearest to where it should be; two equally near is no answer); then, inside the region the edit rewrote, with shorter context (24, 12 and 6 characters before the quote, then after it), accepting a hit only when it is the only one there; then a passage of 24 bytes or more that occurs exactly once in the whole body (the same passage, moved). Never the quote alone: the same word elsewhere is another word.
3. If the quote is gone and every edit that touched the anchor lies inside it, the writer edited the wording in place: the anchor stays active over the new text. If the new text equals one of the wordings, that one is chosen; otherwise the chosen wording takes the new text, unless it is the original (immutable), in which case the text becomes a new manual wording, chosen. An edit that would carry a ghost or alternatives marker, a blank line, or break the limits orphans the anchor instead.
4. Otherwise the alternative is `orphaned`: it keeps its wordings and last anchor, is listed (`alternative.list` with `status=orphaned`: the lost-anchors list), appears in the comment with `"orphan":true`, and is never moved by a guess. It is re-attached when its exact text and context (or a unique long passage) come back (an undo, a restore), or by `alternative.reanchor`, which attaches it to a range: if the text there is a wording it becomes chosen, otherwise it is added as one. Nothing is dropped silently.
5. After placement, overlapping anchors are resolved by orphaning the later one, and every active anchor's context is recomputed from the new body.

Cost is linear: one prefix and suffix scan, a few string searches per touched anchor, no general diff. A save that rewrites several places at once (a find-and-replace by a script) can orphan an alternative whose surroundings changed in two ways; that is the price of never guessing, and the list says so. Measured: a 20,000-word chapter with 500 alternatives (a 380 KB document) saves in about 25 ms when typing at the top moves every anchor, chooses in about 13 ms and lists in about 2 ms (`TestAlternativePerformance`; budget 250 ms, scaled under the race detector).

**The comment format (version 1).** One line of JSON written by Go's encoder with HTML escaping (so `<`, `>` and `&` are `<`, `>`, `&`: a wording can never close the comment) in a fixed key order:

```
<!-- scriobh:alts {"version":1,"sig":"…","items":[{"id":"alt_…","scope":"word","before":"The ","after":" house was","chosen":"alv_…","variants":[{"id":"alv_…","text":"quiet","original":true,"at":"2026-11-13T10:00:00.000Z"},{"id":"alv_…","text":"still","at":"…"}]}]} -->
```

`sig` fingerprints what the comment says (rule 3 above); `before` and `after` are the context; `orphan` is present and `true` only for a lost anchor; `source` is absent for a manual wording and `"generated"` for text suggested by a configured provider (Phase 4); `at` is when the wording was added. The comment carries no author: who wrote a wording is in the tables (and the API), so the line says only what the tables say about it and a user id never leaves the instance in an export or a Git push. A wording read from a comment is the importer's; one that already exists keeps its author across a restore. Items are in document order (orphans last), wordings in cycle order. The reader also accepts the short hand-written shape of the first sketch, `{"scope":"word","quote":"quiet","variants":["still","low"],"chosen":"quiet"}`: the quote is the original, the strings are manual wordings, items with the same quote are placed in reading order, and the writer then emits the long form. Importing or restoring gives every item new ids on import (a new document must not reuse another's) and keeps them on a restore. A set too large for one comment is refused with a message: the sidecar `.alts.json` named in §25 is not built.

**Scope** (checked against the parse, not by guessing): `word` is a run with no whitespace, cut at word edges (Han, Kana and Thai have no spaces, so any cut is allowed there), at most 100 characters; `sentence` lies inside one paragraph or heading (a quote's or list item's own lines too, but not across their markers), trimmed and at word edges, at most 4,000 bytes; the server does not segment sentences, the caller chooses the range; `paragraph` is one whole top-level paragraph or heading, from its first character to its last. Every range lies in one text block of prose: not in code, front matter, a table, raw HTML, the comment or across blocks. Wordings are trimmed, non-empty, hold no blank line, no `scriobh:alts` and no ghost tag, are unique within an alternative, and a word's is one line; a paragraph's parses as a single paragraph. After a choice the blocks around the change are re-parsed and the choice is refused if a heading mark, a list marker or a blank line would change the structure.

**Cycling is an edit.** `alternative.choose` replaces the range with the wording's text in one transaction: the anchor's end moves, every later anchor shifts by the exact difference, the comment is rewritten, the search text, links, mentions and attachment references follow through the save hooks, the previous text is kept as an automatic revision if one is due, and the new text is recorded with reason `alternative` (the note names the scope and the place in the cycle, never the text). It takes `variant_id` or `step` (`next`, `previous`, `original`; it wraps). Goals: the words are not new, so it earns nothing (as for a restore); ghosting, reviving and drag-back likewise; typing and `edit_variant` do. The goals diff reads the body only, so the comment's ids and times never count as words. Undo is another choice.

**Ghosts live in the Markdown.** A ghosted range is wrapped in `<scriobh-ghost>` and `</scriobh-ghost>`: valid inline HTML, shown as plain text by every other renderer (a custom element has no style), and held by the editor as two inline source atoms, so it round-trips with no parser change (spiked first: a whole paragraph, a heading, a list item, a soft break and emphasis; only the soft break normalises, to a space, as everywhere). A custom tag, not an HTML comment, because a line that begins with a comment is an HTML block in CommonMark and would make the paragraph a source block. Pairing is the private-note rule, per text block: the first opener and the next closer form a ghost; an opener met while one is open and a closer with none open are inert; an opener left open ghosts nothing. A ghost never starts or ends on whitespace, and `ghost.create` takes one range per paragraph. Everything between the tags (text, wiki links, hard breaks) is left out of the word count, the search text and mention matching, and stays in the exports by default (`ghosts: keep`); `omit` removes the text and the tags and mends the comment, and HTML never shows the tags. `web/editor/src/count.js` implements the same rule, and the shared corpus checks the two against each other (the bundle was rebuilt for that one change). The `ghosts` table is a derived index (id, range, hash, who and when, kept across saves by matching the text) rebuilt on every save: the Markdown is the truth, so an API client's edit near a ghost cannot desynchronise anything. A ghost may contain an alternative; it may not start or end inside one (409), and an alternative may not be made over a ghost tag. `doc.split` refuses a cut inside a ghost.

**The stash** is project-scoped (`stash_items`: title, Markdown, kind `paragraph`, `note` or `outline`, tags, optional source document, order, trash, a revision for concurrency). It needs the editor role for every op, reads included: unused paragraphs are the writer's cut material, and a beta reader who can read the manuscript does not thereby read what was cut. It has its own external-content FTS table (`stash_text` and `stash_fts`, migration `1420`, kept in the same transaction and rebuilt by `search rebuild`), is not part of any document, count, outline, export or the manuscript search, is part of backups, and is written to `stash/` in the mirror (never in the manifest, so never in a Git push; a test pins `pushable("stash/…")` false). `stash.drag_back` inserts an item into a document through the same save path as `doc.update` (revision-checked, lease honoured, history kept, no goal credit), only when asked: `block` puts it in as its own paragraph before the block that starts at the position or after the one that contains it, `inline` puts one paragraph in at a position; `copy` leaves the item, `move` trashes it. A position inside code, a comment or an alternative is refused.

**Splitting, restoring, trashing.** `doc.split` accepts a document with alternatives when the cut lies outside every anchor: the comment is divided between the halves (the tail's alternatives get new ids) and the cut is refused, with a message naming the alternative, when it is inside one, inside a ghost, or inside the comment. A revision restore makes the alternatives what the restored text's comment says (a restored text with no comment has none), keeps the replaced text, comment included, first, and earns no credit. A trashed document keeps its alternatives; ops on it are a 409. A project purge removes every row (cascades, tested).

**Hardening after review.** Each of these has a test that fails without it.

- **Ghosted text never leaks.** `ghosts: omit` strips the body first and mends the comment only if it can be read (valid, hand-written without ids, or over the limit: all three are handled); it is never a reason to leave the text in.
- **A save cannot be made slow by what is searched for.** One `Rebase` searches at most `alts.ScanBudget` bytes (96 MB, a few tens of milliseconds) across all its anchors; an anchor that has not found its text when it is spent is orphaned (`Report.Exhausted`), never guessed at. An orphan is looked for only where the text changed (the window the edit wrote, plus the reach of its context), and a hit there is accepted only after one scan shows it is the only one. Measured: 200 orphans in 1.5 MB, 1.4 ms for typing at the top, 3 ms in the middle, 63 ms for a rewrite of the whole text.
- **Re-attachment is never a guess.** If an edit removed all of an anchor's text and put nothing there, the alternative is orphaned at once. A partial match needs at least 12 characters of context.
- **Every placement is checked like a new anchor.** An item placed from a comment (a hand-written one, an import, a restore, a Source-view edit), found again after a move, or re-attached, passes `CheckRange` (word edges, prose not code, a whole paragraph for that scope, no ghost tag, no cut character) or is orphaned. A quote inside another word, or in a code block, is not placed.
- **No range touches a marker, and none cuts a character.** Any range that holds or begins or ends inside a ghost tag or `scriobh:alts` is refused (create, reanchor, ghost). Word edges are grapheme edges: a range may not cut a ZWJ sequence, a skin-tone modifier, a variation selector, a keycap or a flag, or a combining mark.
- **Typing on at an anchor's edge belongs to the word.** An insertion exactly at the end (or start) of an anchored word that continues the word ("quiet" to "quiets", "equiet") is adopted as the new wording, so cycling never gives "stills".
- **One revision per autosave interval.** Retyping a non-original chosen wording in the text is ordinary history (the usual throttle); the unthrottled safety revision is for a wording that is actually removed or reworded through the comment.
- **The importer uses the configured limits** (`SCRIOBH_MAX_ALTS_PER_DOC`), and a comment that cannot be used is kept as text with a warning in the preview and in the apply report.
- **One canonical comment.** Text after the comment, or a second comment, is collapsed: every comment line that reads as one (outside a fenced code block) is taken out of the body and the last stands. A document that merely contains the marker string (a code sample) is not rewritten, and keeps its line endings, until a comment that reads as one is in it.
- **A clean Markdown export** is `alternatives: omit` on `export.markdown` (the bundle too): the chosen text alone, no comment; `ghosts` keeps its own option.

**Not built.** The editor decorations, panel, context menu, stash panel, sounds, animation and `a/an` adjustment (UI work). The sidecar `.alts.json`. Cross-project `stash.move` (copy through `get` and `create`; a pinned key cannot name two projects). Stash history beyond the revision counter (F-433's "versioned" is partial). Nested alternatives. Sentence segmentation. Wordings written by a provider (the `generated` source is accepted and stored, nothing creates it). Per-file Markdown import limits do not read `SCRIOBH_MAX_ALTS_PER_DOC` (the bulk importer uses the default 200).

## Phase 4 AI core decisions

The provider layer, the policy, the context builder and its preview, the run pipeline, budgets and the run log. Code: `internal/netguard`, `internal/ai` (adapters, framing, estimates, the recipe registry), `internal/ai/redact`, `internal/domain/ai` (ops, policy, context, pipeline, budgets), `internal/server/ai.go` (the stream route), `internal/config/ai.go`; migrations 0600, 0610, 0620. Rows F-620 to F-626 in FEATURES § AA. Where this departs from [AI.md](AI.md), AI.md has been corrected.

### The seam for recipes and screens

- **A recipe is code**: `ai.RegisterRecipe(ai.Recipe{...})` from an `init` (internal/ai/recipe.go; `internal/ai/freeform.go` is the worked example). It declares id, version, the policy action that allows it, the result kind (text, notes, marks, json), the scope it needs (none, selection, document, project), default chips, parameters (validated, unknown ones refused), an output ceiling and a model class, and two functions: `Build(BuildContext) (Prompt, error)` and, optionally, `Parse(ParseContext) (Parsed, error)`. It never sees the database, a provider, the policy or a key: it receives validated parameters and chips the server already loaded and authorized, and returns instructions and which chips to use. A parse that fails is not an error: the raw text and a warning come back and nothing is applied. Recipe ids must be lower case with dots, dashes and underscores (they travel in events and the run log).
- **A recipe that needs several calls** (a continuity audit over many chapters) is its own op that calls `(*ai.Service).Run` once per call, with `ai.ServiceOf(env)`; every call is authorized, previewed, budgeted and logged on its own. `Service.Preview` and `Service.Run` are the two entry points the ops wrap. `ai.WithStream(ctx, sink)` makes a run deliver text to a `StreamSink` as it arrives.
- **Chips** (`ChipSpec` in, `ai.Chip` to the recipe): selection, sentence, paragraph, document, previous summary, previous document, synopsis, brief, style guide, entities (by id, or `auto` for those named in the text), research, outline, instruction. `chips` omitted means the recipe's defaults; `chips: []` means none. The previous chapter's summary is its `synopsis_long` (else `synopsis_short`): rolling summaries have no store of their own yet, so the summaries recipe should write `synopsis_long` through `doc.update`.
- **Event and audit vocabulary**: events `ai.run.finished` and `ai.policy.updated`; audit actions `ai.run`, `ai.run.blocked`, `ai.policy.set`, `ai.provider.*`, `ai.budget.set`, `ai.price.*`, `ai.settings.set`, `ai.exclude.set`. The event payload allow-list gained `run_id`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens` and `cost_micros`.

### The preview is the send

- **The hash covers the wire bytes.** The body of every provider that may receive the request (the chain, up to four) is built by one function, `Client.Encode`, from structs and never maps, and the preview returns those bodies as `attempts[].body`. `payload_hash` is an HMAC-SHA-256, keyed with a secret the server makes at start (so an editor, who is not shown an instance provider's address, cannot hash guesses at it, and a restart means previewing again), of the ordered set (provider id, kind, model, normalised address, local flag, scope, body, and the policy mode). `ai.run` rebuilds everything from the current data, compares the hash, and sends the very bytes it just built. A fallback goes only to an entry that was previewed. The test records what a fake provider receives and compares it with the previewed bytes, for all four provider kinds and for the stream.
- **Per-request random boundaries without a stateful preview.** The boundary is `SHA-256("scriobh-boundary:" + nonce + ":" + n)` shortened, where the nonce is 16 random bytes the preview returns and the run sends back, and `n` is the first counter whose boundary appears in none of the text. The preview stays stateless (so it is a dry-run-safe POST that writes nothing), the boundary is fresh for every preview, and a text that would contain it changes the bytes and so the hash, which is a 409, never a quiet re-roll.
- **`stream` and `max_tokens` are part of the request.** A stream sends `"stream": true`, so a preview for a stream must be made with `stream: true`; a mismatch is a 409. The output limit is clamped to the recipe's, the server's (`SCRIOBH_AI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`) and, per provider, the model's.
- **Every send is previewed.** `ai.run` refuses without a `payload_hash` and `nonce` (422). Redaction, policy and budget are decided inside the same build, so a preview can say "refused, and why" (`decision`) and the run refuses the same way.
- **What is in the preview and what is not.** Estimates use a documented heuristic (internal/ai/estimate.go: four ASCII characters a token, wider characters more, a little high on purpose); the provider's count replaces it after the run. The response holds manuscript text, so `ai.context.preview` and `ai.run` are `SecretOutput`: they are never kept for idempotent replay (a repeat with the same key is a 409). `ai.run` is `NoDryRun` (it spends money); the preview is dry-run safe.

### Departures from AI.md

- **Fallback and retry are automatic, within what was configured.** AI.md §9 and §11 said never to switch silently and never to retry. The roadmap asks for fallback chains, so: a project's chain (or the instance default) is a list the owner or administrator chose, shown in full in the preview with each provider's exact body; a timeout, a 5xx, a rate limit or an unreachable provider is retried once against the same provider (`SCRIOBH_AI_RETRIES`, with a short backoff that honours Retry-After) and then moves on. A refusal by a key (401, 403), a request (4xx), credit (402), a policy or a budget never retries and never falls back. A failed call that never reached the provider is not charged; one that did is (see Budgets). A stream that has already delivered text is never retried or moved: it ends as an incomplete result.
- **Personal providers exist alongside instance providers** (AI.md: "per-user keys by default"). A household instance wants administrator-managed providers; a person may want their own key. Both are supported: scope `instance` (administrators) or `user`; `SCRIOBH_AI_USER_PROVIDERS=off` switches personal ones off. A personal provider is invisible to everyone, administrators included; the seal context differs (`ai_providers/<id>/user:<id>/api_key/<address>` against `.../instance/...`). A person's own provider is paid by that person: its runs are not charged against the instance's money caps (they count in tokens).
- **`SCRIOBH_AI_ALLOW_PRIVATE` and `SCRIOBH_AI_ALLOWED_HOSTS` are gone.** A provider is local because an administrator declared it (`local: true`), per provider. A writer cannot declare it, on any provider, so an editor cannot aim the server at its own network or the metadata address through `ai.provider.test`. Without it a provider is `PublicOnly` and must be https; with it, loopback, private and carrier-grade-NAT addresses are allowed and `http://` is too. Link-local (the metadata addresses), unspecified, multicast and reserved ranges are refused in every mode, and creating a provider at such a literal address is refused outright.
- **No `HTTP-Referer` by default for OpenRouter** (AI.md §2.1 sent the instance's URL): that would tell a third party where this instance lives. It is available as an allowed extra header; `X-Title: Scriobh` is sent. OpenRouter requests carry `data_collection: deny` unless the provider's routing says otherwise, and `usage: {include: true}` so the reported cost replaces the table.
- **Provider kinds are `openai_compatible`, `openrouter`, `opencode`, `ollama`** (DATA-MODEL had a hyphen in the first).

### The network guard (internal/netguard)

One classifier for Git sync and AI: public, local (loopback, RFC 1918, unique-local, 100.64/10 except the one metadata address inside it) and blocked. Three modes enforced at connect time on the address about to be dialled: `PublicOnly` (a provider not declared local), `RequireLocal` (a provider an administrator declared local, always, and every provider of a `local_only` project: a name that now resolves to a public address is refused, the check AI.md asks for "at send time, not just the flag") and `AllowLocal` (public and local, kept for callers that want both; Git sync uses it with its own opt-in, AI providers do not). The dialer resolves the name itself, drops every refused address, and dials the IP literal with the socket's own control hook checking again, so a DNS answer that changes (rebinding) is caught on the next connection. No proxy from the environment, redirects are never followed (a 3xx is an error), response headers are capped at 64 KiB, bodies at `SCRIOBH_AI_MAX_RESPONSE_KB`, with timeouts for connecting, the first byte, a stream's silence and a stream's length. gitmirror now calls `netguard.BlockedIP` instead of its own copy; its tests and behaviour are unchanged. Test seams: `Options.Guard.Classify` and `LookupIP` (a fake server on loopback can count as public, or as the LAN) and `Options.AllowPlainHTTP`; no configuration sets them.

### Policy and context

- **Modes**: `off` (the default; a project with no row is off), `ask` (available, always previewed) and `local_only`. Allowed actions are a set of flags (`selection`, `alternatives`, `chat`, `critique`, `summaries`, `continuity`, `extraction`, `lab`; the last two beyond the six the plan listed, for the Phase 4 alternatives and the Lab). Turning a project on without naming actions allows all of them. Providers and models allowed, chains per recipe, action or `default`, redaction (`off`, `entities`, `names`), `agents_may_use_ai` (default off), the owner's own monthly cap, and the style guide live in `ai_policies`. The owner sets it; editors read it.
- **"A per-project budget share"** is read as the owner's own monthly cap for the project, in tokens and money (`budget_cost_micros`, `budget_tokens`), alongside an administrator's cap on the same project. Both must have room.
- **Never-send flags.** `documents.ai_exclude` existed with no op to set it; migration 0610 adds the column to `research_notes` and `entities`, and `ai.exclude.set` sets any of the three (an editor for a document or the project's own note; editing rights on the Compendium for an entity or a shared note, since the flag applies to every book that uses it). A flagged item named in a request is refused in plain words; one that would come in by itself (the previous chapter, an entity found in the text) is left out and listed under `skipped` with the reason.
- **Access.** Documents, notes and entities are read inside the project; a foreign, trashed or invented id gets the same "not available" as one the caller cannot see. Entity sheets come from `entities.ReadableSheets`, the same visibility rule as every other read (Compendium membership; a key pinned to a project sees the primary Compendium only), so a person cannot put in what they cannot read. A scratchpad is never read by this code. Relationships and a book's private override notes are not in a sheet.
- **Redaction hides more than the caller can read.** `entities.ScopeNames` returns every name and alias in the project's scope regardless of the caller's access, because a name the caller cannot look up can still be in the text they are sending. The preview lists only the placeholders that stand in the request, so it names no entity the request does not mention; the numbering is by entity, so gaps in the numbers are possible. Only placeholders that were actually substituted into the request are restored: a model can echo any placeholder, and one that stood for nothing in this request must not become the name of an entity the caller cannot read (tested, streamed too). A restored name is the bible's spelling (a name written in lower case comes back capitalised). Capitalised mode (`names`) is a heuristic, not a guarantee: a name written only at the start of a sentence, and nicknames, are not found. Text that is itself shaped like a placeholder is the one case restoration can get wrong.
- **What is sent of a document**: the chosen wording only. The alternatives comment, ghost tags and ghosted text, and `%%private notes%%` (a half-typed one removes the rest of its paragraph) are removed. A document's title is sent only in the label of a chip that names it, and labels are redacted like text. A selection is named like an alternative (UTF-16 offsets with a base revision, or a quote with its context) and resolved on the server; a changed document is a 409.
- **Nothing is truncated.** A piece over `SCRIOBH_AI_MAX_CHIP_KB` is an error that names it; a request over `SCRIOBH_AI_MAX_REQUEST_KB` is refused before sending; a model whose listed window is too small is skipped, with the reason.

### Budgets, usage and the log

- **Money and tokens, monthly (calendar month, UTC), four levels**: instance, person, project (administrators) and the project owner's own. In micro-units of one currency (default USD, `ai.settings.set`); `SCRIOBH_AI_DEFAULT_USER_TOKENS` and `_COST_MICROS` apply to a person with no row.
- **Enforced before the call, in one writer transaction.** The check and the insertion of the run (status `running`, with its worst case reserved: the input estimate once for every send the chain allows, that is for each provider and each retry, plus the longest answer of any of them, at each provider's price; the preview shows it as `reserve_tokens` and `reserve_micros`) are one transaction over `ai_usage_daily` plus the reserved amounts of the running rows, so concurrent runs queue on the one writer and cannot all pass a cap only one fits under (60 concurrent runs against a cap of five: exactly five, tested; 1,000 runs in a loop stop at the cap and the sum matches). At the end the actual usage replaces the reservation in one transaction with the audit entry and the event. Runs left `running` by a crash are closed at start and any older than an hour when a run reserves. The reservation bounds what a run can settle at (each lost send is charged its input, and the reservation holds the input for every send), so the limit left is the estimate's own error: a cap is enforced on the estimate and corrected by the provider's count, so the month can end over by at most the difference between the estimate and the real count on the last run.
- **Money caps count what the instance pays for**: a provider an administrator added that is not local. A person's own provider and a local one count in tokens only (a local provider is charged zero even if an administrator sets it a price). A money cap meeting a paid model with no known price refuses the request (`price_unknown`): an unknown price is unknown, never zero. Prices come from the administrator's table (`ai.price.*`), then the provider's own list (OpenRouter), and a local provider with neither is free.
- **No foreign keys on `ai_runs` and `ai_usage_daily`**: purging a project or deleting a person must not reset a month's spend, and the usage record outlives what it describes. There is no op that deletes a run (AI.md §8 "Delete a run" is not built); the ledger would otherwise be editable.
- **The run log holds no prompt and no answer**: counts, hashes (the payload and the output), the chips by kind, label and size (an entity or shared note is named by kind only, since the log is read by a project owner who may not be in that Compendium), the provider and model, status, timing. Refused requests are logged as `blocked` with the rule that said no. A person's rate limit bounds how fast either kind of row can be written. **Transcripts (keeping sealed text for a few days) are not built.**
- **Rate and concurrency**: runs per person per minute (`SCRIOBH_AI_RATE_PER_MIN`, checked after the cheap validation so a malformed request does not spend it), runs in flight per person, calls in flight per provider (a busy one counts as a failure the chain can move past).

### Streaming, cancellation and output

- **`POST /api/v1/projects/{id}/ai/run/stream`** is `ai.run` with a delta sink in the request context (the way a raw upload body reaches its op), so authorization, policy, budget, audit and event are the op's. Events: `start`, `delta`, `done`, `error`; a failure before the first `start` is an ordinary JSON error. Closing the connection cancels the run and the cancellation reaches the provider (tested against a fake that notices); `ai.run.cancel` does the same from another request (own runs, or any run on a project you own). The non-streamed op is limited to `SCRIOBH_AI_SYNC_TIMEOUT_S` (50, under the server's 60 s write timeout); a long answer should use the stream, whose writes carry their own deadlines and a keep-alive comment.
- **Output is data.** Returned as JSON text, NULs and invalid UTF-8 cleaned, never HTML, never parsed as an instruction, never inserted anywhere by this code; a tool call in a provider's answer is ignored and shows up as an empty answer with a warning. No request to any provider shape carries `tools`, `functions`, `tool_choice` or `function_call` (a test encodes every kind, with and without stream and JSON mode, and a fake provider checks what arrives).

### What the sweeps needed

`TestKeySweep` panicked on a session-only op with no path parameter; it now makes one call for such an op (a small edit to a shared test). The skill test that keeps the word out of the skill's prose now skips each op's own description and the `scriobh-cli ai …` command lines, which carry the feature's name. The mirror coverage test lists every `ai.*` op and the reason it is not a mirror concern, and fails for a new one. Parameters named `before` and `after` are read by the CLI as references to a sibling, so the run log pages with `cursor`; `scope_id` became `subject` for the same reason. Dry-run fixtures drive the AI ops that can be previewed; `ai.run`, `ai.provider.test` and `ai.provider.models.refresh` are in the list that must refuse a preview.

### What was verified, and what a real run still has to confirm

All of it ran against fake servers in the test process. **OpenAI-compatible**: the chat completions shape, SSE with `stream_options.include_usage`, `/models`. **OpenRouter**: the same plus `provider.data_collection`, `usage.include`, the `usage.cost` field and model prices as USD per token strings, as the public documentation describes; a real run should confirm the cost field and that `-1` prices ("varies") are common on the auto router. **Ollama**: `/api/chat` with `stream`, `options.num_predict`, `keep_alive`, NDJSON, `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count`, `/api/tags`. **OpenCode**: the documentation names `GET /api/model` and `POST /api/experimental/generate/text` but gives neither body; the adapter's request (`{model, system, prompt, max_tokens}`), its tolerant reading of the answer (`text`, `content`, `output`, a bare string, plain text) and of the model list (an array or an object holding one, strings or `{providerID, modelID}`) are **assumptions**, isolated in `openCodeBody` and `parseModels`/`parseChat` in internal/ai. Its authentication is HTTP basic with user `opencode` and the key as the password (from the OpenCode server documentation). The OpenCode session routes are not used: a chat is flattened into one prompt for the one-shot route, and the answer arrives whole. Cancellation of a one-shot call drops the connection; whether the instance stops generating is unverified. **Also unverified against real services**: TLS to a provider with a private certificate authority (there is no way to trust one yet), OpenRouter's behaviour on `response_format` for models that lack it, and whether a provider that keeps a connection open after `[DONE]` is dropped cleanly (the client stops reading at `[DONE]`).

### Security review fixes

Thirteen findings from an independent review; all fixed, none disputed. Deferred: none.

1. **Configuration is session-only.** `ai.policy.set`, `ai.exclude.set`, `ai.budget.set`, `ai.settings.set` and `ai.price.set/delete` join the provider ops in refusing every API key (any scope, pinned or not): a prompt-injected agent could otherwise turn AI on, let agents in, lift a never-send flag and then run. `ai.run` and the preview stay open to keys only on a project whose owner enabled `agents_may_use_ai`. The key sweep's session-only branch covers the project ops, and `TestAConfigurationOpIsRefusedToEveryKey` drives each op with keys at every scope, pinned and not, owned by the owner and by an administrator.
2. **The preview lists only placeholders that were used** (`Map.UsedEntries`: put into a text by `Apply`), and a placeholder-shaped token already in the writer's text is remembered as literal and never restored, so `{{CHAR_1}}` typed into an instruction enumerates nothing.
3. **Names are matched on a canonical view of the text** (internal/ai/redact/match.go): NFC, any run of whitespace including line breaks and no-break spaces as one space, invisible characters ignored, case, and apostrophe and hyphen variants; each match replaces exactly the original characters. A primary name also matches as a URL slug (`maire-nolan`), as its own placeholder variant (`{{CHAR_1_u}}`) that restores to the slug, not to the name with a space.
4. **Personal providers need the owner's say-so.** Policy flag `allow_personal_providers` (migration 0690), off by default: with an empty provider list only instance providers are used; a personal provider is used when the flag is on or the owner named one of their own in the list. Default monthly caps are now **1,000,000 tokens and 5,000,000 micro-units (five units of the currency) per person** (`SCRIOBH_AI_DEFAULT_USER_TOKENS` and `_COST_MICROS`; 0 for none), and `ai.status` warns administrators when no cap exists anywhere. **A consequence worth knowing:** the default money cap applies to the instance's paid providers, and a model with no known price under a money cap is refused (`price_unknown`), so a new OpenAI-compatible, OpenCode or not-yet-refreshed OpenRouter provider is refused until a price is set (`ai.price.set`) or its model list carries one. `ai.status` says which providers (warning `unpriced_model`); a local provider and a person's own are not affected. Set the money default to 0 to run on token caps alone.
5. **Every send that reached a provider is charged**, answered or not: timeouts, cancelled or cut-off streams, 5xx, unreadable or oversized answers, and the discarded tries of a retry cost at least the input estimate (and an estimate of what arrived) at that provider's price; a bad key, a rate limit, a refused request or address, a redirect and a connection that never came up were stopped before any work and are free. When the provider reports usage for the answered call, its numbers are used for that call.
6. **The approval covers the destination**: the hash includes each provider's normalised address, local flag and scope, and the policy mode. A provider declared local is always dialled `RequireLocal`, in every project, so a name that starts to resolve to a public address is refused rather than treated as local, shown as local and charged as free.
7. **netguard**: IPv4 carried in IPv4-compatible (`::a.b.c.d`) and 6to4 (`2002::/16`) addresses is classified as that IPv4 address; Teredo, `fec0::/10`, `100::/64`, `64:ff9b:1::/48`, `192.88.99.0/24` and Azure's `168.63.129.16` are blocked. Git sync shares the classifier and its tests stay green.
8. **Accent-dropped names match too** (fix of a follow-up probe): the canonical view is decomposed with its combining marks removed, so "Maire Nolan" finds "Máire Nolan" (and over-matches an unaccented spelling of another word, the safe side). **Instance provider details**: headers, user name, keep-alive and address are for the people who manage the provider; the key hint (last four characters) only for keys of 20 characters or more, and only to those people.
9. **Test and refresh are no oracle**: for a person who is not an administrator, `ai.provider.test` and `.models.refresh` answer "could not be used" without a class or a status; both are rate limited per person (10 a minute).
10. **Early refusal**: `loadChips` keeps a running total and refuses as soon as the chips pass `SCRIOBH_AI_MAX_REQUEST_KB`, before reading the rest (so a request is an error, not a "too_large" decision, when the chips alone are over); previews are rate limited per person at four times the run rate.
11. **Selections** find private notes and ghosts on the whole body and cut them out of the slice, so a range that starts inside a `%%note%%` or a ghost carries none of it (the sentence and paragraph chips too).
12. **Log and settlement**: the server log has a provider failure's class and status only. An unreadable `settings` table fails closed (409, logged); only "no row" is default-on. A settlement write is retried once and then the run is marked `unsettled` (migration 0695 rebuilds `ai_runs` with the longer status list) keeping its reservation, which the budget counts as spent for the month the run began in.
13. **Output filter** (`cleanOutput`): every piece of provider output, whole or streamed, loses C0 controls other than newline and tab, DEL, the C1 range, U+200B to U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069 and U+FEFF, and invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD, before restoration, parsing or delivery.

### Not built

Transcripts, the egress log (AI.md §8), a model picker and screens of any kind, deleting a run, scheduled model-list refreshes (a refresh is a button: no request without a person), embeddings (Phase 6), and any default injection of the style guide: a recipe lists the chip it wants. (The actions and per-class defaults came with the recipes: next section.)

---

## Phase 4 recipes decisions

The recipes, what happens to their results, the accept ops, reviews, the Lab, summaries, chat, extraction, the style guide and the class defaults. Code: `internal/ai/recipes_*.go` (prompts and parsers, no database), `internal/ai/analyse` (the Lab's local analysers), `internal/domain/aiwork` (ops and what is kept), `internal/domain/docs/ai_write.go` (the write seam), the result hook in `internal/domain/ai/result_hook.go`; migration 0630. Rows F-185.. and F-640.. in FEATURES § AB.

### The seams added to the core

- **A result hook inside `Service.Run`**, after the run has settled (so it cannot touch the run record or the budget). A streamed run and a plain run therefore give the same result: the stream route is `ai.run`. The hook receives the restored text, the parsed data, the chips as loaded (before redaction), the document as the request saw it and the selection resolved on the server (UTF-16 range and source), and returns the shape a screen uses. If it fails the run still returns, with the text and a warning and nothing kept.
- **`ParseContext.Chips`**: a parser sees what the request carried, so it can refuse a citation that was not sent. Two new chip kinds: `summaries` (the synopses of earlier chapters, each as `[doc:ID]`) and `timeline` (events as `[event:ID]`, the deterministic check's findings as `[finding:ID]`, as far as the caller may read them, from `timeline.AIFacts`).
- **`docs.ApplyAIEdits`, `docs.AddGeneratedVariants`, `docs.UpdateTx`**: the write seam, taking a transaction, built on the alternatives session (anchors rebased, ghosts synced, hooks, goals, events). `doc.update` was split into a transaction part (`UpdateTx`) with no change of behaviour. `docs.LeasesOf(env)` hands the lease table to the layer when ops are registered (a dry-run view has no services, so it is captured by closure).
- **Class defaults**: chains may be keyed `class:cheap|strong|fast|local`. Resolution: the request's own provider, the project's chain for the recipe, the action or the class, `default`, then the person's class chain (`ai_user_defaults`), then the instance's. A recipe's class is its `Class` field.

### Accepting: the server holds the text

- **Held in the database for 30 minutes, sealed when the server has a key.** Memory would lose a suggestion in a restart and be wrong with a second process. The text is sealed with `SCRIOBH_SECRET_KEY` (`internal/secrets`, bound to the run and the person), so a backup or a copy of the database does not carry a suggestion in the clear; with no key the text is held as it is (`ai.status` already says `credentials_ok: false`). The row (`ai_pending`) is deleted at the first of: an accept or reject of its last suggestion, an expiry check (every read treats an expired row as absent), the purge every recipe run and accept performs, the hourly sweep the server runs (and once at start: `aiwork.Sweep`), the end of the person's membership or editing role (a trigger, 0640), the project, the document or the person. The core's "no transcripts" rule stands: the run log is unchanged; this is the explicit, short-lived exception, and it holds only what can be accepted.
- **What accept verifies**: the row belongs to the caller and the project and has not expired; the hash stored with it equals `ai_runs.output_hash` of that run (the core's own record of what the model said); each item's text equals its stored hash; the caller's optional `expect_hash` equals it (so the text reviewed is the text applied); the policy is on and allows the action, and an API key only where agents may use AI (a project whose AI was switched off since refuses, and so does the server's switch). The caller supplies no text at all.
- **Where it applies**: at the stored range when the revision is unchanged, otherwise where the original text is found again with ALL the context stored with it (the words around it in its paragraph, and a marker where the paragraph or the document starts or ends), exactly once; otherwise 409 `stale` (there is no fallback to "the only occurrence": a deletion or a rewrite must never land on a lookalike). `base_revision` must be the document's current revision (the usual 409 with `current_revision`). The write refuses a range that lies in or reaches into a private note, ghosted text or an alternative's anchor, a replacement that changes the document's blocks, and text with control characters, invisible or direction-overriding characters, `%%`, ghost or alternatives markers, HTML comments, or links, images and addresses the original did not have (see the review fixes below). Edge white space of the selection is left alone. A deletion takes one neighbouring space.
- **One transaction** covers the write, the history entry (reason `ai-accepted`, the previous text kept first, note `recipe, run id`, never text), the event, the audit entry and the ledger row. No goal credit (`NoCredit`), as for a restore. Accept is idempotent per run and suggestion: the ledger (`ai_accepts`, text-free) outlives the held row.
- **Continue** inserts after the selection (the text before the cursor): a space is put in front unless the text starts with a line break.
- **Alternatives**: `ai.accept.alternative` adds candidates with source `generated` to an existing alternative or to one made over the text the run was made for (its original stays chosen); `choose` names a candidate to put in the text in the same save (history reason `ai-accepted`, the note naming the alternative). Candidates are filtered by `alts.CheckVariantText` for the scope.

### Anchoring: never trust a position

A model's positions are never read. Notes and marks carry a quote and some words around it, and the server finds the quote itself. Two rules, one for what only points (critique notes, continuity findings, held selections) and one for what can be edited (Lab marks): a note or selection follows its quote when the quote has all its stored context or is the only occurrence; a mark must have ALL its stored context (same paragraph only), once, because a deletion must never land on a lookalike. Under redaction a restored name is the bible's spelling, so a quote written in lower case may not be found (the note is then `unplaced`). Marks the server made follow the edits an accept made (they shift with the text); marks after any other edit relocate by context or go `stale`, never guessed. When the model's own context is slightly off, a unique quote still anchors and an ambiguous one is dropped and counted.

### Critique, continuity, extraction: reviews

- Notes are bounded (60, 600 characters, one example of 160 flagged when cut); fields that look like replacement prose (`rewrite`, `replacement`, `suggestion`) are never read. Categories are a fixed list with aliases (anything else is `other`), severities low, medium, high.
- A continuity finding must cite at least one source that was in the request: an entity (by the block's `source=` id; a field is kept only if the sheet has it), a timeline event or finding, an earlier chapter or the target document. Others are dropped and counted. Entities come from `ReadableSheets` and the timeline from `AIFacts`, so the caller's own visibility rules apply; a finding that cites a sheet the caller cannot read is dropped. The deterministic `timeline.check` is unchanged and works with AI off.
- Reviews are **private to their author** (a 404 for everyone else, owners and administrators included), kept until deleted (the oldest beyond 100 a person per project go), removed with the project, document or person. Reading, resolving and deleting one's own kept results is not gated by the AI policy (the kill switch and, for an API key, `agents_may_use_ai` apply); accepting is gated by the policy as well. The Lab's local marks are listed and accepted with AI switched off.
- Extraction creates nothing. `ai.extract.accept` needs the Compendium to be one the project draws on and one the caller edits; a name or alias clash is the entity layer's 409 naming the entity (no merge is offered; the review shows `exists_as` for readable entities). Proposed fields the Compendium does not define are left out and listed.

### The Lab

- **Local analysers** (long sentences over 40 words, English hedge list, double spaces, repeated `,`/`;`/`:`/`..`, space before punctuation (not before `; : ! ?` in French), the odd form out between straight and curly double quotes) run in `ai.lab.analyse` with no policy, no provider and no cost, with AI off. They skip headings, code, URLs, wiki links, ghosted text and private notes. The hedge list is English; another language gets none. Running again replaces the person's open local marks of those kinds.
- **Model marks** come from `lab.convoluted`, `lab.tone` and `lab.trim` (modes filler, adverbs, repetition, dialogue_tags): the kind is the recipe's, never the model's. The plan's four trim steps (slight trim to cut in half) are not built as severities; the four modes are what exists.
- A mark with a fix is `delete` or `replace`; an observation can be accepted and then only means "resolved" (nothing is written). `accept_all` takes marks in document order, skips overlaps (earlier start wins, then the longer, then the lower id), skips stale ones and lists why, and writes one revision. `ai.lab.preview` returns the same plan with a word diff and writes nothing. Accepting model marks follows the policy; accepting local ones does not.
- Marks are private, superseded by a new run of the same kind, kept until accepted, dismissed or the document goes.

### Summaries, chat, style, defaults

- **Rolling summaries are client-driven.** Every send must be previewed and hash-bound, so a background worker cannot run one. The server keeps the plan and the position (`ai_jobs`: resumable with `ai.summary.rolling.get`, cancellable, skippable); the client asks for the next step, previews and runs it as any request, and accepts it with `job_id`. The plan previews the first chapter for real, estimates the whole job (the heuristic of the preview, an approximate cost scaled from the first chapter's price when known) and answers `decision.allowed=false` (not an error) when the policy, the providers or the monthly caps cannot cover it, creating nothing. Empty and never-send chapters are left out. One open job per person and project.
- **Chat** is held by the browser unless a chat is created (`ai.chat.create`): then it is stored per person and project (50 chats, 200 messages each, deletable). `ai.chat.send` previews through `ai.chat.preview` with the kept turns (the last 20); a streamed run is added afterwards with `ai.chat.append`, which checks that the answer's hash is the run's recorded output hash. A chat answer proposing wording for a selection in a ```replacement block is a suggestion accepted with `ai.accept.edit`; nothing else of a chat reaches the text.
- **Style guide**: `ai.style.get/set` write `ai_policies.style_guide` (the column the core already had), for the project's **owner** only (it is sent with every member's default requests, so an editor could otherwise steer other people's requests; editors read it, and the chip is labelled as the project's, set by its owner), up to 8,000 characters; creating the row does not switch AI on. It still reaches a provider only through the chip.
- **Configuration is for sessions.** `ai.style.set` and `ai.defaults.set` write the policy's own columns and the instance's chains, so like `ai.policy.set` they refuse an API key at every scope (tested). A key pinned to a project may use `ai.extract.accept` on that project's primary Compendium only, as everywhere else. The accept ledger is read per person too: after an accept, another person's request with the run id answers as an unknown id does.
- **Defaults** `ai.defaults.set`: project scope needs the owner, instance scope an administrator, user scope is one's own; a `local_only` project's chains and the `local` class name local providers only; every entry names a provider the person can use.

### Review fixes: what changed after an independent review

- **One set of rules for what may enter a manuscript** (`internal/text/aisafe.go`, used by the recipe parsers and by the write seam alike). *Invisible characters*: every Unicode format character (Cf, which includes the soft hyphen, U+061C, the direction marks and overrides and the tag block) and the line and paragraph separators and variation selectors other than U+FE0E and U+FE0F are removed from names, quotes, notes and phrases and refused in text that goes into a document; the zero-width joiner and non-joiner stay (emoji sequences, Indic and Persian scripts need them). The core's own output cleaning (merged separately) also strips joiners, so an emoji sequence in a model's answer arrives split; that is the core's rule, not this layer's. *Links*: a suggestion may keep a link, image or address the text it replaces already has, character for character, and add none; `javascript:`, `vbscript:` and `data:` addresses are refused always; a `[[wiki link]]` is refused unless the same one is in the original; a text that replaces nothing (a continuation, a summary, a sheet's fields) has no link at all. *Block structure*: no new heading, list, quote, fence, table, rule, indented-code or `:::` line unless the original had the same line; a continuation may add paragraphs only, and not into a list item or a quote; the block check fails closed when the parser cannot place the text.
- **Private notes and ghosts are hidden ranges** (`text.HiddenSpans`: merged, sorted, disjoint). The Lab's analysers skip them, a model mark inside one is not made, the accept planner skips one that is, and `docs.ApplyAIEdits` refuses any range that lies in or reaches into one, not only a range that holds `%%` or touches a tag.
- **Context is exact, with the paragraph's edges as context.** A held selection and a Lab mark are found again only with all the context stored with them (`paragraphContext`: the words before and after inside the paragraph, plus a marker where the paragraph or the document starts or ends), once. A whole-paragraph quote therefore no longer matches the same words typed inside another paragraph; an identical paragraph typed as a paragraph elsewhere is, by its text and context, the same place. A quote that repeats more than 20,000 times is not unique.
- **Dismissed marks stay dismissed** (and resolved observations stay resolved): a new run skips a mark with the same kind, quote and context. Only open marks count against the 3,000 cap; settled marks are kept 90 days and at most 1,000 a document.
- **The analysers run outside the writer**: `ai.lab.analyse` reads the document from a snapshot, analyses, then takes the writer to check the revision and hash and replace the marks; if the document changed it reads again (three times) and then answers 409.
- **Never-send reaches the timeline chip**: an event whose place or participant is a never-send entity, and a finding that names one, or a never-send chapter, is left out and counted in the preview's skipped list. (An event whose own title names a never-send thing cannot be told apart; the audited text is not searched for names.) The summaries chip already left out never-send chapters, and the entity chip never-send sheets.
- **Retention**: an hourly sweep (and one at start) removes expired held suggestions, the accept ledger after 30 days, finished jobs after 30 and abandoned ones after 60 days, settled marks after 90 and reviews with no open note after 180. Triggers (0640, 1530) delete a person's held suggestions, reviews, marks, chats and jobs on a project when their membership ends or they are no longer an owner or editor, and their continuity and extraction reviews when their access to the Compendium ends. A kept chat holds at most 2 MB, and a person's chats on a project 20 MB; the rolling plan reads sizes, not chapter text.
- **Smaller**: choosing an AI wording is recorded as `ai-accepted` (the note names the alternative); `ai.style.set` and `ai.defaults.set` are session-only (earlier); reading and settling kept results with an API key needs `agents_may_use_ai`; a note is capped in the parser (600 characters, an example 160, exactly) and in the database (a trigger); the held selection's context uses the same paragraph rule.

### Verified only against fakes, and not done

Everything ran against fake provider servers returning canned good, malformed, truncated and hostile answers; **no real model has been run.** What a real run has to confirm: that models follow the JSON shapes and the "notes only" and "mark, never rewrite" instructions well enough to be useful (the parsers tolerate and bound the rest, but quality is unmeasured); that `extract.*` works with `response_format` (it is the one recipe whose kind is `json`, so JSON mode is requested) on the chosen models; the token and cost estimates of the rolling plan against real counts; and the local analysers' sentence and quote rules on real prose in several languages (English only is tested). Not built: ghost-text continuation, outline and beat generation, interviews, brainstorming, image understanding, saved prompt recipes, hunk-level acceptance of one suggestion (the diff screen can offer whole suggestions or whole marks), a project-wide "AI alternatives" for several selections at once, an event for the screens when a review changes (a screen reads after each op), and the sweep's expiry is hourly, not exact (an expired row is never read in between).

---

## Phase 3 screens: decisions and deviations

What the Phase 3 screens decided where the plan was silent, and where they differ from it. The rows that say what is built are in FEATURES, section Y.

- **Names from the bible in the text (`mentions.js`, lazy).** The marks are decorations from `mention.list?spans=true`, never document content. The server's places are UTF-16 offsets over the stored Markdown; the editor holds the same text as a tree, so each place is turned into editor positions by reading the span's own text from the Markdown the editor has just serialised and finding it, in order, among the editor's prose (code blocks, `%%private notes%%` and wiki links held back as characters that match nothing, whole words only). If any span cannot be found, or the server says `spans_complete: false`, **nothing is drawn**: a wrong underline is worse than none. The ask is made 1.2 s after load, 2.5 s after a save, and when the tab becomes visible; never while keys are going down (it waits for 1.5 s without one) and never when anything is unsaved, and an answer is dropped if the text or revision changed meanwhile. Between asks, an edit drops only the marks it touched and moves the rest (a document diff, not a re-search). Line style carries the meaning: solid thin underline for a name, wavy when the same words could mean another entity; a wiki link stays dotted and a missing one dashed.
- **Click.** A plain click or tap on a mark shows the card (summary, kind, "This book's version", the other meanings) and moves the caret as ever; **Ctrl or Command with a click** opens the sheet. Taking over a plain click would make it impossible to put the caret inside a name. The caret resting inside a mark for 0.9 s also shows the card (never while typing); Escape closes it.
- **Toggle.** "Show or hide the underlined names from the bible" in the palette, remembered in `localStorage` (`scriobh:mentions`), not a server preference.
- **"In this scene" (`scene.js`, lazy).** A third tab of the inspector: point of view and place, each linked to the bible or free text kept and marked "Not linked to the bible"; the entities the text names with kind and the book's own summary; a plain sentence when some are left out (a Compendium the person is not on) and a calm "still looking" line, with up to six re-asks 3 s apart, while the index catches up. The pickers are a combobox over `project.entity.list` (characters for a point of view, places for a location). Unlinking keeps the text. The Details fields follow what the tab writes.
- **Where this appears.** A book's entity sheet lists the documents (reading order, snippet, count, first appearance, 20 a page) through `mention.by_entity` and `entity.first_appearance`; a Compendium's sheet lists the books through `entity.appears_in` and counts, never names, the ones the caller cannot open. While the index owes a scan the section asks again (up to six times, 3 s apart) and swaps only itself. A Compendium sheet with no books yet asks a few times too, because `entity.appears_in` has no way to say that a scan is owed for a project that has no rows yet.
- **Outline orders.** `/p/{slug}/outline?sort=timeline|pov` and the same fragment; the three orders are real links, so the page works without script, and `outsort.js` swaps the list in place. The order and the columns (story date, point of view, status, words) are remembered **in this browser** (`localStorage`, `scriobh:outline`), not per person on the server and not per project. F-102 asked for "per project"; a server preference would need a new key, which this round did not add. The flat orders draw no move tools and no drag grip, say so, and keep editing a synopsis, status or target; an open editor holds back a redraw. In the point-of-view order the groups come from `outline.get` (alphabetical by label, none last), and the page says when no scene has a point of view yet. Grouping by part or by period (F-104's other halves) is not built.
- **Live updates.** One stream a page, lazy (`live.js`), stopped while the page is hidden and resumed with `Last-Event-ID`. A change to the open chapter shows a bar: with unsaved words it only says so (saving will ask which version to keep, as for any conflict); with none it offers Reload or Keep mine. The tree follows after 1.2 s unless a row is being renamed or dragged. The outline redraws after 0.7 s, never during a drag. Not followed: the library tiles (there is no stream scoped to a person), and the Git status banner, which keeps its own polling because Git operations emit no events.
- **Carry-forwards.** Inspector fields are saved before sign-out (`beforeSignOut` waits on `inspector.flush`) and on tab close (a keepalive `PATCH` with the revision the page holds). Outline moves are queued and each is worked out from the list as the last one left it. An instance administrator who is not a member is a stranger on the pages (decided in "Phase 3 screens: review fixes").

---

## Screenshots in the repository

`docs/screens/phase-N/` holds a reviewable subset: desktop light, desktop dark and mobile light of each screen. The screenshot scripts under `scripts/` regenerate every variant (mobile dark and the Sepia, Slate and Moss spot checks) on demand; those are not committed, because a full set is more than 70 MB and every release would add it to the repository's history again.


## Phase 3 screens: review fixes

Decisions taken while fixing the independent review of the Phase 3 screens.

- **Drafts belong to a person.** Every unsaved copy a page keeps in this browser has the person's id in its key: `scriobh:scratch:{person}:{p|c}:{id}`, `scriobh:research:{person}:{p|c}:{id}:{note}` and the reading position `scriobh:read:{person}:{project}`. Signing out removes every `scriobh:(draft|held|scratch|research|read):` key, after asking when a scratchpad or research draft differs from what the server holds (the question compares the draft's text with the server's, as it does for a chapter). A session that ends without a sign-out leaves the keys. The scratchpad, research and reading keys carry their owner's id, so the next person on the browser never reads them. **Open gap:** a chapter's own draft (`scriobh:draft:{doc}` and `scriobh:held:{doc}`, written by `doc.js`, which is at its size limit) is keyed by the document alone, so a later member of the same book on the same browser can be offered the previous person's unsaved chapter text until a clean sign-out removes it; the key needs the person's id too, which means room in `doc.js` or moving the draft code into a lazy file. The sign-out question also counts another person's leftover drafts, because it compares every draft key it finds with what the current person's session can read. Other keys the Phase 3 scripts write are interface preferences, not text: `scriobh:mentions` (underline names), `scriobh:outline` (order and columns), `scriobh.read.scroll` (continuous scroll) and the manuscript tree's collapsed rows; they are shared by whoever uses the browser.
- **A Compendium in the trash is read-only, in one place.** The registry refuses every write op that names a Compendium with a 409 ("This Compendium is in the trash. Restore it to change anything in it.") while `trashed_at` is set. The ops that must keep working there are listed once, in `trashedOK` in `internal/ops/registry.go`: trash, restore, archive, unarchive, purge, export, the mirror's rebuild and membership. A new op is covered by default; `TestAuthzSweepCompendium` calls every Compendium op on a trashed one. Only an owner finds a trashed Compendium (everyone else gets the 404 they always got); its pages show a banner with Restore and draw none of the forms.
- **Archived is a shelf.** An archived Compendium stays writable, as an archived project does. Its pages carry a quiet "Archived" banner and an owner's Unarchive.
- **Addresses.** A Compendium's slug is unique per owner, so a person can read two with one slug. `/c/{slug}` is the address of their own, or of the only one they can read; any other Compendium whose slug is ambiguous among those they can read is `/c/{slug}~{last six characters of its id}`. The resolver accepts both; every page takes the address from one helper (`compAddresses`/`addressed` in `compui.go`), so tabs, cards, the project panel and redirects agree. Projects have the same collision (`projects.IDBySlug` prefers the person's own): the resolver accepts `/p/{slug}~{suffix}` now, but the links of the library and the project pages are still built from the plain slug, because a project's slug comes straight from `project.get` and `project.list` in the API; making those carry an address is an API change and is left for a decision.
- **An administrator who is not a member is a stranger on the pages.** The ops keep "an administrator acts as the owner" (the API and the CLI are how an instance is looked after). On the UI layer, any `/ui/projects|compendiums|docs|entities|timeline/events/{id}` route, `/timeline/events/{id}` and `/files/{id}` answers 404 to an administrator who is not a member of what it names, exactly as the slug pages do (`internal/server/uiscope.go`, one check in front of the mux). Administration has its own pages (settings, backups, people).
- **Merge shows what Merge does.** The preview carries a fingerprint of the plan the op returned; the Merge button sends it back, the plan is worked out again, and a different plan (either entity changed, or the form is older than the fingerprint) refuses the merge with a 409 and the current preview.
- **One-shot words.** The people page no longer prints text from its address: what the last action wants to say (a name, what was granted) is kept on the server against the session for two minutes and spoken once.
- **Names under the cursor.** "Open the entity under the cursor" is a palette command (Mod+K, or Mod+Shift+P inside the editor); no key is bound, because the free chords type characters on some layouts. A card that appears because the caret rests in a name is also spoken in the page's polite live region.
- **Live refresh.** The entity and research lists gather changes for 400 ms, ask once more if a change arrived while they were fetching, keep the focused row and the scroll, and answer a conditional request with 304 when nothing changed (lists carry an ETag and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`). `live.js` grows its wait until the server has really sent an event, and starts again when a page returns from the back-forward cache.
- **PDF preview.** The frame is opened only by a click, carries `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"` and `allow=""`, and has no `sandbox` attribute: Chromium's PDF viewer will not run inside a sandboxed frame. The file's own response is what protects it (`default-src 'none'`, no scripts, `frame-ancestors 'self'`); download is the first link.
- **The point of view and the place are written into the scene's text.** `scene.set_pov` and `scene.set_location` copy the chosen name into the scene's own point-of-view and location text, which is what the manuscript, the mirror and a Git push carry. A project member who is not on the Compendium therefore reads those names, in the scene's text field and in the Git manifest. That is intended (the person who chose them chose to put them in the manuscript); the scene tab says so beside the pickers.

---

## Phase 5 review and sharing decisions

Comments, threads, the review queue, suggestions, roles, share links and notifications, as built (backend only: the screens are the UI agent's). Code: `internal/domain/review` (the ops), `internal/domain/comanchor` (anchors, a leaf package so the document write path can call it), `internal/domain/share` (links and what they serve), `internal/server/share.go` (the public routes); migrations `0420` to `0460`. The seams added to existing code are small and listed below.

### Roles

- **Audited, not changed.** A table test (`internal/server/roles_test.go`) lists every project op that departs from "a GET needs the reader, anything else the editor" and why. Reviewer is the new level in between: `comment.*` (create, list, get, update, delete, resolve, reopen, reanchor, people) and `suggestion.create/list/get/withdraw`. Nothing else is open to a reviewer: not the queue, not accepting, not the stash, AI or Git.
- **A reviewer sees all comments and suggestions of the project**, their own and other reviewers' (a small group works in the open; a blind read is a share link per reader, below). A reader sees none and is never mentioned. A reviewer resolves, moves and withdraws only what they started; an editor or the owner any. Authors are named; a guest is "Guest: Name"; a removed account is "Removed user" and the text stays.
- **The sweeps know the difference between role and object.** Six ops check the thing as well as the role (`comment.update/delete/resolve/reopen/reanchor`, `suggestion.withdraw`): a caller whose role is enough but who did not write it gets 403 *after* the role check passed. `objectGated` in `authz_test.go` lists them, and only they may answer 403 to a caller the role admits; each rule is tested in `internal/domain/review`.
- **What readers can already read.** Unchanged and not narrowed here: a member who is only a reader can read research notes, tags, statistics, the revision history and, through `doc.get`, private notes and the alternatives annotation of the chapters, as before. That was decided in Phases 1 to 3 for people the owner added by name. A share link is far narrower (below). If the owner of a book wants a member who reads the manuscript and nothing else, that is a new role or a per-op change, and a question for the owner of the product, not for this backend.
- **`comment.people` is an exception to the owner-only member list**: it gives the mention picker the display names and ids of the owner, the editors and the reviewers (no emails, no readers), who all see the same threads and authors anyway. `@name` is resolved against that list only, so mentioning cannot be used to ask whether a stranger is a member.
