- **Keys.** A key pinned to the project reads comments at the read scope and writes them at the write scope (the comments are the key owner's); link management and notifications are browser-session only, so no key reaches them (`TestKeysReadCommentsWithReadAndWriteThemWithWrite`).
- **Members and §34 stay as they were.** `project.member.update` changes a role among editor, reviewer and reader (the owner's cannot change); the Compendium prompt, its defaults and its result are untouched; a removed member is off the project at once and loses the notifications of it.

### Comments

- **Anchoring is the alternatives anchoring, with three differences.** `alts.Rebase` runs once per save over all of a document's anchored threads (`AllowOverlap`: two reviewers may comment on the same sentence), with the scope `span` (any range inside one block of prose at character edges; `alts` itself refuses `span` as a scope of an alternative). The writer editing the commented words in place makes the comment follow the new words; `anchor_original` keeps what it was first made on. Quotes and contexts are Markdown source, offsets UTF-16 units of the body, as for alternatives.
- **In the save transaction, not lazy.** A read has no old body, so it cannot tell "the text shifted" from "a lookalike is where it used to be". `comanchor.AfterWrite` runs in the transaction of the four writers of `documents.content` (`doc.update`, `revision.restore`, `doc.split`, and the alternatives/ghost/drag-back/accept seam), costs one indexed query when the document has no comments, and is bounded: at most 1,000 anchored threads a document, and Rebase's scan budget. Measured (`TestReviewPerformance`, best of five, quiet machine): a save of an 80-paragraph chapter that holds 1,000 open anchored threads (the most a document may hold, all re-anchored and all moving at once) and 1,000 more comments 15.4 ms against 1.5 ms with no comments; 200 threads of a 2,000-comment project listed in 4.0 ms; the 40 threads of a chapter with plain hints in 4.8 ms; the queue over 2,000 threads in 6.2 ms. The budgets in the test are 80, 60 and 150 ms and 40 ms of added save time, scaled under the race detector.
- **Never a lookalike.** Beyond Rebase's own rules: a comment whose words stood in two places, and which an edit touched, is found again only where all of the context it had still surrounds it, else orphaned (Rebase would take "the one copy of this long passage left"); the same for a comment that was already lost and would be re-attached by a like passage: it comes back only for an undo or a restore that brings its surroundings back; a comment on the part of a document that `doc.split` moves is orphaned first (`comment.reanchor` with `document_id` moves a thread to the new document); a comment whose words become ghosted, or end up in a private note or an HTML comment, is orphaned. `TestAnchorInvariantAfterEveryWritePath` drives every write path at random and checks that an anchored comment always quotes exactly what it points at.
- **What an anchor may touch.** One block of prose; not code, a table, raw HTML, front matter, the alternatives line, a private note, ghosted text or an HTML comment, because the quote is stored and shown to everyone who can read the thread. Offsets are accepted only at the document's current revision (409 with `current_revision`); a quote is found at any revision and must be unambiguous.
- **A selection in a reading view.** The reading view shows rendered text, not Markdown, so `plain: true` says the quote and its context are as rendered. The server does not search the source for them: it searches **the visible text** (`text.PlainBlocks`: the prose of each paragraph and heading as the renderer's own parse reads it, whitespace as a browser shows it) and places the hit in the source through a map from every byte of that text back to the source. Front matter, private notes, ghosted text, code, link and image targets, a wiki link's target (it shows its label), raw HTML, HTML comments and footnote bodies are not in the visible text, so a selection can neither find, nor be made ambiguous by, nor confirm a phrase that exists only where a reader cannot see it. Formatting inside a selection is no obstacle (the range runs from its first source byte to its last, markup included); there is no widening to the paragraph any more. Several candidates are told apart by the words the reader saw around the selection, else the answer is "occurs in several places". The other direction: `comment.list` with `document_id` and `plain=true` gives each anchor as rendered (`quote_plain`, `before_plain`, `after_plain`, read only from inside the anchor's own block, with notes, ghosts and HTML comments cut out first). They are hints; offsets and quote are the anchor. A title-only save changes the document's revision but not an anchor's `revision`: trust `state`, not a revision comparison.
- **Text and limits.** Comments are plain text (the screen escapes it), up to 10,000 characters (2,000 for a guest), with line endings as LF, control characters refused and invisible and direction-overriding characters removed. Threads are one level deep; 500 live replies a thread, 20,000 live comments a project and 1,000 open anchored threads a document (1,000 resolved ones besides): a deleted comment is a tombstone, counts towards none of these, and the hourly sweep removes tombstones older than 30 days. One person may write 60 comments an hour on one project (a 429 that says so; a guest has the hourly limit of their link). Reword within 15 minutes (the author only); delete is a tombstone (a thread with nothing live is not listed). Comment search is a substring match in `comment.list` (`q`) for people who can read comments: there is no full-text index of comments, and comments are never in the document search.
- **Mentions** are members who can read comments, resolved when the comment is written (explicit ids or `@name`), notified in the app only. A guest mentions nobody.
- **Not document content.** Not in `documents.content`, search, word counts, exports, the mirror (its poll ignores `comment.*`, `review.*`, `share.*` and `suggestion.*` events: `TestReviewDataIsNeverMirrored`), a Git push (`pushable` refuses any such path), or an AI context. In backups (the database file) and removed with the project (cascade, tested). Events carry ids and counts only (`comment_id`, `thread_id`, `suggestion_id` joined the allow-list); the stream delivers review events only to people who can read comments.

### Suggestions

- **A row beside the text, accepted through the save seam.** `docs.ApplyAIEdits` became `applyEdits` with a policy (reason, the wording of its refusals and the label of the kept revision), so an accepted suggestion of a person passes exactly the checks an accepted AI suggestion does: the revision, the lease, a range in or reaching into a private note, ghosted text, an alternative or a marker refused, the replacement text held to one filter (no markers, HTML comments, control or direction-overriding characters, or links the text did not have; `docs.CheckInsertion`), the structure of the text kept. `suggestion.create` runs the same checks without writing (`docs.CheckReviewEdits`), so what can never be applied is refused when proposed. The revision reason `suggestion-accepted` needed a table rebuild (`0420`, with an upgrade test that keeps history).
- **Goals: no credit.** The words are a collaborator's, and accepting them is an editing decision, not writing, so an accepted suggestion earns the acceptor no words added (the same rule as an accepted AI suggestion). Typing the same words later does.
- **Stale handling** is the rule for AI accepts: at the revision the suggestion was made on, its stored range; otherwise each change is found again with all the paragraph context stored with it exactly once (an insertion: its context either side exactly once, and not less than 12 characters), else the whole suggestion is a 409 `stale` naming the changes, and nothing is applied. A suggestion applies whole or not at all. The helpers `LocateIn` and `ParagraphContext` moved from `aiwork` to `alts` so both use one.
- **`suggestion.accept_all`** takes the open suggestions of one document: stale ones, ones that fail a check now, and ones that overlap an earlier one are skipped and listed with a reason (the suggestion whose first change starts earlier wins, then the one made first); the rest apply as one revision. `preview: true` returns that plan and writes nothing.
- A suggestion by a guest of a share link is not offered: a link's scopes are read and comment.

### Share links

- **Not registry ops, and why.** A registry op's caller is a signed-in person: `Register` wants an `/api/v1/` path, `authorizeEarly` refuses a caller with no user, roles and key pins are about people, and the authorization sweep expects 401 from anyone anonymous. A link answers a stranger with a 404 that must look like every other. So the public routes are a table (`shareEndpoints` in `internal/server/share.go`) behind one entry point; the guest's write goes through `review.InsertComment`, the same function `comment.create` uses; the owner's side (`share.*`) *is* registry ops (owner, browser session only, so an API key of any scope is refused). The sweep over the public side is `TestNoPublicRouteLeaksAndEveryAnswerIsLockedDown`, which walks the table with every method and checks that the mux has no other `/s/` route, that nothing private is in any answer and that every answer has no cookie, no-referrer, noindex, no-store and the CSP; a new route must be added to the table to exist.
- **The principal** is a `share.Link`: what the link allows (scope, documents, ghosts, download) and nothing about a person. It has no session; the session and API-key middleware do not run for `/s/` (a cookie or key sent along is not looked at); nothing is set.
- **The token** is `shl_<ulid>.<43 characters of base64url>`: 256 bits of secret, shown once, only the SHA-256 of the secret stored, compared in constant time (`crypto/subtle`) after finding the row by id. An unknown id does the same comparison against a dummy hash. Garbage, a wrong secret, an unknown id, a revoked or expired link and a link of a project in the trash (or purged) all give one 404 with one body (`TestEveryTokenThatOpensNothingGetsTheSameAnswer`, the difference in time between a wrong secret and an unknown id is bounded by `TestWrongSecretAndUnknownIdTakeAboutTheSameTime`, skipped under the race detector). A link never used on another project's ids: every document id is checked against the project's own tree.
- **No oracle for hidden text.** A guest anchors a comment with a plain selection, found only in the visible text (see "A selection in a reading view"), so what happens to a guess does not depend on what is hidden: a phrase in front matter, a private note, a footnote only a note refers to, an HTML comment, a link or image target or a wiki-link target gets the same answer as one that is nowhere (`TestAGuestsAnswerDoesNotDependOnHiddenText`, `TestAPlainSelectionNeverSeesWhatIsNotRendered`). Whatever is wrong with a selection gets one 422 with one message (`TestAGuestCannotConfirmGuessesAboutHiddenText`); a member, who can read the notes, is told why. A reply to any id that is not a thread of the guest's own link (another link's, a writer's reply, another project's, one that does not exist) is the same 404, asked before anything else about the id (`TestAGuestCannotTellWhichCommentIdsExist`). The manifest does not carry the owner's label for the link.
- **What a link serves.** The manifest (title, contents in reading order, counts), a document as a fragment from the whitelist renderer (`text.RenderHTML`, local files off, headings shifted), comments, and the download if allowed. Never notes or research documents, the stash, scratchpads, settings, members, history, other links or anything AI. **Not a document the writer left out of the compile** (`include_in_compile` false), nor anything beneath one: a link shows what the book is, not scratch drafts or cut chapters; this holds in the manifest, the document, its comments and the download (`TestLinksDoNotShowWhatIsNotCompiled`). `ai_exclude` is about AI only and hides nothing here. Private notes, HTML comments, front matter and the alternatives annotation are not in it; ghosted text is left out unless the owner chose `keep` (then the words are shown and the tags never are); images in the text are notes (an attachment needs a session). The download is **not** the Markdown export (which keeps notes and the annotation verbatim) and is not a second sanitizer: `text.CleanMarkdown` is built on the renderer's own parse, so it cuts exactly what the reading view leaves out (front matter, private notes as the renderer pairs them, HTML comments, a footnote referenced only from a private note) and leaves every other byte, code blocks and spans included (`TestTheDownloadLeaksNothingTheReadingViewHides`). A text it cannot place is not handed out. Builds are cached for a minute by the link and the hash of what they were built from, and at most two run at once (a third is a 429): a stranger with a link cannot make the server parse a book again and again. A link that names documents shows them and their descendants; wiki links point nowhere. It either shows the whole manuscript or the documents it names (`whole` is a column, not "none named"): a link whose named documents are all gone, purged or trashed, shows nothing and answers the same 404.
- **Guests.** A comment link takes comments with a display name (60 characters, shown to the writer as "Guest: Name"), written through `InsertComment`: the same limits and notifications, 2,000 characters, no mentions. A guest sees only the threads started through their own link, and replies from the writer's side in those threads (by name, never an email); one link per reader keeps readers apart. Guests cannot edit or delete (nothing proves it is the same person), cannot suggest, cannot see another link's threads (a reply into one is a 404).
- **Limits.** Per client address first, before the token is looked at (garbage tokens spend only that; `SCRIOBH_SHARE_RATE_LIMIT`; an IPv6 address counts as its /64), per link after it is known (`SCRIOBH_SHARE_LINK_RATE`) and, for a passphrase link, only for callers who hold the proof, so someone with the token and no passphrase spends their own address's allowance and never the readers', 12 unlock attempts a minute per address, 20 comments a minute per link, `SCRIOBH_SHARE_COMMENTS_PER_HOUR` an hour and 500 in all per link (every refused attempt counts against the hour: a stranger feeding it guesses spends it), 50 live links a project, a 32 KB body. The same comment (name, text, place) sent again within a minute is a double submit and is answered with the first (200, `duplicate: true`). Use counts and last-use times are kept in memory and written in batches (at most every 15 seconds, on shutdown and by `share.usage`), so a public request never waits for the writer; the cost is that a crash loses up to 15 seconds of counts.
- **The passphrase** is a gate, not encryption: argon2id (19 MiB, two passes). Strangers' attempts share two hashing slots (a third is a 429); the owner's hashing when making or changing a link has two of its own, so neither waits for the other. Wrong passphrases are counted **per link and per client address bucket** (an IPv4 address, or an IPv6 /64, hashed): the first three are free, from the fourth each earns a wait before that bucket may try again, even with the right passphrase (30 seconds, doubling, at most an hour; 429 with `Retry-After`); a bucket quiet for a day starts again, a right passphrase clears its bucket, and a new passphrase clears every bucket. A stranger who keeps guessing therefore locks only their own address, never the readers (`TestUnlockFailuresAreCountedPerClientWithAnEscalatingWait`); the owner sees the total, the last try and how many clients are waiting in `share.usage`. Unlocking returns a **proof** (an HMAC over link, passphrase hash and expiry under a key derived from the instance secret; eight hours) which the client sends in the `X-Share-Unlock` header on every later request. It is not a cookie and nothing is stored: changing the passphrase, or revoking the link, ends every proof. A reading page that is navigated to (not fetched) cannot send a header: the screen keeps the proof in memory or session storage and fetches the pages' data.
- **Headers and logs.** `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow, noarchive`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, the standard strict CSP, no `Set-Cookie`. The token is in the path, so it is in no log line of this server (the request log records the route pattern; the two places that logged a path redact `/s/`: `TestATokenNeverReachesALogLine`). **A reverse proxy's access log will contain it**; set the proxy to leave `/s/` paths out or log them without the path. `GET /s/{token}` answers the manifest; it and `/api/manifest` each count a view.
- **Guest notifications are a digest.** What guests write in one thread within an hour is one notification for the owner with a count (`count`), not one a comment; notifications that came from guests are kept apart with a cap of their own (100), so a flood of guest comments never pushes a mention or a decision out of the 500 a person keeps (`TestGuestCommentsAreDigestedAndCannotEvictMentions`).
- **View limits were dropped.** An opening count that refuses later requests makes a link unreadable after its first page; counting views for the owner (`share.usage`) is kept.
- **Owner ops.** `share.create` returns the token once (`SecretOutput`, `NoDryRun`, never stored for replay); `share.list` never has one; `share.update` changes label, scope, documents, passphrase, expiry, download and ghosts (not a revoked link); `share.revoke` is idempotent; `share.usage` reports views, last use, threads, open threads and lockout. Link ids appear in audit lines; labels and comment text never do.

### Notifications

- In-app only (the privacy invariant): no email, no push, no webhook, and **no event**: a per-person stream does not exist and an event about "someone mentioned you" would go to everyone on the project's stream. The screens poll `notification.count` (when the tab is visible, now and then). Rows are ids; the list reads the comment when it is shown (an excerpt of 140 characters, only for a person who can still read comments on a project they are still on and that is not in the trash, unless they own it), so deleting or rewording a comment removes its words from the notification. One notification a person per comment, the strongest reason first (mention, reply, comment on your project); 500 a person, read ones swept after 60 days (hourly sweep).

### Seams added to existing code

`alts`: `ScopeSpan`, `Options.AllowOverlap`, `LocateIn` and `ParagraphContext` (moved from `aiwork`, which keeps thin wrappers). `docs`: `comanchor.AfterWrite` in the four content writers; `ApplyReviewEdits`, `CheckReviewEdits`, `CheckInsertion`. `revisions`: the reason. `events`: four payload keys. `mirror`: its poll ignores review events. `server`: the `/s/` routes, no session middleware for them, their headers, a 32 KB body limit, log redaction, the review events filter on the stream, and the notification sweep. `config`: three share limits. Test infrastructure: the sweeps create a comment, a suggestion and a link as fixtures (`reviewFixtures`).

### One departure from the invariants, ratified

Invariant 3 says every write is an op. **The owner of the product has decided to keep the guest comment write as a public route, not an op, and this is the record of that decision.** The reasons: a registry op's caller is a signed-in person (its path, its authorization, the sweeps, the roles and the key pins are all about people), a link answers a stranger with a 404 that must look like every other, and an op rendered into the CLI, the skill and MCP would hand an agent a way to speak as a guest. What keeps the departure honest: the route calls the same domain function as `comment.create` (`review.InsertComment`), so no rule differs; `TestTheGuestRouteAndCommentCreateAgreeOnTheRules` runs the same cases through both and fails if their outcomes drift (body validation, invisible characters, anchors in private notes and ghosts, unknown and foreign documents, the project cap and the reply cap); and the route is one entry in a table that a sweep walks. The guest write has two things comment.create does not: the link's hourly limit and the double-submit guard. `share.*`, which manage links, are ops.

### Not done, or only partly

- **No screens** (reading view with a comment margin, composer, threads, queue, suggestion mode, link management, public pages) and no HTML for `/s/`: the public routes answer JSON. The reading view for members is the existing `/p/{slug}/read` page plus `comment.list?document_id=…&plain=true`.
- A comment cannot be anchored across paragraphs (comment on the first, or on the document); no `comment.search` index; no per-reader isolation between reviewer members; `doc.split` orphans comments on the moved text instead of moving them; `GET /s/{token}` is not an HTML page; guests cannot suggest; no invite-by-link (F-241), no review export, no reader progress.
- `TestCommittedSkillIsCurrent` fails until `make skill` is run on the merged branch: the regenerated skill and CLI snapshot are not committed from this branch, as asked.

## Phase 5 export and import decisions

The backend of "Revision" that is not comments, suggestions or sharing: compile, DOCX, EPUB 3, the submission bundle, and the Word and Obsidian imports. **No screen is built.** Ops: `compile.preview`, `export.compile`, `export.submission`, `compile.preset.list/create/update/delete`, `compile.contact.get/set`; `import.preview/apply/append_preview/append` gained `docx`, `layout` and `docx_headings`. The tables are in [DATA-MODEL](DATA-MODEL.md) (`compile_presets`, `manuscript_contact`, migration 1120), the use in [OPERATIONS](OPERATIONS.md#compile-docx-epub-markdown-and-the-submission-bundle).

### What was checked, and what was not

This is the part to read before saying "it works".

- **Checked:** every DOCX and EPUB the tests write is parsed part by part with `encoding/xml` and checked by a Go validator (`internal/publish/check`). For a DOCX: well-formed parts, a content type for each, relationships that resolve and never leave the package (no `TargetMode`, no hyperlink, OLE, macro or template relationship, no `.bin`, `embeddings/` or `vbaProject`), every `pStyle`, `rStyle`, `numId`, `footnoteReference` and `r:embed` defined, unique drawing ids, and the **schema order** of the children of `pPr`, `rPr`, `sectPr`, `tblPr`, `tcPr`, `style` and `settings` (Word rejects a file whose properties are out of order). For an EPUB: `mimetype` first, stored, exactly `application/epub+zip`, no extra field; a container; an EPUB 3 package with a title, a language, a `urn:uuid` identifier that is the unique identifier, and `dcterms:modified` in the required form; a manifest whose items exist, are unique and are all in the archive; a spine of manifest items; exactly one navigation document with a `toc` nav whose links are in the spine; the NCX; every XHTML file well-formed, titled, with unique ids, no script, form, frame or object, no event handler, no `src` outside the package, links that resolve (fragments included), void elements empty. Mutation tests break each rule and check the validator names it. `xmllint`, where installed, agrees that every XML part is well-formed.
- **Checked with a second implementation:** LibreOffice 26.8 (`SCRIOBH_LIBREOFFICE=1 go test ./internal/publish ./internal/imports`, off by default) converts the Shunn, UK and styled DOCX to PDF, and a PDF page-by-page look at the Shunn file showed the title page (contact block top left, "about 84,300 words" top right, the title about a third down), the header (`Ní Bhriain / THE SALT ROAD / 4`, absent on page 1), the chapter opening a third of the way down a new page, the first paragraph after a break unindented, `#` centred, a bulleted and a numbered list that starts at 3, a table, code and a real footnote. LibreOffice then saved that DOCX again as a DOCX of its own (other styles, other XML) and it imports to the same chapters, the same emphasis, footnote, scene break and list.
- **Not checked, and only these can confirm it:** that **Word** opens the files without a repair prompt and shows the title page, header, double spacing and scene breaks as intended (the exit criterion says so; no Word was available, and nothing here claims it); that a **Kobo**, Apple Books or any reader opens the EPUB; that **epubcheck** passes (no epubcheck jar was present; the Go validator is not a substitute for it); that a Google Docs export imports as well as a LibreOffice one. Two choices that depend on Word's own behaviour and so are unverified there: the drop before a chapter title (see below) and the cover as an SVG image (Kobo's library thumbnails may not draw an SVG; the cover is also the first page of the book).
- **Measured** (Apple silicon, a quiet machine, 120,480 words in 60 chapters of varied text, through the registry: database read included): DOCX 26 ms and 41 MB allocated, 224 KB; EPUB 25 ms and 33 MB, 256 KB; Markdown 23 ms and 37 MB; the preview 23 ms; the validators 19 and 9 ms. The test asserts 40 s per file and 3 GB (ten times the time under the race detector), so it catches a slip to quadratic work, not a regression of tens of percent.

### Selection

- **`include_in_compile`.** A document marked "do not compile" is left out **with everything inside it**: leaving a chapter out of a book leaves its scenes out too (a scene in an excluded chapter is reported as "inside a document marked do not compile"). This is applied by the compile and, a **behaviour change**, by `export.markdown` in single-file mode and `export.html`. **The Markdown bundle (the zip, the documented full copy and the format an import restores) keeps every document by default**: a full copy omits nothing; `include_excluded: false` filters it, and `include_excluded: true` keeps everything in a single file or the HTML. The result's `skipped` and the manifest's `not_compiled` list what was left out, and the Export dialog's download sets an `X-Export-Skipped` header when it filtered (the screen can say so). The mirror, the Git push and the backup are unchanged: they hold every document. This supersedes "`include_in_compile` is ignored by both exports" above.
- **Order and kinds.** Front matter documents first and back matter last, each in tree order, wherever they sit in the tree; a front or back matter document with no text whose contents are all front or back matter themselves is a folder, not a page. **Whatever is inside front or back matter is front or back matter**: it comes first or last with it (a scene inside a front matter page joins that page, and a back matter page is never glued to the last chapter), and a preset that switches front or back matter off leaves out the whole subtree (`switched_off` in the preview counts these; `excluded_total` counts only the do-not-compile ones, and each excluded document carries its reason). Parts are heading pages (`part_pages: false` folds a part's own text into what follows). Chapters are numbered across the book. A scene joins the chapter above it, separated by a scene break (or, with `scene_titles`, a subheading); a scene before any chapter joins an unnumbered, headingless section. Notes are left out (`notes: appendix` puts them after the back matter under "Notes"); the preview counts them. Documents with no title and no text are left out and reported.
- **Ghosts, alternatives, private notes.** Compile presets omit ghosted text (`ghosts: keep` prints it as ordinary text); the alternatives comment is never in a compile (the chosen wording is the text); `%%private notes%%` never are, in any format. Markdown front matter is not text and is dropped. Wiki links are their label. Raw HTML is shown as text.
- **Footnotes** are real: a Word footnote, an EPUB `epub:type="footnote"` aside in a footnotes section at the end of each chapter's file (not a separate endnotes file), a Markdown footnote. A note referenced twice becomes two notes (a printed page and Word cannot point at one note from two places), numbered through the book; notes inside notes are dropped; the copies are bounded per compile, not per document: one budget of 16 MB of copied note text (and 20,000 references per document) is carried through every document of the book, so five chapters of 16 KB cannot become 50 MB (a test pins it; the worst case is a book of about 17 MB for a few KB of input).
- **The input hash** (`input_hash` in the preview, the result and the manifest) covers the preset id, every option, the title, subtitle, author, byline, language, project kind and cover colour, the project's `updated_at` (the date every part of the file carries), the contact block (hashed into the whole, never kept), each included document's id, compiled-text hash, title, kind and role in order, and the images that were embedded (key and hash). A bundle's hash also covers its synopsis and cover letter (so the description and genre). The same input hash is the same content; the preview and the export agree on it (the preview reads the images' stored hashes, the export the bytes, so a damaged image file makes them differ, honestly).
- **Warnings** (codes): `chapter_untitled`, `front_matter_untitled`, `scene_break_at_end`, `scene_break_at_start`, `empty_document`, `not_compiled`, `notes_excluded`, `no_author`, `no_contact`, `image_missing`, `image_remote`, `image_skipped`, `no_synopsis` (submission), `nothing_to_compile` (the export refuses), `writer` (something the DOCX or EPUB writer left out, such as a table of more than 63 columns). Gaps the writer could not see are not warned of.

### DOCX

- **Hand-written** with `archive/zip` and string building; no dependency. Parts: `[Content_Types].xml` first, relationships, document, styles, settings, numbering, footnotes (always, with its separators), two headers (default and first page), two footers when page numbers go in the footer, core and app properties, media. Deflate with a fixed time and fixed entry order: the same input is the same bytes within a build, and the tests also pin the SHA-256 of every part of the sample files (`internal/publish/testdata/golden-parts.txt`), which does not depend on the compressor.
- **Styles, not formatting.** Normal (the line spacing), Body Text (the indent), First Paragraph (no indent: the first paragraph after a heading, a scene break, a quote, a list, code or a table), Title, Subtitle, Byline, Contact Block, Heading 1 to 6, Chapter Title (a chapter inside a part, based on Heading 1, outline level 2), Chapter Opening, Scene Break, The End, Quote, List Paragraph, Code and Code Char, Figure, Table Text, Table Grid, footnote text and reference, header and footer. A paragraph carries only its style (plus an indent for a quote inside a quote, a numbering reference for a list item, and a page break in the rare chapter with no heading). Emphasis is direct `i`, `b` and `strike` on runs (a character-level mark, not a paragraph look); an inline code run uses the Code Char style. Italics are italics, never underlined. Real Word names where Word has them ("heading 1", "Quote", "List Paragraph", "footnote text"); "First Paragraph" is Pandoc's name for the same thing, not Word's "Body Text First Indent", which means the opposite.
- **The drop before a chapter title** is a paragraph, not a space-before. LibreOffice ignores a paragraph's space-before at the top of a page after a page break, which put every chapter title at the very top; a paragraph of exact height (style Chapter Opening, which also carries the page break) is honoured by both it and, as far as is known, by Word. Without a drop (`chapter_drop: 0`) the heading style breaks the page itself.
- **Header.** `Surname / TITLE / page` from page 2 with a `PAGE` field (different first page: an empty first-page header), or `Title / page`, or none; `header_upper` upper-cases the title (Shunn does, the UK preset does not); `header_title` is the short title (default the title, cut at 40 characters at a word boundary). `STYLEREF` is not used (LibreOffice and older Word versions render it badly); the surname comes from the contact block's `surname`, else the last word of the byline ("Ní Bhriain" needs the explicit surname).
- **Title page.** Manuscript layout: the contact block top left (name, address lines, email, phone), the rounded word count top right on the same line by a right tab ("about 84,300 words", or "Word count: 84,300" for the UK preset, to the nearest hundred, to ten under a thousand), the title a third of the way down, the subtitle, "by Name". Book layout: the title, subtitle and byline centred. The word count is the compiled text's own count.
- **Lists** use a bullet definition and, for each ordered list, its own numbering instance that starts where the Markdown says (a list that starts at 3 shows 3). **Tables** are Word tables (fixed layout, header row repeated, alignment kept, at most 63 columns). **Images** are inline pictures of the project's own PNG, JPEG or GIF attachments at their pixel size (96 dpi), scaled to the text width, with the alt text; WebP is left out with a warning (Word cannot embed it); an image without a known size is a bracketed note. **Right-to-left** languages get a `bidi` default and `rtl` runs. **Language** is the project's (`en` is written `en-US`; the UK preset says `en-GB`).
- **Never in a file:** an external relationship, a hyperlink relationship, a macro, an embedded object, a field other than `PAGE`. Control characters, lone surrogates, U+FFFE/FFFF and the bidirectional embeddings and overrides (U+202A to U+202E, the "trojan source" characters) are removed from every string that reaches XML; markup is only ever written by the writer.
- **Fonts** are a short list (Times New Roman, Courier New, Georgia, Cambria, Garamond, Palatino Linotype): the file names a font, it does not embed one, so a reader without it gets a fallback.

### Contact data

`compile.contact.set` stores the contact block in its own table (editor and owner only, reads included). The audit entry says the block changed, not what it holds; it is not in events, any manifest, the mirror or a Git push, and only a hash of it goes into an export's input hash. **Deliberately visible to every editor**: any editor of the project (not only the owner) can read and change the contact block, it stays with the project through an ownership transfer, and it is in the preview and the file for whoever compiles. A writer who wants a manuscript without it sets `contact: false` for that compile (the block is left off the DOCX title page, the word count stays top right, the preview omits it too). It is in the preview's response (`title_page.contact`) and in the file, because both are the writer's own, shown to the writer. The responses of `compile.contact.set` and `compile.preview` hold the block, so those two ops are `SecretOutput`: their responses are never kept for idempotent replay (a test reads the `idempotency_keys` table). It is in the database and so in a backup, like every other personal detail.

### EPUB

- **Package.** `mimetype` stored first; `META-INF/container.xml`; `OEBPS/content.opf` (version 3.0; title, creator, language, `urn:uuid` identifier derived from the project id by hashing so it is stable and is not the id, `dcterms:modified` = the project's `updated_at`); a navigation document (contents nested by part, and landmarks); an NCX for older readers; one XHTML file per section, named by reading order; one stylesheet. Written with a fixed time and order.
- **Content** is XHTML written from the neutral structure with only the elements it names and attributes it builds (`em`, `strong`, `del`, `code`, `a` for http, https and mailto, `img`, `blockquote`, lists, tables, `pre`, footnote asides), escaped and stripped of what XML cannot hold; raw HTML is shown as text. No script, no remote resource, no external stylesheet. The stylesheet names only `serif` and `monospace`, sets no colour and no gradient (the reader's own theme applies), and sizes everything relatively.
- **Cover.** The generated SVG, as the cover image (`properties="cover-image"`) and a first page; a project's own raster cover does not exist yet. Rasterising it needs a font and a rasteriser, and none is bundled: readers that cannot draw an SVG cover in their library will show none.
- **Images** are the project's own attachments and small inline pictures, written as files under `images/` named by their hash. **Right-to-left**: `dir="rtl"` on the document and `page-progression-direction="rtl"` on the spine.

### Plain Markdown and the submission bundle

- `plain-markdown` writes the same structure as Markdown with one spelling for everything (`*`, `**`, `~~`, backticks, `[^n]` footnotes, `* * *` for a scene break, a pipe table); an image becomes a bracketed note (an attachment's address means nothing outside the project). It is a compile, not the Markdown export: the Markdown export keeps the stored text byte for byte for restoring, the compile writes the text a reader sees.
- `export.submission` takes a DOCX preset (default `shunn-us`) and writes `<slug>-manuscript.docx`, `synopsis.md` (the project's description, else the chapters' long or short synopses under their headings, else a placeholder and a `no_synopsis` warning), `cover-letter.md` (title, kind, genre and the rounded word count filled in, your contact block at the top, the rest in brackets) and `manifest.json` (hashes; no contact data), stored, sorted, with the project's time. **Recorded as format `docx`** with preset `submission/<id>`: the `exports.format` constraint has no zip value and changing a constraint means rebuilding a table.

### Presets and permissions

- Built-in presets are code (`shunn-us`, `shunn-us-courier`, `uk-manuscript`, `uk-manuscript-courier`, `styled-docx`, `epub3`, `plain-markdown`); a custom preset stores every option, so a later change to a built-in never alters it; options merged onto a preset (`options` in the call) override for that compile only, field by field (a pointer or a non-zero value; `false` and `0` are choices). Names are unique per project when folded. A preset is looked up by id **and** project (404 from another project), never changed or deleted when built in.
- Every compile op needs an editor or the owner; a reader or a reviewer is refused (403) and a stranger gets 404. `compile.preview`, `compile.preset.list` and `compile.contact.get` are read-scope for API keys (they change nothing); the exports and the writes are write-scope. A dry run of an export records its row and rolls it back and has no data directory, so images are left out of the preview file with a warning.

### Importing a Word document

- **Through the folder importer.** The .docx is converted to a folder of Markdown files (`NN-slug.md`), which the importer reads with its own preview, token, edits (rename, kind, skip, split, reorder), caps and provenance. A .docx is limited as it is elsewhere: the central directory is walked before the archive is opened (10,000 entries), each XML part is read through a limit (96 MB, 160 MB in all) whatever it declares, XML is decoded as a stream without entities, a DOCTYPE is refused, nesting deeper than 80 is refused, a paragraph or table of more than 400,000 elements is refused, and macros and embedded objects are counted, never read. Hostile fixtures in the tests: not a zip, no document part, a DOCTYPE with entity definitions, a part of 100 MB, nesting.
- **What becomes what.** Heading 1 (or any style with outline level 1, found through `basedOn`) is a chapter, the heading its title ("Chapter 3: The Road" loses its label, as this program writes it; "Chapter 3" alone stays a title); `docx_headings: parts` makes Heading 1 a part and Heading 2 a chapter (text under a part heading before its first chapter becomes a chapter of its own, and a warning says so); deeper headings stay as Markdown headings, so `split_scenes: h2` turns Heading 2 into scenes. Title and Subtitle (before the first heading) and the title page's own styles (Byline, Contact Block) are the project's facts, not text; the author is the document's creator. Text before the first heading is a front matter document. A paragraph is a paragraph; bold, italic, strikethrough and a code character style are kept; **underline is dropped and counted** (Markdown has none, and in an old manuscript it means italics, which a script cannot tell from a link); a link is kept for http, https and mailto and is its text otherwise. A Scene Break style, a lone `#`, `* * *`, `---` and the like are the canonical `* * *`; **two or more empty paragraphs between paragraphs are also read as a scene break**, a heuristic that is counted and reported. Lists (bullet or ordered, from the numbering definitions, nested by level, an ordered list starts where Word's does), quotes, code styles and tables (pipe tables, merged cells repeated) are kept; footnotes become Markdown footnotes at the end of the chapter they are in.
- **Text is escaped** so nothing a document says can be a Scríobh construct: `%%` is not a private note (a private note would hide text), `[[` is not a wiki link, `<` is not raw HTML or a ghost, a line that starts `:::` is not a directive, a line that starts `#`, `-`, `+`, `=`, `~` or `1.` is not a block. Smart quotes and every other character are kept.
- **Tracked changes:** the final view (insertions kept, deletions dropped), with the number reported. **Comments:** the count is reported and nothing is imported (comments come with review). **Images, text boxes and drawings:** not imported; each image is marked `[image]` in the text and counted. Headers and footers are ignored and counted.
- **Not done:** images and attachments (the importer has no attachment path and the upload ops cannot run inside the import's transaction; this needs the importer to store files after its commit and rewrite the text, which is its own piece of work); Word comments; a table of contents field; equations; text boxes; re-import diff.

### Importing an Obsidian vault

- **A choice, never a guess.** `layout: obsidian` in the preview. A `.obsidian` folder does not switch it on; Hugo and Jekyll are not detected either. The Git import keeps its two layouts. The vault is a zip or a folder listing; a wrapper folder is dropped; folders become parts and files chapters, as in any folder import (numbers sort first, then names the way a person counts).
- **What is rewritten** (outside code fences and code spans): `[[Page]]` and `[[Page|alias]]` stay; `[[Page#Heading]]` and `[[Page^block]]` point at the page (`[[Page|Page > Heading]]` when there is no alias); a folder path in a link is dropped; `[[#Heading]]` is plain text; `![[Note]]` becomes `[[Note]]` (the embedded text is not copied; counted); `![[picture.png]]`, `![[file.pdf]]` and local Markdown images become `*[image: name]*` and `*[attachment: name]*`; `> [!type] Title` becomes `> **Type: Title**`; ` ^block-id` at a line's end is removed; `dataview`, `dataviewjs`, `query` and `tasks` fences and Templater `<% %>` commands are dropped and counted; `.canvas` files and Excalidraw drawings are skipped and listed; `.obsidian/` is hidden and skipped.
- **Comments.** Obsidian's `%%comment%%` is the same syntax as a Scríobh private note, so a comment stays private (verified: the word count and search text omit it). A private note ends with its text block (a paragraph, a heading, a list item, a table cell), and a delimiter that does not pair inside one is printed, so **no comment is ever left open across a line**: the line that opens a comment closes it at its end, every line inside it becomes a private paragraph of its own (a heading mark, bullet, quote mark, fence, rule, table row or indent in it is dropped with its structure, since what is private needs none), and the line that closes it goes on as text. A comment in a table row is cut out of the row (a cell cannot hold half of one). Tests cover headings, lists, quotes, fences, rules, indented lines and tables inside a comment and assert that none of the text reaches `text.Structure`, the search text or the count. An unclosed Templater command (`<%`) ends at the next blank line, or after 50 lines, and the preview says how many did not close: it no longer eats the rest of the note.
- **Tags** (`#tag` in the text, `tags:` in the front matter) are listed in the preview (up to 20) and left as text; there is no op that adds a tag to a manuscript document yet. Front matter stays in the text exactly, so `title:` and the properties survive. Non-UTF-8 files, symlinks, unsafe paths, hidden files and huge files are skipped and listed as in any folder import.
- **Not done:** images and attachments (as for Word); aliases from the front matter as link targets; nested-tag taxonomy; `==highlight==` (left as written).

### Other

- `scriobh-cli` takes `--out` as `--output`. `scriobh import` takes a .docx as its argument, `--layout obsidian` and `--docx-parts`.
- The ARCHITECTURE document said exactly two layouts are recognised; that rule stays for the Git import and is stated as such, and the folder and zip import's third, explicit layout and its Word source are described there. ARCHITECTURE also said EPUB was validated with epubcheck in CI, and F-398 promised page images or a PDF; both are corrected to what was built.
- **Not built:** a PDF export (Phase 6); exporting one chapter or a range; saved front and back matter builders; a re-download of a past export (the file is not kept); the screens; page images in the preview; EPUB import; Word comments, tracked changes as suggestions and image import.

### After an independent review

Fixes, each with a regression test that fails on the earlier code.

- **Obsidian comments** never cross a block (see Comments above), and a `%%` inside an image's description or a wiki link's label is dropped by the structure builder as it is in a paragraph (`text.Structure`, the DOCX, the EPUB and the Markdown writers). `RenderHTML` (the HTML export) still shows such a description or label as written; it is outside this change.
- **DOCX import is bounded in time and memory.** Every token of every kind is paid for from a budget (a `w:t` of a million comment-split pieces was quadratic and is now refused), the text kept in one paragraph or table is capped at 8 MB (a 40 MB run in an 83 KB archive allocated 1.18 GB; it is now refused after about 230 MB, mostly the inflated part itself), the Markdown made is capped at twice the import limit, and the core properties are read through builders with a cap. Footnotes are relabelled in one pass (16,000 notes took 17 s; they take about 0.3 s) and their number and size are capped before a file is composed. A panic in the converter (a negative list level used to cause one) is recovered in `docxToFiles` and `obsidianConvert`, since only the HTTP binding recovers; negative levels are clamped at read time.
- **DOCX escaping.** Leading white space (including a no-break space) is dropped before a line is escaped, so `" # x"`, `"  - x"`, four spaces and `" ==="` after a line break are text; the first line of a list item or a quote is escaped like any other (`- # x`); a table cell's pipe is escaped once; a heading's text is escaped; footnote ids are read as plain numbers and anything else is not a note (so an id can never put text in the Markdown); a note referenced from a table cell gets its definition; a note inside a note is dropped; a link address is accepted only as a valid http, https or mailto URL with every control character, space, bracket, angle bracket, quote, backslash, backtick, brace, caret, parenthesis and bare percent sign percent-encoded (an address with a newline and `[[Wiki]]` used to split the paragraph and make a live wiki link).
- **Compile.** `include_in_compile` is a subtree rule, front and back matter are subtree roles (above), the input hash covers more (above), the Markdown writer writes only the notes its text refers to (a ghosted reference's note is gone), a text option can be cleared (`clear`), `contact: false` leaves the block off, pagination walks each paragraph once (eight 500 KB paragraphs in about 30 ms), and the bundle default for `export.markdown` is the full copy.
- **Not changed:** the hash of a dry run differs from a real one when the project has images (a dry run embeds none); a damaged image file makes the preview and the export disagree about the hash; `RenderHTML` as above.


## Phase 3.5 screens: decisions and deviations

Decisions taken while building the alternatives, ghost and stash screens.

- **Keys.** Alt+Shift+A add, N next, P previous, O original, G ghost or revive, S stash. They are checked in Chromium on macOS and in the end-to-end scripts; on a layout where Alt+Shift+letter types a character the palette has every command, and the toolbar and the context menu have them too. One key ghosts plain text and revives ghosted text.
- **The middle dot.** The brief's "highlighted middle dot when you are on a non-original wording" is drawn as the ring at each end of a mark becoming filled (and the underline heavier). A dot inside the text would take width and move the line; the rings take none.
- **Ghost dimming.** Ghosted text is dimmed to 70% and struck through with a dotted line, not 10%: at 10% a ghost cannot be read or found, and the line is a cue that does not depend on opacity. 70% is the least that keeps the text at 4.5:1 in all five themes (sepia is the tightest at 4.92); `scripts/check-contrast.py` reads the opacity from the stylesheet and checks it for every theme.
- **Audio cue.** Off by default, toggled from the palette, kept in this browser (`scriobh:altsound`). A server preference would need a migration for a setting that is a matter of one device's speakers.
- **Cycling goes to the server.** A cycle is an op (`alternative.choose`), so the text changes when the server answers, about 30 ms on the 4x-throttled phone with 200 alternatives in 20,000 words. Pressing keys quickly queues them and each applies in order against the range as it is then (a range looked up when the answer arrives, never one remembered from before). The editor's undo does not undo a cycle; the server's history does.
- **Locking.** During an op the editor refuses typing (`aria-busy`) but stays editable, because switching to read-only drops focus and the next key would go nowhere.
- **Hot path and reload.** Choose, edit, reorder and add-wording splice the answer's `replaced` text into the page's idea of the stored text and swap the comment line locally. Create, delete, ghost, revive, stash and re-anchor ask for `with_content` and reload the text (the caret kept by position).
- **One list, not three tabs.** The panel lists every alternative of the chapter with its scope as a tag, rather than Word, Sentence and Paragraph tabs for the selection (F-403, partial).
- **Five inspector tabs wrap.** At the width of the inspector, five tabs do not fit a row; they wrap to a second row rather than scroll out of sight.
- **No pointer drag from the stash.** Cards are put back by a form (at the cursor or the end, paragraph or inline, copy or move), which is also the keyboard and touch route. Dragging is not built.
- **Drafts and the person's id.** Chapter drafts and held text are `scriobh:draft:{person}:{doc}` and `scriobh:held:{person}:{doc}`. Keys from before the change have no owner: they are no longer dropped (see "Phase 3.5 screens: review fixes"); the chapter's page takes over what is kept for it.
- **A bug found and fixed at its source.** The server handed the alternatives ops `Limits{PerDoc: n}` and left the per-wording limit at zero, so every wording after the first was refused. It now starts from the defaults and sets only the configured figure (`TestAlternativesLimitsAreNotZero`).
- **Project addresses.** `project.get` and `project.list` return `address`; every link the pages build uses it. The ops schema changed, so the skill and CLI snapshot need `make skill`.
- **format.js.** The formatting bar moved out of `doc.js` into a lazy file to make room for the save contract; its budget is enforced like the others.
- **Send to the stash moves the text.** The selection goes into the stash and is removed from the chapter by an ordinary edit (one undo step, saved like typing); the item is never deleted for you, and the stash is where it comes back from. A copy that leaves the text in place is the browser's own copy and paste.
- **Chords read the browser's selection first.** The editor learns of a selection the keyboard made a moment after the key (the browser reports it asynchronously and the editor does not look again at a key it has no use for), so Alt+Shift+A pressed straight after Shift+Arrow could act on the earlier selection. For 600 ms after a key that moves the selection the chord handler reads the browser's selection itself (public editor API only); a selection the page made (the editor's own state) is never overwritten by an older browser selection.
- **A right click inside a selection keeps it.** Some browsers collapse the page's selection between the press and the menu; the selection at the press is put back when the click was inside it, as a native menu would behave.
- **Offline.** The shell holds every file under `/static`, the lazy ones included (`format.js`, `alts.js`, `altpanel.js`, `stash.js`); `e2e-pwa.mjs` checks the cache for them. A page itself is never kept, so a reload with the network cut shows the offline notice, as for every page.


## Phase 3.5 screens: review fixes

Decisions taken while fixing the independent review of the alternatives screens. Each fix has a step in `scripts/e2e-alts2.mjs` (named "review N") that failed before it, and the unit tests under `web/editor/test`.

- **The editor is held from the moment an op is asked for.** Not after the pending save has gone: the keys typed during the flush were being taken and then lost (or never saved). A counter (`hold` and `release`) keeps it held through every op queued behind the first. The page refuses the characters and the keys it can see, and the editor refuses every edit of the text itself (`ed.alts.lock`, checked in `dispatchTransaction`), so undo, bold, the toolbar, paste, drop and an input method change nothing while held. Selection changes still go through. An op waits for a composition in progress to end before it reads the text, because that cannot be refused.
- **What "saved" means after an op.** `settle(version)` marks saved the version that was stored (taken after the flush, with the editor held), not whatever the editor holds when the answer arrives; anything typed since stays unsaved and is handed over again. A reload of the stored text never replaces text the editor holds that the server does not: it says so, leaves the text, and the next save meets the changed revision and shows the conflict bar.
- **Answers about the marks are checked against the text they were asked about.** `refresh` throws an answer away when the editor's document or the stored revision has changed since the question went out (and asks again), instead of drawing places that belong to older text on the new text.
- **The server's range is checked before it is used.** A choice names the source text it replaced; the editor puts the new wording over the words it holds at the mark only if those are the same words (compared as words, not markup: at the edge of a run of emphasis the source names the asterisks and the editor's positions do not). If not, nothing is replaced and the stored text is taken whole.
- **Marks are asked for again after a save that touched one.** An edit at either edge of a mark, or inside it, flags it; the `saved` event then asks for the places again once nothing is being typed (300 ms, or 2.5 s after a mark was lost).
- **A new wording keeps the emphasis around the old one, not inside it.** The old source is parsed: marks it produced by itself (its own asterisks) are not carried onto the new wording; marks the text sat inside are.
- **Typing cost does not grow with the number of marks.** The decorations are kept from one document to the next and mapped through each edit (`mapSet`); they are rebuilt only after an ask, an op, or an edit that took a mark or a ghost tag in or out. The id lookup is a map. The shell's decoration slot takes arrays or sets. With 1,000 alternatives in a 20,000-word chapter the editor's state update per edit went from 16 ms (p50) to 1.6 ms at 4x CPU.
- **Marker characters.** `rangesOf` puts one marker character, chosen so that it is not in the text, at every end of every range and reads them back in order, so no number of alternatives can run out of codes or mistake a real character for a marker. If the parse swallows a marker (inside a link address, say) the ranges are asked for again with a code of their own each, as far as the private-use area goes. (`sourceOf` and `offsetOf` still use two fixed private-use characters; a chapter that contains them would confuse them. Not changed.)
- **Send to the stash removes the range that was stashed.** The range and document are taken when the key is pressed, the editor is held until the stash has answered, and the removal is that range of that document, or nothing and a message. It is refused in a locked, read-only or too-long chapter.
- **Foreign HTML cannot make held source.** The clipboard classes that mark a ghost tag or an alternatives block are removed from pasted HTML that does not carry the editor's own marker (`data-pm-slice`).
- **Drafts under the old key are taken over, never deleted.** Opening a chapter moves what an earlier session of this browser kept for it (`scriobh:draft:{doc}`, `scriobh:held:{doc}`) into the held list of the person who opened it, and the draft bar offers it. This replaces the earlier decision to drop them at load. A chapter that is never opened keeps its old key, and sign-out asks about it like any other draft.
- **Project addresses in the palette.** The palette opens a project by its `address`. The other places that build a project address from a script (the write page's data attributes, the importer's own new project) already carry the address or name the caller's own project.
- **Chords during composition.** A chord is ignored while an input method is composing (`isComposing`, key code 229, the editor's own composing flag). The chords are by position, so hints are taken from the browser's layout map where it has one (Chromium) and the US letter otherwise, and the panel says so. On Windows, pressing Alt and Shift together without a letter can switch the input language when more than one is installed; the chords here have a letter and do not, but a person who finds a flash of the language indicator can use the palette instead.
- **Toolbar.** When the formatting bar is wider than its column (a phone, or the inspector open) a button at its end scrolls it a page at a time and back, so nothing is hidden without a cue. If the bar's script does not arrive, the bar says so in words and the text still saves.
- **Targets.** The buttons in the cycler and the panel are as wide as they are tall (`--target`: 44 px on touch, 32 px with a pointer, as everywhere). The cycler opens above the mark so it does not cover the line being read; Escape closes it and focus stays in the text.
- **One announcement.** A message is a toast (a live region of its own) or, with no toast, a line in the status region: not both. The caret coming into an alternative or into ghosted text is said (not while typing). The marks carry a title.
- **The panel is left alone while a wording is being typed in it.** A redraw waits for an edit box to lose focus, and words typed in a card's "Another wording" box and its caret survive any other redraw.
- **Not done: undo across a server-driven replacement.** An op that takes the stored text whole (a ghost, a stash, a re-anchor, a delete) starts the editor's undo history afresh. The panel's help says so; the chapter's history keeps every earlier text. Keeping the editor's history across these would mean mapping it through a replacement the server made, which is not feasible here.
- **Naming a passage across a hard-wrapped line.** (Round one left this undone; round two does it.) The saved text keeps a line break inside a paragraph that the editor spells as a space, so such a passage was not found. It is now asked for again after the editor's spelling has been stored, and found.


## Phase 3.5 screens: second review round

Decisions taken while fixing the second independent review. Each has a step in `scripts/e2e-alts2.mjs` named "round 2, N", and unit tests in `web/editor/test/alts.test.mjs`.

- **Lone surrogates.** A surrogate half with no partner is replaced by U+FFFD where text comes in (plain and HTML paste, pasted Markdown, the Source box) and where it goes out (the editor's output and `plain()`), because the server stores it that way and a saved copy that differs from the sent one made the page send it again for ever and keep the hold on. Separately, when the server's answer to a save differs from what was sent (beyond the alternatives comment), and the editor has not moved on, the page takes the answer as the text it holds; after four such answers in a row it says so and stops. (Test: a pasted half settles with at most three saves; a server that rewrites its answer is not answered with a storm.)
- **Markers are chosen per call.** `sourceOf` and `offsetOf` use characters of the private-use area that the chapter does not contain, require exactly one of each to come back, and require that taking them out gives the plain serialization; otherwise the text "could not be named exactly" and the person is told in plain words. A quote that occurs twice is sent with enough context to be found once (the context doubles up to 4,096 characters, then it is refused). The first repro in the review ("target" quoted as "old words") was the marker collision, not a separate fault: with no collision the quote was already "target".
- **Position, not only words.** A choice is applied only if the server's range parses (from the stored body) to the position of the mark, as well as holding the same words.
- **The editor's spelling is stored before an offset is used.** A chapter written by the CLI or an import keeps its own spelling until the first edit ("a * b", a two-space break, "# Title #", an escaped underscore, a link with spaces), so source offsets mean different places in it and in the editor's serialization. Revive and "put it in the chapter at the cursor" check that the two agree (the comment line aside); if not, the editor's own spelling is saved first (`flush(true)`, which hands the serialization over even when nothing was typed) and they are checked again; if they still disagree the operation is refused with a message, never turned into "at the end". Passages named by their words are asked for once more after the same save if the server does not find them as spelled. Storing the editor's spelling is what the first edit of the chapter would do anyway.
- **Legacy drafts, again.** Keys without a person (`scriobh:draft:{doc}`, `scriobh:held:{doc}`) cannot be told apart by person, so they are adopted only by someone who can write to the chapter; a reader's page leaves them alone. They are filed as held copies marked "kept before drafts were tied to a person, so it may not have been yours" (a second writer on the same browser can still be offered them: that cannot be avoided without an owner on the key). The room for held copies is 8; an entry that does not fit stays in the old key, and an old key is removed only when everything in it was kept. Discard removes the copy the bar describes, and nothing else; the bar says how many older copies there are.
- **Source view.** While the editor is held, Ctrl or Command with E does nothing, the Source toggle is refused in the editor itself, and the Source box is read-only. Operations are refused from the Source view.
- **The hold and composition.** The hold waits for an input method's composition to end before it takes effect (its own steps cannot be refused), and is owed from the moment the operation is asked for.
- **The hold ends when the change has landed.** The marks and the ghosts are asked for again after the hold is released (a slow answer cannot keep keys refused); the operation's own answer is given once they are in. A failed read of the new text after a committed change says the change was made, not that nothing was.
- **Tab is not trapped by the hold.** Only keys that change the text, and Ctrl or Command with Z, Y, X, V, B, I, U, K or E, are refused.
- **Smaller.** A send that cannot name a selection (reanchor, ghost, create) says so; "the details changed elsewhere" settles the save it was waiting on; the panel's blur handler is attached to whichever element it is shown in; `exclusive` releases in a `finally`.
- **Limits raised.** `alts.js` 15 to 16 KB and the writing glue (`doc.js` + `tree.js`) 20 to 21 KB gzip, with the table in ARCHITECTURE.md, in one commit.


## Phase 3.5 release notes: what is not verified

v0.3.5 adds alternatives, ghosts, the stash and the formatting bar to the writing view. Checked in Chromium (desktop and a 390 px emulated phone, 4x CPU) by the end-to-end scripts, and by two independent reviews with their fixes. Not verified, and not claimed:

- Real IME input (Japanese, Chinese, Korean) and the dead-key / Alt+Shift layout switch on Windows. Composition is waited for, and chords are ignored while composing, but this was exercised with synthetic events only.
- Keyboard-layout key hints come from the browser's layout map, which only Chromium has. The toolbar titles in the template show the US letters.
- Safari, Firefox and a real touch device. Drag of a ghost or a held alternative back into the text was driven with pointer events only.
- Undo history across a change the server made (cycling a wording, accepting an alternative): the editor's history cannot be kept across one, and the panel says so.
- A dead network can keep keys refused for up to about 30 s while an alternatives or stash change is in flight, because the hold ends when the request finishes or the page's own timeout fires. Ordinary typing and saving are never held.
- A second writer on the same browser can be offered a legacy draft written before drafts were tied to a person; those old keys have no owner to check.
- Screenshots in `docs/screens/phase-3.5` are a subset (desktop light and dark, mobile light); not every one was looked at after the last regeneration.

---

## Phase 4 screens: decisions and deviations

- **One panel, not a drawer.** The chat, the reviews and the Lab are tabs of the inspector, which is already a side panel on a wide screen and a full-height sheet at 390 px. A separate chat drawer would have been a second panel mechanism with its own focus rules. The AI tab has two views (Chat, Reviews), each its own lazy file.
- **Every send goes over the streaming route.** The preview is made with `stream: true` because the server binds a run to the preview by payload hash and a nonce, and a preview made without it would not match the route the run uses. Cancel on a stream aborts the request and calls `ai.run.cancel`.
- **Always ask.** The backend stores no "do not ask again", so none exists. A preview is made before every send, including Try again and every chat message.
- **Send is not disabled while a preview is being made again.** Disabling it lost the click when a field blurred on the way to the button. A stale preview makes Send refuse with a message instead, and the server refuses a payload that differs from the one previewed.
- **The Lab belongs to editors, not to AI.** The tab, the local checks and the marks exist with AI off. The model checks appear only when AI is on. Marks are drawn through the one decoration slot and are listed in the panel; a mark whose words have changed says so and cannot be accepted.
- **Accept as an alternative** for a rewrite adds an ordinary variant through the alternatives ops; there is no op to add a suggestion as a *generated* variant, so such a variant is not labelled generated. The candidates of the Alternatives recipe are (the op marks them generated).
- **Reviews have no bulk dismiss op.** "Dismiss all" calls the note op once per open note.
- **Kept chats have no retention setting.** The backend keeps a kept chat until its owner deletes it, so the page offers Delete and "Delete all my kept chats" and says that is the retention.
- **A rolling whole-manuscript summary** has ops and no screen yet; the chapter summary does.
- **Extraction needs a bible.** `ai.extract.accept` creates an entity in the project's primary Compendium; with none, the review says so and points to the Compendium page.
- **Page structure.** `/settings/ai` and `/p/{slug}/ai` are Go-rendered shells; their script reads and changes everything through the REST ops with the session, so the page and the CLI are the same thing. `aiStateOf` decides the writing view's `data-ai` (on, off, unavailable, none) from `ai.status` and `ai.policy.get` as the person.
- **Budgets of the new files** (gzip, enforced in `internal/server/web_test.go`, listed in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`): `aiboot.js` 1.25 KB rides with the writing view only when AI is on for the project; `ai.js`, `aipanel.js`, `aichat.js`, `aireviews.js` and `lab.js` are fetched when a control is first used; `aiadmin.js` is on the two AI pages only. No existing limit was raised. The sheets are `ai.css` (the two pages), `aiwrite.css` (the writing partner in the writing view) and `lab.css` (the marks).
- **The "AI is off" state is shown in two places, not on every project page.** The chip is in the writing view's header (short on a phone: "AI on", "AI local", "AI off"); every other project page has the AI tab, which opens the page that states it. A chip on each of the other project headers was not built.
- **A tap on a Lab mark in the text never opens a panel.** It highlights the mark's entry in the list only when the Lab tab is already open, so tapping to place the caret at 390 px does not cover the text with the sheet.
- **Verified only against a fake provider.** `scripts/fake-ai.mjs` answers by task from a canned table; no real model has been run, so how well real models follow the JSON shapes, and how readable a real preview of a long chapter is, are unchecked.

## Phase 4 screens: fix round (security and privacy review, then the accessibility review)

- **A proposal never takes focus.** The card for a proposal, the alternatives card and the summary card appear without moving focus, because the writer may be typing and a key meant for the text must reach the text (an `a` typed as the answer arrived had accepted a proposal). The arrival is announced; Alt+Shift+J and the palette command "Go to the proposal that is waiting" move focus to the card; A, L, R and T act only when the key is pressed with focus inside the card. On a touch screen the key hints are not shown.
- **The AI tab no longer assumes the boot file came first.** `aipanel.js` and `lab.js` wait for `aiboot.js` (an event, at most eight seconds), `aiboot.js` tells a waiting part when the core could not be loaded, and the panel then says so plainly with a Try again button; the inspector's own lazy loader has the same button.
- **The summary accept goes through the held editor** like every other write to the chapter: the pending save first, the page's own revision and tab, and the stored text and revision taken afterwards. It no longer writes the page's revision itself.
- **Lab marks are tracked through edits.** The marks live in one decoration set that is mapped through every transaction; Show and next/previous read their positions from it, so they select the right words after text is typed in front of them.
- **The key chord is Alt+Shift+I** everywhere (the palette said Ctrl+Shift+I, which opens developer tools).
- **Accept all in the Lab** takes at most 300 marks (the server's limit), in document order, and says so; the rest stay open. The local check keeps at most 300 marks of one kind and says when it stopped; the list says when it stops at 1,000.
- **The request fingerprint is keyed.** It is an HMAC-SHA256 the server makes with its own key over the exact request, not a plain SHA-256 anyone could recompute; the preview says "Request fingerprint (keyed)". The preview also shows how many tokens the request counts against a monthly cap, and the refusal message uses the same figure, with thousands separators (it counts the input once for every try the providers allow, plus the longest answer, so it can be larger than the two numbers shown first).
- **Keep on mid-chat keeps only what comes after.** The panel says so before it is turned on. Earlier turns are not stored (a stored turn must carry the run that produced it).
- **"Keep as an alternative" for a proposal** adds an ordinary alternative and records the run as rejected, since the proposal itself was not applied; there is no op that records "accepted as an alternative", and nothing is marked generated.
- **Settings pages.** A reader opening `/settings/ai` is told it is not for them and is offered nothing; a writer sees no administrator setting. The project's policy controls are shown exactly when the op would allow the change (the owner, and an administrator, who acts as the owner through the ops; an editor reads).
- **When an administrator turns AI off for the whole server**, the project's AI page says "AI is turned off for this server" and nothing else it could ask: while the kill switch is on every AI op on a project (including listing and deleting kept chats and reading use) answers 409 by design, so the page cannot offer those. They are untouched and return when it is switched on. If kept chats should be listable and deletable while the switch is off, that is a backend decision (the `gate` in `internal/domain/aiwork`); it was not made here.
- **Destructive buttons are real confirmations.** The first press arms the button and says so; the second works no sooner than half a second later; it disarms after five seconds, on Escape and when focus leaves; a double-click does nothing. Applied to Delete this review, Delete this chat, Remove provider, Delete all my kept chats and Dismiss all.
- **The Lab has its own colour** (`--lab`, a muted amber; an olive gold in Sepia), apart from the accent that the alternatives use and from the comment line in all five themes; each of the nine kinds has its own line (shape and weight) and nothing else is drawn in the text, and `scripts/check-contrast.py` checks the colour against the page (3:1), its distance from the accent and the comment line, and that no rule uses `--lab` for text.
- **Not changed:** the unnamed-button report: the 151 by 32 px button of the add-provider form is "Add the provider" and has a name (a scan of the pages finds no control without one).
- **New limits.** Because of the above, the gzip limits of five of the AI files were raised in the same commit as the code and in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: `ai.js` 12 KB, `aiadmin.js` 11.5 KB, `lab.js` 7.5 KB, `aireviews.js` 5 KB, `aipanel.js` 2 KB, `aiboot.js` 1.25 KB. The writing hot path is unchanged.

## Phase 4 release notes: what is not verified, and decisions taken at the tag

v0.4 adds the AI core, the recipes and their screens (providers, per-project policy, the context preview, selection actions, chat, the Lab, reviews). Checked against a **fake provider** by the end-to-end scripts, and by independent security, privacy and interface reviews with their fixes. Not verified, and not claimed:

- **No real model or provider has ever been run.** OpenRouter, Ollama and the OpenAI-compatible shapes are exercised against fakes; the OpenCode request shape is assumed from its documentation. Streaming, error shapes, cut-off streams and token counts from real providers may differ.
- No screen reader (VoiceOver, NVDA) has been used. The accessible names, live regions and focus handling were checked with the browser's accessibility tree and keyboard-only walks (`e2e-ai-keys`).
- Reload with the network cut on the two AI pages is untested (the e2e scripts run without the service worker); only asking while offline is.
- Real devices, Safari and Firefox. The proposal chords (Alt+Shift+I, Alt+Shift+J) were matched in Chromium on this machine; macOS option-layers and AltGr layouts were reviewed by reading.
- **Kept chats, never-send flags and usage cannot be managed while the server-wide AI switch is off**, because every AI op answers 409 then. Deleting kept data should work with AI off; this is queued for Phase 6 hardening.
- "Keep as alternative" adds an ordinary alternative and records the AI run as rejected; no op records "accepted as an alternative". Nothing is labelled as generated.
- Turning Keep on in the middle of a chat stores only the turns after that point; the panel says so.
- A preview's fingerprint and nonce are not single-use: a scripted client can send the same approved request again until restart. **Decision:** the approval authorises that exact payload from that person for that project, and the payload hash binds it to the current text and provider, so a replay sends nothing the person did not approve; it can only spend budget, which the caps still bound. The screen never offers a resend. Revisit if a stricter "one approval, one send" is wanted.
- Screenshots in `docs/screens/phase-4` are a subset (desktop light and dark, mobile light).

## Phase 4 screens: second fix round (a read-only verification of the first)

- **A held key is one press.** The card's keys ignore auto-repeat, a key that acted on a card is swallowed until it is let go (wherever focus went), and closing a card collapses the selection to its end before focus returns to the text, so no repeat can be typed over the words the proposal was about. (Try again keeps the selection: the next request is about the same words.)
- **One chord table.** Every Alt+Shift chord of the writing view is claimed in one object, `scriobh.chords`; a chord claimed twice is listed in `chords.clash`, and `e2e-ai-edit` fails if it is not empty. The proposal's chord is Alt+Shift+J (Alt+Shift+P was already "previous wording").
- **A synopsis accept leaves the text alone.** It is held and flushed like a write, then only the new revision is taken (the synopsis is not in the body); the editor's text and its undo history stay. Only if the stored body is not what the page holds is it taken whole.
- **The guard** (Delete this review, Delete this chat, Remove provider, Delete all my kept chats, Dismiss all, Delete a named version) takes focus back only if focus is still on it, on the page body or on the panel that holds it; otherwise it disarms. A confirmation made with the keyboard needs a new press after the first was let go: a held Enter, and the repeats that follow it, confirm nothing.
- **Focus is moved only where the action began.** A finished review, a failed chat answer and the retry button of an AI tab that could not load move focus only if it is still inside the panel (or nowhere); otherwise the result is announced. A run started from the text or the palette never moves it.
