- **Lab marks and a paragraph that ends in a space.** The places the editor's parser gives are in the document the stored text parses to, which has lost the spaces a paragraph starts and ends with; they are carried over block by block to the editor's own document, so a mark is on its word after every save. (The alternatives' marks use the same parser; they were measured on the 20,000-word chapter and are right after a save, and `e2e-ai-perf.mjs` checks both.)
- **One card slot.** The summary card and the proposal card share one slot, so there is one `#ai-pop`.
- **A mark is a line, not a letter.** The letter drawn after each mark in the text added width (so a refresh that added or dropped marks moved the caret line), failed text contrast and could be read aloud as prose. It is gone: each of the nine kinds has its own line, the list names the kind with its letter, and `--lab` is a line colour only.
- **Smaller.** A part that loads but does not register counts as not loaded (with the retry); a failed Accept gives the button its focus back; the Lab list has one live region; `__pycache__/` is ignored and the compiled file removed from git.


## Phase 5 screens: decisions and deviations

Decisions taken while building the screens for review, suggestions and sharing (the other Phase 5 screens are added under their own headings below). Each has a test or a step in `scripts/e2e-review.mjs` or `scripts/e2e-share.mjs`.

### Reading view and the margin

- **Built on `/p/{slug}/read`.** The Phase 3 reading mode (one chapter to a page, continuous scroll, the remembered place, the contents) is the whole-book reading page; reviewers and up get one more sheet (`review.css`) and one more lazy script (`review.js`, 11 KB gzip), a reader gets neither. `read.js` did not grow. New chapters that arrive while scrolling are noticed by a `MutationObserver` on the page's stream and get their marks.
- **A margin list, not threads pinned beside their words.** The margin is a sticky column of cards in the order of the text (a bottom sheet under 1024 px); clicking a mark opens its card, a card's Show puts focus on its mark. Pinning cards to their lines and moving them out of each other's way is a layout problem with no good answer on a phone; the list is robust, keyboard-friendly and honest.
- **What a selection is.** One block of prose (a paragraph, a list item or a heading in the text; not a chapter title, a table cell, an image's note or a struck-through run, which the source spells differently). The page strips the footnote number and an image's note from a selection, and its text rule is the server's (runs of white space are one space, none at either end). `TestRenderedBlocksAreVisibleBlocks` pins that each block the renderer makes is one `PlainBlocks` knows; `TestWhatTheReadingViewCannotSelect` pins what it cannot.
- **Ghosted text is not in the reading view.** It was: the member reading view rendered the ghost tags as text. It now shows the book as it stands (`StripGhosts`), as a link does.
- **Plain hints through the visible-text map.** `Hinter.For` used the source substring's own plain text, which gave `quiet* house` for "quiet house" in `*quiet* house`: the marks could not be found on the page. It now maps the source range back through `PlainBlock.Visible`, the inverse of `Source`.
- **A suggestion cannot leave half a pair of marks.** A plain replacement is widened over the marks right outside it when that makes its text the selection, and refused when no widening does (a selection that starts in the middle of an emphasised word), with a message that says to select whole formatted words.
- **Keys read the page's own cursor.** A page of text has no caret, so `]` and `[` place one and Shift with the arrows extends it with `Selection.modify` (the same in Firefox and Chromium). Chords are ignored in a control, with a modifier, and in the margin (where `J` and `K` mean threads). After the Comments and Suggesting buttons focus returns to the page so the keys work.
- **A reviewer's "next unresolved".** `review.queue` is the editor's (the roles table pins it), so a reviewer's `N` walks their own list in reading order; an editor's uses the queue and, when the thread is in a chapter that is not on the page, goes to `/p/{slug}/read/{doc}#t={thread}` (the existing redirect puts a scene on its chapter's page).
- **Counts.** The contents show "N open" per chapter from `comment.list` (200 at a time, up to 2,000), the button shows the project's total.
- **Not done.** Editing a comment within its 15 minutes (the op exists; delete, reply and resolve are on the screen), `comment.reanchor` from the screen, comments across paragraphs, and a per-reader progress marker.
- **The 60 comments an hour limit.** One person may write 60 on a project in an hour; the 40-comment walk stays under it (one person writes the 40, others write the replies and the keyboard comment).

### Suggestions

- **`suggestion.create` and `suggestion.list` gained a plain mode** (above): without it a rendered page can neither make nor draw a suggestion. `FEATURES` F-705, tested by `TestSuggestionsInPlainText` and `TestCommentPlainHintIsTheRenderedText`; the skill and the CLI snapshot were regenerated.
- **Drawn only when they can be placed.** A suggestion made on the text as it still is (`stale` false) is drawn; one whose chapter has changed since is listed with its warning and not drawn, because its place cannot be known exactly; it can still be accepted where its words are found again exactly once, or rejected. After any decision the chapter fragment is fetched again, so the page never shows text the server no longer holds.
- **Suggesting is a mode, the marks are not.** Turning Suggesting on lets a person make suggestions; open suggestions are drawn for everyone who can read them whether it is on or not, so an editor reviews them in context without a mode.

### The writing view

- **Comment marks stay out of the editor.** The writing view's one decoration slot is the alternatives' (`docs/EDITOR-SPIKE.md`): marks, ghosts and the shell's rules for them are rebuilt only on a few events, and typing must stay under 16 ms. A second family of marks in the same slot would have to be mapped through every edit as well and would add a second answer that can go stale while typing. The Comments tab and the reading view show the anchors; `doc.js`, `tree.js`, `editor.js` and `alts.js` did not change. `inspector.js` gained one entry for the tab.
- **The tab is lazy and its sheet is lazy** (`review.js` adds `review.css` when the tab is first opened), so the writing page's own CSS budget (20.5 KB of 25) is untouched.

### Share links

- **One address, two answers.** `GET /s/{token}` answers a page to a request that asks for `text/html` and the JSON manifest to one that does not, exactly as before. Every other path under `/s/` stays JSON. The page is a shell that holds nothing of the project (not its title) and is identical for a link with a passphrase and one without, so it tells a stranger nothing the JSON did not; the script fetches everything through `/s/{token}/api/...`. The sweep (`TestNoPublicRouteLeaksAndEveryAnswerIsLockedDown`) is unchanged and still walks the table; `TestAShareLinkOpensAPageThatHoldsNothingAndLeadsNowhere` and `TestEveryLinkThatOpensNothingGetsTheSamePage` add the page, with the same header rules, and check that no token reaches a log line.
- **One page for everything that opens nothing.** A token that is garbage, unknown, wrong, revoked, expired, of a project in the trash, or a path that is not an endpoint: one 404 page with one body, whichever way it was asked (the JSON answer is unchanged). It says neither "revoked" nor "expired" nor "passphrase".
- **No frame, no cookie, no way in.** The page uses `layout-bare` (no account menu, no palette, no manifest, no service worker registration, no theme preference, a skip link and landmarks), and the service worker is told to leave `/s/` alone. `fetch` is made with `credentials: "omit"` and `referrerPolicy: "no-referrer"`. The proof of an unlock and the name for the next comment are in `sessionStorage`, nothing in `localStorage`. A language comes from `Accept-Language` and is stored nowhere.
- **A first visit to a locked link logs a 401 in the browser's console.** The script asks for the contents first (a locked link answers 401, which is how it knows to ask for the passphrase), and a browser logs every refused request. The end-to-end scripts allow it for those steps. Making the page know in advance would make it differ between links, which the test above forbids.
- **The link is shown once.** It is on the answer to the form that made it, in a read-only box with the path (the script puts this installation's address in front of it: the server does not know its public address) and a copy button; the list, a reload and the JSON list never show it. A reverse proxy's access log will still contain the path.
- **Parts, not just the whole.** The picker lists the manuscript's parts, chapters, scenes and front and back matter with their depth; a part brings what is inside it. Which parts a link shows cannot be changed afterwards from the page (`share.update` can): the page says to make another link, so what a person was shown never changes under them.
- **Public page limits.** Chapters load as they are reached (a sentinel and a button for the whole text, for printing); marks for a guest's own threads come from the hints the document answer carries.

### Interface language and right-to-left text

- **Catalogue, not a framework.** `internal/i18n` (see ARCHITECTURE, "Interface language and text direction"). Page strings are looked up on the page (`Page.T`), not in the template function map (which is bound when the templates are parsed and knows no request); fragments rendered without a page need the language handed to them (none of the new ones is).
- **The language is a preference** (`language` in `prefs.set`, validated against the catalogue), not the profile's `locale` (that is a formatting locale and has its own list); Settings has one select for it under Look. A language with no file is refused (422).
- **Strings built in a script come from the page**, with English defaults in the call; a test keeps the defaults and the catalogue equal, and the pseudo-locale test shows any string a script built by itself (it would be English in a bracketed page).
- **Direction is per book, from its language; no new setting.** The brief's "a `dir` setting per manuscript/document (rtl/ltr/auto via the language)" is the language (auto) plus `unicode-bidi: plaintext` per paragraph (the per-paragraph `dir="auto"`); an explicit override is not offered because a book written right to left in a language not on the list would need only its language set, and nothing needs a book whose language says one thing and whose direction another. If one does, `project.update` would need a `direction` field and a migration.
- **Tested with Arabic and Hebrew** in the editor round trip (`unicode-rtl-arabic.md`, `unicode-rtl-hebrew.md`, with emphasis, a list, a quotation, a link and Latin and digits inside), in the reading view and the writing view (`TestManuscriptDirectionIsHonouredWhereTheTextIsShown`) and in the share route's text. A real right-to-left reader, caret movement and selection in the editor with an input method, and printing, were not tried.
- **Not done:** a translation of any screen (the catalogue holds English only), plural rules beyond "one" and "many" keys, number and date formats from the language (dates on the new screens are UTC text, as on the Git pages), and an RTL mirror of the older screens' physical-property CSS.

### Import pages: Word and Obsidian

- **Same page, same preview.** `import.js` gained `fromDocx` and two controls (the heading level of a Word file; "this is an Obsidian vault", which sends `layout: "obsidian"`), and grew from 6.0 to 6.4 KB gzip inside its 6.5 KB limit; changing either control makes the preview again. The preview already carries the warnings of the conversion and the skipped list, so nothing new was drawn: a `.canvas` file, a dropped `dataview` block and a picture's note show up where a skipped file did.
- **Obsidian is a choice**, never detected (a `.obsidian` folder does not switch it on), as the backend decided. A Word file is sent as base64 like a zip, so the same 34 MiB limit applies, said in words.
- **Checked with** a `.docx` made by this program's own compile (not Word's, not Google's) and a vault written by the test; real Word and Google exports, and a vault with thousands of notes, were not tried through the page.

### The fix round after review (review, sharing, people, console)

- **Diagnostics keep patterns and classes, not requests.** The log ring holds the route pattern a request matched (`GET /s/{token}`, never the path), an error's class (`ops:internal`, `*fs.PathError`) and never its text; messages and attributes are scrubbed of absolute paths (the data directory first) and whole URLs besides emails, tokens and bearer values. A canary test drives a panic and a failed request through the real logging paths.
- **The console is by id and in pages** (50 to a page, `admin.user.get`); its ops answer 404, not 403, to anyone else (`Auth.NotFoundForOthers`), as the pages do.
- **A plain suggestion refuses what the page does not show**: HTML comments, private notes, ghosted text and a link's address covered only in part. The AI path uses the same hidden check.
- **Share create redirects** and the one-time link is held in the session's short-lived flash (two minutes, in memory, taken once): a reload shows nothing and makes no second link. If the server restarts between the redirect and the page the link is not shown again and a new one is made.
- **The bidi rule for each paragraph is for right-to-left books only.** In an Arabic book the first letter of a Latin paragraph still turns that line round (a mixed-direction book wants it); in an English book it never does, whatever script is typed (e2e types the first letter into both).
- **A guest's comments belong to the link.** The page says "Guest: Name" for each, and "(you)" only for what this tab made (session storage), and no longer says "You see only your own comments". A new comment's draft is kept per link in session storage until it is sent or cancelled; replies are not.
- **Times**: one form, the person's own zone and language, the full time in the title; the review page writes UTC and a small script (`when.js`) localises it, so it reads without one. The console and the share links list stay UTC.
- **The frame around a page** (header, project menu, palette) is older than Phase 5 and is not in the catalogue; the sweep test covers each page's own content. The export dialog and the older screens are not in the sweep.
- **Found on the way:** the plain hint cut a multi-byte letter in two (Arabic, Hebrew, accents) because `PlainBlock.Visible` ranged over the text by rune; fixed with a test. A chapter titled only "Chapter 4" is headed "Chapter 4", not "Chapter 4: Chapter 4", in the compile and the files.
- **Budgets raised**: `review.js` 12 to 14 KB, `share.js` 7 to 8 KB, `read.js` 3 to 3.5 KB; `arm.js` (1.75 KB) and `when.js` (0.6 KB) are new. All stylesheets together are 46.0 KB of the 64 KB cap.
- **The exported files changed too**: the "Chapter 4: Chapter 4" fix is in `publish.HeadingText`, so a chapter titled by its number is headed with the number once in the DOCX, EPUB and the preview. Plurals in the compile and import warnings are a small Go helper ("1 document was"), not catalogue keys; the page's own counts use `one`/`other` keys (`Page.N`).
- **Not done**: one solid Accept among several on the review page (each card keeps its own; the brief did not say which should stand out); the preset delete on the Compile page still uses the browser's own question; a guest's reply drafts are kept in memory (through a redraw), not across a reload; the "CHAPTER 1" label takes the book's language from the catalogue, which has only English, so nothing changes yet; "Untitled" (the placeholder every screen shows for an empty title, `displayTitle`) and the "bytes" unit are not in the catalogue and are named in the sweep's allow-list, as are the system and queue identifiers on the diagnostics page; option labels on Compile come from the ops' English descriptions.

### Compile, the administrators' console and the menu

- **Compile builds its form from the ops, not from a list in the page.** The server hands the script the preset list (with options resolved) and each option's type and enum read from the schema of `compile.preview`'s options; the page only knows how to group and label the keys it has words for (`cmp.js.o_*`, `v_*`), and shows any other under "More" with its key humanised. A format or an option another branch adds appears. The preview is drawn from `pages[].blocks[]`; when a preview carries `print.pages[]` (placed lines) those are drawn instead, "as placed", from `lines` or `blocks`. **The placed-lines shape was written to the description of it, not tested against a real PDF preview: it needs a check when the PDF branch merges.**
- **Compile is for editors; others get a 404**, not a 403: it is a tool, and a reviewer who cannot compile need not be told it exists. (The review and share pages say 403 to a member who cannot use them.)
- **The download goes through a UI route** so the manifest summary can ride in headers (the op's REST answer is the file alone). The page says whether the input hash equals the preview's, and "No" with a reason when the book changed in between.
- **Presets**: saving takes the changes from the built-in the preset is based on (and emptied text boxes as `clear`); updating stores the full set. Deleting asks first.
- **Contact block**: visible to every editor by design; the screen says so before the form, and nothing in logs, events, manifests or Git holds it (tested).
- **Export dialog**: a three-way choice rather than a checkbox, because the default differs by layout (a zip is the full copy). The notice is built from the `X-Export-Skipped` header the route already set.
- **Do-not-compile in the outline** is not added: the outline's rows do not carry the flag, and the inspector's checkbox already is the toggle.
- **Menu by role**: `ptabs` takes the caller's role when the page has it (all project pages that have `.Data.Project` do); the menu then hides Review, Compile and Share links from roles that cannot use them. A page without a role shows all three.

### Administration

- **A disabled state beside the removed one.** `users.disabled_at` (migration 0110) is the reversible state; `deleted_at` stays the removal. Every place that turns a credential into a person checks both: the session lookup, the API-key lookup, the sign-in (`SignIn` refuses with "switched off"), `Users.Get`, and adding someone to a project or a Compendium by email. A live event stream authenticates again at every heartbeat through the same two lookups, so it ends with the session. MCP is on another branch and uses the key lookup. Share endpoints are unaffected: guests are not users, and a switched-off owner's links keep working (revoke them if that is not wanted; the owner's own actions on them need a session).
- **Roles from the identity provider.** At every sign-in an instance role is set again from the IdP's groups. So `admin.user.set_role` works only where no administrator group is configured (administration is then sticky once given, which is how claim mode already behaves) and says so otherwise; a writer or reader set here is set again from their groups at the next sign-in. The console shows no role controls where the IdP decides.
- **The last administrator** who is switched on cannot be switched off or demoted, and you cannot switch yourself off. A switched-off administrator does not count.
- **Typed confirmation is the screen's**: the ops take no confirmation (the CLI and agents have dry runs and the audit log); the handler checks the typed name against the person's name.
- **Quotas are read-only** (F-716): the limits are values captured when the attachment ops are registered and applied to everyone alike; a per-person override needs a table, a check inside `checkQuota` and a decision about who may raise a limit, and is not a seam that exists. Projects are not limited.
- **Diagnostics** read counts, never rows. The table names come from the schema (and match `^[a-z0-9_]+$`), the config shape from an allow-list with a test that fails for any config field not classified, queue states from four job tables, disk by area (a file's name is never recorded), and the log from a ring (200 lines) that keeps only a short list of attributes, scrubbed as they are kept and again when the bundle is built. The scrubber (`admin.Scrub`) removes link tokens, bearer and basic credentials, credentials in URLs, email addresses, query strings and any long secret-looking run. The mirror's own state is not in it (it has no table); git pushes, imports and AI runs are.
- **Not done:** an audit-log viewer, the OIDC test, the group-to-role editor (F-320, F-329), a storage report with orphan cleanup (F-332), and SMTP.

## Phase 5 and Phase 4 screens: what the merge left as it was

- Two guards for actions that cannot be undone exist side by side. The AI screens (`ai.js`, `aiadmin.js`, `versions.js`) arm the button itself and take a confirming key only as a new press after the first key was let go. The review, sharing and people pages use `arm.js`, which opens a small panel with focus on Cancel and the confirming button off for half a second. Both ignore a held Enter and a double click; neither can be reached by mistake. They are not one piece of code because the two sets of pages were built apart; unify them when either set is next changed.
- The Comments tab of the writing view's inspector is shown to people who can edit. A reviewer reads and comments in the reading view (`/p/{slug}/read`), not in the writing view.
- FEATURES sections: the Phase 4 screens section is lettered AF, so that every section letter is used once (AC is the Phase 5 review and sharing backend).

## Phase 5 release notes: what is not verified, and decisions taken at the tag

Not verified:

- The merged tree (Phase 4 screens plus Phase 5 screens) had no independent review of its own. Each side was reviewed and fixed alone; the merge was checked by the full CI run, the rest of the browser scripts, and by hand at the places the two sides meet (the inspector's tabs, the project menu, the key chords, the roles).
- The AI screens' words are written in the templates and scripts, not in the i18n catalogue, and they were not looked at under the right-to-left pseudo-locale. The review, sharing, compile and admin screens are in the catalogue.
- Nothing here has run against a real AI provider, a real mail or chat service for notifications, or a real device. Share links were exercised against this server only.
- The two confirm guards (see above) are still two pieces of code.

Decided at the tag:

- A reviewer is not offered the project's AI page: the tab and the menu entry are hidden, as Share links is hidden from everyone but the owner. Every page that knows the caller's role now passes it to the desktop and the phone tabs as well as to the menu (it used to reach only the menu, so the desktop tabs offered everything).
- Review's keys in the reading view (Shift and the arrows to extend a selection, C to comment, S for a suggestion) use no letter with Alt+Shift (Alt with the arrows only widens the selection to words), so they cannot clash with the writing view's Alt+Shift letter chords, and they act only inside the reading view.
- The inspector's tabs (Details, Versions, In this scene, Alternatives, Stash, Comments, Lab, and AI when it is on) wrap onto two or three rows in the 280 px panel, at 1280 px and at 390 px. Nothing is clipped and nothing scrolls sideways; a single row would need either shorter names or a scrolling strip, and both are worse for a keyboard and for a screen reader than wrapping.

## Phase 6 site decisions

Static site publishing for blog mode (roadmap Phase 6, F-234), as built: backend only, the screens are the UI agent's. Code: `internal/site` (the pure generator, its folder writer `dir.go` and its checker `check/`), `internal/domain/site` (selection, options, the ops), `compile.ResolveImages` (the images come through the compile's door); migration `1140`; ops `site.options.get/set`, `site.doc.list/set`, `site.preview`, `site.build`, `site.publish`, `site.status`. Tables: [DATA-MODEL](DATA-MODEL.md#site_options-site_docs-site_state). Operating it: [OPERATIONS](OPERATIONS.md#publishing-a-static-site).

### What was checked, and what was not

- **Checked, by tests that fail on a regression.** A Go checker (`internal/site/check`, standard library only) runs over every site the tests build: each page tokenizes as well-formed XML (so the generator writes HTML that is also XML: void elements closed, only the five entities), one `<h1>`, one `<main>`, a title, `lang` and `dir`, unique ids, an `alt` on every image, no script, style, iframe, form, object, `svg` element, `style` attribute or event handler; every `href` and `src` resolves to a file in the site, fragments included, an outside address is allowed only as a text link with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` (never loaded), no `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:` or scheme-relative address, root-relative addresses only on the 404 page; the stylesheet has no `@import`, `url(`, gradient, shadow or `@font-face`; RSS 2.0 and Atom parse strictly (no DTD, no CDATA) with the elements a reader needs, RFC 822 and RFC 3339 dates, unique GUIDs and ids, absolute links, and a feed body that is itself well-formed with absolute addresses; the sitemap lists absolute, unique locations; every path is safe (no `..`, no backslash, no case collision, no name Windows reserves, no trailing dot or space). Mutation tests break each rule on a good site and check that the checker names it.
- **Canaries.** `TestNothingHiddenReachesTheSite` (the generator) and `TestSiteBuildIsADeterministicZipThatPassesItsCheck` and `TestSitePublishWritesTheFolderAndOnlyThat` (through REST, a real database and a real folder) put a secret in every place the brief names and search the whole tree, the zip bytes, the feeds, the export manifest and the published folder: a private note (inline and block), ghosted text, an HTML comment, front matter, the alternatives annotation, a `%%` inside an image description and a wiki label, a footnote only a private note refers to, a document marked do not compile, a draft, an idea, a note document, a draft scene in a done chapter, a research note, the contact block (name, email, address, phone), a comment, a revision label **and an old revision whose text the current one lacks**, a suggestion, an alternative's variant, the stash, a Compendium entity (name, summary, body), the synopsis fields, the title of a part that is not published, and all five members' emails. Not canaried by name, because they are not read by this code at all: Compendium and stash are covered by the entity and stash canaries above; events, the mirror and Git are not touched (the site writes no events and is in neither).
- **A browser.** The generated sample site was served by a small Go server with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'`, `nosniff` and no directory listing, and loaded in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px, light and dark, over the front page, a post with a picture, a footnote and a quote, a post with lists, a table and code, the About page, the tag index, a second listing page and a post with an Arabic title: no console message, no CSP violation, no request to another origin, no `<script>`, `<style>` or `style=` in the live DOM, no horizontal scroll; the screenshots were looked at. The same site was opened from `file://` (no server), clicked through from the front page to a post (a picture, a stylesheet one folder down), back, to the tag index and a tag. A right-to-left site (`ar`) was built and looked at at both widths: the column, navigation, cover and pager mirror, and the stylesheet uses logical properties only (a test pins it).
- **Publication is a person's act (changed after the Phase 6 review).** `site.publish`, `site.options.set` and `site.doc.set` were callable with a write-scope key, over REST and over `/mcp` (new groups were exposed by default), and a write key pinned to one project could publish its done chapters to the public folder. They are now session-only, like `share.create`: an API key of any scope gets 403 and they are not tools. `site.build` stays open to keys (the zip goes to whoever asked, and nothing becomes public), as do `site.status`, `site.options.get`, `site.doc.list` and `site.preview`. The command line signs in with a key, so `scriobh-cli site publish`, `site options set` and `site doc set` no longer work; the Site page does them.
- **Not checked, and only these can confirm it.** Real hosting: the Caddy and nginx snippets in OPERATIONS were written from those servers' documentation and not run. Feed readers: the feeds pass a strict XML parse and the checker's rules, not NetNewsWire, Feedly or the W3C validator. Search engines: the sitemap and robots.txt were not submitted to any. A screen reader was not run (the page has a skip link, landmarks, `lang`, `dir`, an `alt` on every image and contrast-checked colours; that is not an audit). Safari and Firefox were not used. Windows file systems: the names are checked for the reserved ones and for case collisions, not written to NTFS.
- **Measured** (Apple silicon, a loaded machine, so read these as upper bounds). The generator alone, 2,000 posts and 218,000 words with tags, categories and 50-per-page pagination: 94 ms, 163 MB allocated, 2,416 files, 10.9 MB, a 3.0 MB zip in 48 ms. **Through the registry and the database** (`TestSitePreviewOf2000Chapters`: 2,000 chapters made with `doc.create`, then `site.preview` over REST): 70 ms for 210,000 words; the build, with the zip, 97 ms. One 4 MB post with 40,000 footnote references, a 50,000-item list and 200 nested quotes: 650 ms and 48 MB out (the footnote copies are bounded by the compile's 16 MB budget). The tests assert 6 s, 10 s and 1 GB (ten times the time under the race detector); they catch a slip to quadratic work, not a regression of tens of percent.

### Decisions

- **Posts.** A chapter is a post; a scene inside one is folded in after its text with a scene break (as the compile does); a scene outside any chapter is a post of its own; a part is a container, never a post, and its title is its chapters' category only if the part could itself be published (see below); front and back matter documents are pages and what is inside one is part of it; `options.posts: top_level` makes every top-level document a post and folds everything under it. A document's own `role` (`post`, `page`, `skip`) wins. A document has one place in the site: **a chapter nested in a chapter folds into it** (after its own text, like a scene), and so does anything in a post that is not itself forced to be one; nothing is published twice.
- **Status.** Only `done` is published; `include_drafts` adds `draft` and `revise`; `idea` and `outline` are plans and never are. The status of a scene inside a chapter counts: **a draft scene in a done chapter is left out** (with the reason), and so are its words. `documents.status` has no `published` value and none was added.
- **The same rules as a compile and a share link.** `include_in_compile` is a subtree rule (the document or any ancestor): leaving a chapter out of a compile leaves it out of the site. Notes (kind `note`) and everything in one are never published and are counted, not listed. Research and Compendium documents are not in the source. `ai_exclude` means nothing here.
- **A part's title is text too.** A part that is marked do not compile, skipped, or whose status does not allow publishing gives its chapters no category, so a planning title never leaks through a category page. A part's own text is not published and the preview lists it as left out ("a part is a container"); `role: post` publishes it. **Role `skip` on any document, part or chapter hides its whole subtree**, and so does every other reason a document is not published (status, do not compile, a trashed parent): see "Inherited blocking" below.
- **No publication date existed.** `documents.story_date` is in-story time. The date is a field of its own in `site_docs`, not a column of `documents` (that keeps the document write path, `SourceDoc`, the mirror and Git out of it). No date means the date the document was created, with a `no_date` warning; the date is shown as `YYYY-MM-DD` and keeps its time of day for feeds. **A date in the future publishes immediately**: nothing consults the clock (that is also what makes the output deterministic); schedule by not publishing.
- **Tags and excerpts are the site's own.** `document_tags` exists but has no ops and holds the manuscript's private labels; front matter is dropped by the structure builder and is not read. So tags are per post in `site_docs` (up to 10, 40 characters, folded to one), and the excerpt is the owner's `excerpt` or the post's first paragraph. **The synopsis fields are never the excerpt**: they are planning notes and could say what the post does not. A missing excerpt is a `no_excerpt` warning, not an error.
- **Slugs.** Letters and digits of any script, lower-cased, Latin accents folded (`é` to `e`, `ß` to `ss`) and other marks kept, everything else one hyphen, at most 60 bytes, never a name Windows reserves, never a dot or a slash; an emoji-only title gets `post-<hash>`; collisions are compared folded (case and normalization) and settled by a suffix made of the document's id, oldest first and explicit addresses first, so an older post keeps its address when a newer one is added. Posts, pages, tags and categories each have a namespace of their own; `pages/index.html` is reserved.
- **Addresses.** Every link is relative and names its `.html` file, so the site opens from any folder, from a sub-path and from `file://`. Tag and category listings are `tags/<slug>/index.html`, `…/2.html`; the front page is `index.html`, `page/2.html`. The 404 page cannot use relative links; with a base address its links are absolute, without one they start at `/`.
- **Without a base address** there are no feeds, no sitemap and no canonical links (a feed needs absolute links and a relative one is invalid): the preview warns and the pages carry no dead link to a feed. `noindex` writes `Disallow: /`, a `noindex` meta tag, no sitemap and no canonical link.
- **Feeds.** `dc:creator`, never `<author>` (it must be an email); the site's name stands in for an author the project lacks. GUIDs are UUID-shaped hashes of the project and document ids with `isPermaLink="false"`, so renaming a post, changing its address or moving the site leaves them alone. The feed's build date is the newest post's date, never the clock. Full content is escaped HTML in `content:encoded` and Atom `content type="html"`, not CDATA (a `]]>` in a post cannot break it); its image and footnote addresses are absolute. **Atom `updated` equals the publication date** (edits are not tracked: a changed post does not re-appear in readers).
- **Images.** Only the project's own PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP attachments, resolved by `compile.ResolveImages` (sniffed from the bytes, checked against the stored hash, pixel-bounded). **A `data:` picture is never published** (a bracketed note, in the generator and in the caller); an SVG, a remote or a missing one is a bracketed note and a warning. Every picture's EXIF, XMP, IPTC and comments are stripped before it is named (GIF and WebP too; a JPEG keeps its colour profile and Adobe marker) and a file that cannot be read cleanly is not published. The file name is the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the stripped bytes; width and height come from the decoded header. A post with an image without a description gets a `missing_alt` warning.
- **No JavaScript, no search.** A progressive search needs script and an index file; it was left out, as the brief allowed.
- **Fonts.** System fonts only. The embedded Newsreader subset was not copied: the system serif stack costs nothing and loads nothing. The site's own words ("Older posts", "min read", "Skip to the content", "All pages") are English; the language option sets `lang` and the direction, and paragraphs, headings and lists carry `dir="auto"`.
- **The cover.** `show_cover` puts the project's generated SVG cover on the front page as an `<img>` (an SVG as an image cannot run script; the checker allows only plain shapes and text in an SVG file). There are no social cards (no outside request, no raster font).
- **Accents.** Five (`moss`, `slate`, `sepia`, `meadow`, `contrast`): the app's Paper and Ink colours with the accent chosen; each is checked at build time (and by a test) at 4.5:1 for the accent, the secondary and the muted text on the page and inset backgrounds of both themes (a Go port of what `scripts/check-contrast.py` holds the app to); a choice that fails is replaced by moss with a warning. A custom colour is not offered. Slate, sepia and meadow have dark values chosen here (the design document gives only the light ones).
- **Navigation.** At most six pages, then "All pages" and a page that lists them, because every page is in the navigation of every page and the output would otherwise grow as pages times files.
- **Delivery.** `site.build` is a download like `export.compile` (a deflated zip with a fixed time and sorted entries), recorded in `exports` as format `html` and preset `site` (the table's format list has no zip value and changing it means rebuilding the table); `site.publish` writes a folder. The preview and the build share one function, so their `input_hash` agree (a test). Publishing needs the administrator: `SCRIOBH_SITE_DIR` or `SCRIOBH_SITE_PUBLISH=1`; the folder is never read from a request (a test sends `dir`, `path` and `root` in the body and finds nothing written there). The site is `<root>/<slug>--<id6>/public`; the name is found again by the id tail and by the record's project id, so a rename keeps it. **An API key of the write scope can publish** (it is an editor-level write like a compile, and the folder is the administrator's); a read key can preview and read status. **A trashed project cannot be published** (409) though the owner may still preview and build (like every export); what was published stays where it is until the project is purged, which removes it by the same rules; an administrator who wants it down sooner removes the folder. Nothing is published automatically and nothing is scheduled.
- **Not built: the Git branch.** "Push `site/` to a separate branch" needs the Git engine to hold a second local branch with its own snapshot and divergence rule per remote; the engine is one branch of the manuscript, its remote's branch is a setting of the remote, and the push refuses any path that is not a document. That is not a small extension, so it is not built. A project folder under `SCRIOBH_SITE_DIR` can be a Git repository of the administrator's own.
- **Not built, either:** search, scheduled publishing, publish webhooks or pings, comments, a translated interface for the site's words, the project's own raster cover, pretty URLs without `.html` (a proxy does that), a change list since the last publish, Newsreader.

### What a screen must do

A **Publish** page for a project (a blog project's own tab, reachable from the Export dialog for any project):

1. Load `site.status`, `site.options.get` and `site.preview` together. Show the **status** line: "Published 2 March, 14 posts, 3 pages" from `publish.at` and the counts, "Never published" when `publish.at` is empty, "Changed since" when the preview's `input_hash` differs from `publish.input_hash` (and the same for the last build). If `publish_enabled` is false, say why in one sentence (the administrator has not set a folder) and offer only the download; if `enabled` is false, say the server has site publishing off and show nothing else.
2. Show the **posts** (newest first) with title, date (and `date_from: created` as a nudge to set one), reading time, excerpt (`excerpt_from: body` as a nudge), tags and category, each with an edit affordance that calls `site.doc.set` (role, address, date, excerpt, tags; an address conflict is a 409 with a message). Show the **pages** the same way, and the **left out** list with each document's reason and a one-click `site.doc.set` `role: post` or `skip`, so a document marked do not compile or still a draft is visibly not published.
3. Show the **warnings** grouped by code with the document (`warnings[].document_id`, `title`), the site-wide ones (`no_base_url`, `no_author`, `about_missing`, `accent_replaced`, `drafts_included`) at the top; none blocks publishing.
4. **Options** form from `site.options.get` (`defaults`, `accents`): title, tagline, base address (the one that matters: say what it unlocks), language, posts per page, `include_drafts` (with the count of drafts it would add), feed content, the accent as five swatches, nav links, About page picker (a page document), footer, `noindex` ("This is a staging site"). Send only what changed to `site.options.set`; "reset" sends `options.clear`.
5. **Download** calls `site.build` and saves the zip (the REST route returns the file); **Publish** calls `site.publish` with a confirmation naming the location (`<folder>/public`, never a full path) and, if `replaced`, that the previous publish will be replaced; on a 409 show the message as is (it names the file that blocks it). Never publish on page load or on a timer.
6. Mobile (390 px): the lists are cards, the warnings collapse, the buttons are full width; keyboard: every control reachable, the confirm dialog traps focus and Escape cancels.

### Review notes

Fixes made after an independent look, each with a test that fails on the earlier code: `data:` pictures were published although the documents said never; a part that was skipped or not done still named its chapters' category; a part's text was dropped without a word; the skip link made a right-to-left page scroll sideways (a physical `left` offset) and the stylesheet used `margin-left` and friends (now logical properties, pinned by a test); a list item of one direction inside a list of the other overflowed; every page listed every page in its navigation; publish on a trashed project went through; the canary corpus lacked synopses, an old revision, a suggestion, an alternative, the stash and a Compendium entity.

### Phase 6 site: the fix round after an independent review

- **Inherited blocking (a privacy leak, fixed).** `choose` checked a folded document's own status and its nearest anchor but not the levels between, so chapter (done) > scene (draft, idea, skipped) > scene (done) published the inner text in the post, `feed.xml` and `atom.xml`. Now every document that is not published (status, role skip, do not compile, a parent in the trash, a note) is *blocked*, and a document whose parent is blocked is blocked: the documents come parents first, so the state flows down any depth, in `chapters` and in `top_level` mode, in front matter and under a skipped part. A part's status is the one exception (it is a container: its status keeps only its title out of a category; skip and do not compile on a part do hide its chapters). A scene restored alone from a trashed folder is re-parented to the top level by `doc.restore`, so it is a top-level document of its own and is published as one; a document whose parent is simply missing is excluded with the reason "its parent is in the trash". Test: `TestNothingUnderAnUnpublishedDocumentIsPublished` (both modes; draft, idea, outline, skipped and not-compiled intermediates three levels deep, a skipped and a draft chapter, front matter, a skipped part, a trashed intermediate, a top-level draft chapter), with canaries searched in every file of the zip (posts, index, `feed.xml`, `atom.xml`).
- **Full Unicode case folding.** `fold` (slug collisions) and the checker used `strings.ToLower`, so `ΟΔΟΣ` and `οδος` (final sigma), `İstanbul`/`istanbul`, `STRASSE`/`Straße`, ligatures and the Kelvin sign made two names that APFS and NTFS treat as one file, and `WriteDir` failed with "file exists" (a 500). Now both use `cases.Fold` over the NFC form (and the checker also over NFD), and `Slugify` folds the same way. A test builds 32 titles of this kind and asserts that no two output paths collide under fold+NFC and fold+NFD. A write that fails is a 409 with a reason ("two file names differ only in case or accents...", "Scríobh may not write to the publish folder", "the disk is full"), never a 500 and never a path.
- **Feeds and escaping.** The footnote link of a full-content feed body was written with the base address unescaped, so a base such as `https://example.org/a&b'c/` made the body not well-formed; every `href` and `src` the generator writes now goes through `esc` (audited), and a test builds sites with `&`, `'`, `"` and `<` in the base address and runs the checker over the feeds.
- **Pictures: an allow-list, applied everywhere (`internal/imagesafe`).** What each format keeps is named, and the rest goes: PNG keeps IHDR, PLTE, IDAT, IEND, tRNS, gAMA, cHRM, sRGB, iCCP, sBIT, bKGD, pHYs, acTL, fcTL, fdAT (caBX/C2PA, eXIf, tEXt, iTXt, zTXt, tIME, prVt and every unknown chunk go, and the bytes after IEND); WebP keeps VP8, VP8L, VP8X (its EXIF and XMP flags cleared), ALPH, ANIM, ANMF (cleaned inside) and ICCP; JPEG keeps its tables, frame and scan, a JFIF header rebuilt with its thumbnail fields zeroed and the thumbnail cut, Adobe's APP14 marker, an ICC profile only if it is well-formed, and drops every other APPn, JPGn and comment and everything after EOI; GIF keeps the frames, the graphic control and the loop count. WebP dimensions are read from the VP8, VP8L and VP8X headers and refused beyond 100 megapixels. The compile's image door (`describe`) calls it, so **the DOCX, EPUB and static site all get cleaned pictures; this closes the carry-forward that DOCX and EPUB images kept their EXIF**. The picture's `Hash` stays the hash of the stored file (so a preview, which cannot read the bytes, and an export agree on the input hash); `Data` is the cleaned file. The print (PDF) compile is on another branch and is not in this tree: it reads pictures through the same `describe`, so it gets the cleaning when the branches meet, but that was not run here (a merge test should put the GPS canary through it).
- **Orientation.** The EXIF orientation is read before the EXIF is dropped. For a JPEG with orientation 2 to 8 the picture is decoded, turned (all seven cases are tested with a pixel check and the new width and height), and written again at quality 92; the published width and height are those of the turned picture, the warning `image_rotated` says so (the colour profile of a photo taken in Display P3 may not survive the re-encode), and a photo over 25 megapixels is refused as too large rather than published sideways. PNG and WebP orientation notes are ignored. DOCX and EPUB receive the turned picture from the same function (a DOCX cannot take a matrix).
- **Right to left.** `<pre>` and `<code>` carry `dir="ltr"`; the previous and next arrows and the pager follow the site's direction (the arrow is its own aria-hidden span, the label its own `dir="auto"` span, so an ellipsis stays at the logical end of a title); the skip link hides by `translateY` rather than a physical offset. The `ar` sample was built and looked at at 390 and 1280 px: no sideways scroll, arrows pointing the right way, code left to right.
- **Smaller.** (a) *Stable addresses*, see F-771 and OPERATIONS: pinned in `site_docs.pinned_slug` (migration `1141`) at the first build or publish, used before new ones; the three repros (a backdated or newly done post with the same title, an explicit address taking an older post's, a title change) are `TestAnAddressThatIsOutDoesNotMove` and `TestPinnedAddressesStayWhateverArrives`. (b) *Edited files*: `checkManaged` compares the recorded SHA-256 and refuses with the names; `overwrite_edited: true` on `site.publish` replaces them; purge still removes. (c) Nested chapters fold into their parent chapter (the code did; this document said otherwise). (d) `ParsePublished` range-checks both forms (1970 to 9999); zip dates clamp to 1980 through 2107 and stay deterministic. (e) `compile.ResolveImages` returns the resolver's warnings; the site passes `image_skipped` ("too large") through, and the per-post `image_missing` now says "could not be published (not found, too large, or not a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP)". (f) WebP bounded, see above. (g) `pages` is `[]`, not `null`, and so are `posts` and `nav` (`TestNoSiteOrCompileResultHasANull` walks every site result and the compile reads for a null). (h) The purge hook takes the per-project lock (`TestThePurgeWaitsForARunningPublish`).
- **A concurrency gate.** `site.preview`, `site.build` and `site.publish` run inside a small semaphore (`internal/domain/site/gate.go`) read from **`SCRIOBH_COMPILE_CONCURRENCY`** (default 2, 1 to 32), waiting two seconds and then answering 429 "busy". It mirrors `compile.Gate` of the print branch (same variable, same default, same answer) as a separate type because that branch is not in this tree; when the branches meet, the config field `CompileConcurrency` is declared twice (identical) and the compile gate can be passed to these ops in place of this one (they only need `Acquire`). The service is registered as `site.limits`.
- **`SCRIOBH_SITE_DIR` outside the data directory.** An explicit folder may not be the data directory, hold it, or lie inside it (`files/`); symlinks are resolved before the comparison. `SCRIOBH_SITE_PUBLISH=1` is the way to use `<data>/sites`.
- **Skip keeps children? No.** Role `skip` on a document hides its whole subtree (decided and implemented with the inheritance above); to publish a child of a skipped document, move it out of that document or take the skip off (a forced role does not escape a blocked parent).

## Phase 6 print decisions

Print output: a PDF written by this program, one HTML file with CSS paged media, and a print cover. **No screen is built.** Ops: `export.compile` and `compile.preview` took the formats `pdf` and `html-paged` (presets `print-6x9`, `print-5.5x8.5`, `print-5x8`, `print-a5`, `proof-letter`, `proof-a4`, `paged-html`); new ops `compile.cover_spec` and `export.cover`. No migration: `exports.format` already allowed `pdf` and `html` (the paged HTML is recorded as `html`). The plan said "paged HTML and headless Chromium"; that line (F-224, ARCHITECTURE) is changed: a browser or any other binary at runtime would break "one binary, no optional dependency".

### What was checked, and what was not

- **Checked, always:** every PDF the tests write is parsed by `check.PDF`, a reader that shares no code with the writer: header, one cross-reference table with exact 20-byte entries, the trailer (`/ID` twice, equal), every object where the table says and back to back, every stream exactly `/Length` long, every reference resolves and every object is reachable, the page tree (`/Parent`, `/Count`), page keys, boxes, resources, content-stream operators (only the 26 the writer uses, balanced `q`/`Q` and `BT`/`ET`, operand counts, fonts and XObjects that exist on the page, text only as whole two-byte hex codes that are in the font and never code 0), each font (Type 0, Identity-H, a CIDFontType2 of the same name with a tagged subset, an embedded TrueType whose table checksums, `head`, `loca`, `hmtx` and composite glyphs are checked and which carries no hinting and no table but the six it needs, `/W` and a ToUnicode map that agree code for code, blocks of at most 100), images (JPEG markers, Flate streams that inflate to exactly the rows promised, palettes, soft masks), the outline (links, counts, titles) and page labels; and **no** `/JavaScript`, `/JS`, `/OpenAction`, `/AA`, `/Launch`, `/URI`, `/GoToR`, `/SubmitForm`, `/AcroForm`, `/EmbeddedFile`, `/Names`, `/Annots`, `/Metadata`, no encryption, no link annotation (the writer emits none: the validator's rule is zero), and an Info dictionary of hex UTF-16BE strings. Seventeen mutation tests (same-length byte changes) break one rule each and check the validator names it. The tests also compare the text the validator extracts through each font's ToUnicode map with the book's paragraphs (in order, with all whitespace removed, head and foot lines left out), check that the preview's page count, page labels and each chapter's start page are the file's, and pin the SHA-256 of two sample files written with uncompressed streams (`testdata/golden-pdf.txt`; the compressor cannot move it; `SCRIOBH_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` rewrites it when the layout is meant to change).
- **Checked with a second, independent implementation (macOS PDFKit and CoreGraphics, Preview's own engine, driven by a small Swift script that is not committed):** the files open; the page count, page size, title, author, page labels (`i`, `ii`, ... then `1`) and outline are read; text extracts; pages render. **Looked at by eye**, on pages rendered from the final code: a title page; a copyright page beside a dedication; a contents page (leader dots aligned, no running head); a part page; a chapter opener with its sink, the label and the title, no head, the number at the foot and the first paragraph not indented; a left and right spread with running heads, page numbers, a scene-break ornament, a bulleted list, a quotation and a footnote under its rule (the rule clear of the text: a first render had the body running into the notes, which `TestBodyAndNotesFitThePage` now pins). Looked at in earlier renders whose layout has not changed since: a table, a numbered and a nested list, a code block, a JPEG, a drop cap with a chapter ornament, crop marks, an endnotes page with the number in the head, and the cover wrap with guides and the barcode box. The repeated table header was checked by a test only, not by eye. That is all that was looked at: not every page of every file.
- **Chromium** (the Playwright build in `scripts/`) printed the paged HTML with `preferCSSPageSize`: 432 x 648 pt pages, mirrored margins, running heads and page numbers, the closed Content-Security-Policy (no console message, no request outside the file) and hostile titles shown as text. What it does not do is in F-224 above.
- **Not checked, and only these can confirm it:** that a **printer or a proofing service** accepts the files (no vendor was available: PDF/X, PDF/A, colour profiles, bleed and margin rules are theirs); that **Acrobat**, a hardware RIP or any reader other than PDFKit and Chromium's PDF viewer opens them cleanly; the files with **qpdf, poppler or mutool** (none was installed; `SCRIOBH_PDFTOOLS=1 go test ./internal/publish -run ExternalTools` runs them when present and was never run by its author); the paged HTML in Firefox and Safari or in Prince, WeasyPrint and Paged.js.
- **Measured** (Apple silicon, a quiet machine): 150,000 words in 75 chapters, 6 x 9 in at 11 pt: layout 52 to 110 ms, layout and writing 150 to 210 ms, 456 pages, a 1.09 MB file, about 700 MB allocated in all (garbage collected as it goes), the validator 74 to 128 ms. 2,000,000 words (the export's cap): about 2.1 s, a 14.4 MB file, 1.7 GB of heap in use at the end. Hostile inputs (a 120,000-word paragraph, a 100,000-character word, a 4,000-row table, forty nested quotes, thirty nested lists, 20,000 code lines, 3,000 headings, 60,000 random characters) each in under 0.2 s. With the race detector the bounds are ten times looser and the whole `publish` suite takes 25 s.
- The binary grew from 30,601,650 to 34,065,378 bytes (+3.46 MB, budget 40 MB): 2.96 MB of fonts, the rest code.

### Fonts and licences

- **Liberation Serif** (regular, italic, bold, bold italic) and **Liberation Mono** (regular), version 2.1.5, SIL Open Font License 1.1: the book faces. Liberation Serif is metric-compatible with Times New Roman and Liberation Mono with Courier New, so a manuscript's font choice means the same thing in the DOCX and the PDF. **DejaVu Serif** and **DejaVu Sans**, version 2.37, the Bitstream Vera licence (free to use, copy, embed and sell with software, modified copies must be renamed): fallbacks only, for characters the Liberation faces do not have (the fleuron, symbols, arrows, rarer letters).
- The UI fonts under `web/static/fonts` were not used: they are WOFF2 (Brotli, which the Go standard library cannot decode), Newsreader is one variable file with no italic or bold, and that directory is served and has a four-file budget. The new files are in `internal/publish/font/data/` with their licences, their hashes (`SOURCES.txt`, pinned by `TestEmbeddedFontsAreTheOriginals`) and the repository's first `NOTICE` file. They are embedded **unmodified** (the OFL asks that a modified version drop the reserved name) and cut down to the glyphs used only inside each PDF, which both licences allow. They came from the LibreOffice 26.8 bundle on the build machine, which ships them unchanged.
- **What the faces cannot do:** the `kern` table is read, GPOS and GSUB are not (no ligatures, no small caps, no contextual forms, no mark positioning); text is normalised to NFC so most accented letters are precomposed; Arabic, Hebrew, Syriac, Thaana, the Indic scripts, Thai, Lao, Tibetan, Myanmar, Khmer, Mongolian, Han, Hiragana, Katakana and Hangul are **never drawn** (they would be drawn wrongly: shaping and direction are not done); each such character is a question mark and the warning `script_unsupported` names the scripts. A right-to-left language is refused outright by the export (the preview says `layout_refused`). Characters in no face are a question mark and `missing_glyph` lists the first twelve code points. Control characters, joiners, bidirectional marks and overrides, the soft hyphen, the byte order mark and variation selectors are dropped silently. Nothing ever draws glyph 0: the validator fails a file that does.
- A DOCX font name that is not embedded (Georgia, Cambria, Garamond, Palatino Linotype) falls back to Liberation Serif in a PDF with `font_substituted`.

### Typesetting decisions

- **Integers.** Every length is an integer in thousandths of a point, and the break arithmetic (ratios, badness, demerits) is integer. Go may fuse a multiply and an add on arm64 and not on amd64, which can move a line break; the break arithmetic is integer, and the few conversions from a float option (`math.Round(v * k)`, one multiplication, no addition to fuse) happen once. That is the design, and the pinned-bytes test passes both built for arm64 and built for amd64 (run under Rosetta on an Apple-silicon machine), which is the evidence for it; no physical amd64 machine was used. Float options (size, margins) are converted once, with rounding.
- **Line breaking.** Knuth-Plass total fit, in three passes of rising tolerance (badness 100, 800, any), with the paper's line penalty, adjacent-fitness and forced-break rules, variable measure for the lines beside a drop cap, and a first-fit fallback for a paragraph of more than 60,000 items (about 25,000 words) or when no solution exists. A word wider than the measure is cut into pieces no wider than nine tenths of it with a costly break between them, so a line can never be overfull because of one word. Unjustified text is scored by how short each line falls. A hard line break ends its line at natural width. **Not built:** hyphenation (the `hyphenate` option is refused: Liang patterns are licensed data that was not available here and was not written from memory; `soft hyphen` is dropped), paragraph-level `looseness`, optical margins.
- **Leading and the grid.** The leading is the face's natural line height times `line_spacing` (1.1 in the print presets: 11 pt on 13.9 pt). The vertical measures of headings, scene breaks (two lines), images, and the space above and below lists, quotes and code are whole body leadings or paired halves, so ordinary full pages end on the same line and the bottoms of facing pages meet. Table rows (0.9 leading plus padding), footnote areas and a nonzero `space_after_pt` break the grid.
- **Pages.** The first page is a right-hand page. Chapters start on a right-hand page with a blank left-hand one inserted when needed (`chapter_start: recto`), on the next page (`new-page`) or run on (`continuous`; parts, front matter and back matter still start a page). The page count is made even with a blank last page. Front matter (the title, copyright, dedication, epigraph, contents and other front sections) is numbered `i, ii, ...` and the numbering restarts at 1 on the first part or chapter, as PDF page labels too. No number or head on the title page, copyright page, dedication, epigraph or blank pages; chapter openers have a number at the foot and no head. A section titled "Copyright", "Dedication" or "Epigraph" (case folded) gets its page; any other front matter is set as a chapter without a number.
- **Rules about where a page may end.** A paragraph keeps its first two lines and last two lines together (a three-line paragraph is not split); a heading, a chapter opener and a scene break stay with what follows (and a scene break with what precedes); a table's header row stays with its first row and is repeated when the table continues; a table row taller than half a page is cut by lines; an image is never split. When no legal place exists the page ends where it must and `widow_orphan` counts it.
- **Footnotes** are set at the foot of the page their reference is on, under a short rule; a note that cannot fit with its reference moves the line to the next page, and a box alone on a page whose notes do not fit carries the rest onto the following page. `footnotes: chapter` and `book` make endnotes (after each chapter, or one "Notes" section after the last chapter). The note text the layout will set is bounded at 4 MB for the book (on top of the structure's 16 MB copy budget); beyond it a note is a line saying so, with the warning `layout_limit`.
- **What a PDF never holds:** a link annotation, an action, a form, an embedded file, JavaScript, a timestamp the input did not give (the dates are the project's `updated_at`), the user, the machine, or the clock. The document id is a hash of the input hash, the title, the identifier and the page count. Links in the text are text (the address in parentheses with `link_urls`). The Info dictionary has Title, Author, Subject (covers), Producer and Creator ("Scriobh", no version, so a new build does not change the bytes), and the catalog says the language.
- **Images.** JPEG (RGB or grey) is embedded as it is; PNG without alpha or interlacing is embedded as it is (its data stream is read as a Flate stream with a PNG predictor after being checked to inflate to the right size); other PNG and GIF are decoded to 8-bit samples with a soft mask, within 16 million pixels each and 64 million in all; WebP, CMYK JPEG and anything damaged are left out with a grey box and `image_not_embedded` (the preview, which does not read image bytes, lays them out at the same size). A picture is set on its own, centred, at the text width, or at 96 pixels to the inch when it is smaller than that, and at most three quarters of a page tall; `image_low_resolution` reports pictures under 300 dpi at that size (almost every picture taken from the web will be).
- **Numbers that are not a printer's.** The inside margin the program warns about (0.375, 0.5, 0.625, 0.75, 0.875 inch for up to 150, 300, 500, 700 and more pages), the 24-page minimum and the quarter-inch spine that can carry type are typical figures for paperbacks from print-on-demand services, written from general knowledge and **not verified against any printer's guide** (a fetch of one vendor's help page returned none of its numbers). They only produce warnings. The paper's thickness is the one number a cover needs and the one that varies by printer and paper, so it is a required input (`page_thickness_in`), with no table of stocks.

### The paged HTML

One `<style>` whose SHA-256 is in a `Content-Security-Policy` meta (`default-src 'none'; style-src 'sha256-...'; img-src data:`); the hash covers exactly the text between the tags. No script, link, frame, form, remote URL or event handler, a test asserts each; titles and bylines reach CSS only as strings in which every character that is not plain ASCII text is a hexadecimal escape, so nothing can end the string, the rule or the element. Images are `data:` URIs of the project's own PNG, JPEG and GIF (24 MB at most; more is left out with `image_not_embedded`). It uses `@page :left`/`:right` margins, `@top-center` and `@bottom-center` boxes, named pages, `break-before: right` and `target-counter` for engines that have them. Footnotes are endnotes per chapter and the contents have no page numbers in Chromium (the leader and `target-counter` are for Prince, WeasyPrint and Paged.js); **page numbers count from 1 and chapter openers keep their head in Chromium**, which implements neither `counter-reset: page` nor `:first` per named page. Use the PDF for a printer.

### Result shapes, and what a screen must do

- `export.compile` returns the usual `Out`: `format` is `pdf` (content type `application/pdf`, `<slug>-<preset>.pdf`) or `html` (`text/html`, `.html`); `warnings` carry the codes above in `code` (a writer's warning of another format keeps `writer`); `input_hash` is the preview's.
- `compile.preview` for a PDF preset returns `page_count` (the file's) with `page_estimated: false`, `outline[].page` (exact), `running_header` as `author | title`, `pages` empty and `print`: `page_width_pt`, `page_height_pt`, `text_width_pt`, `text_height_pt`, the four margins, `font_size_pt`, `leading_pt`, `lines_per_page`, `media_width_pt` and `media_height_pt` (larger with crop marks), `front_pages`, `body_start`, `leaves`, and `pages`: the first `pages` pages (1 to 12, default 3) each with `number`, `label`, `recto`, `blank` and `lines` (`x`, `y`, `size`, `text`, `kind`: text, heading, note, head, folio, image, table; points from the top left of the trim box, at most 80 lines of 160 characters). A screen draws a proof from them, or shows the PDF.
- `compile.cover_spec` returns `page_count` and its source (`layout` or `given`), `spine_in`, `spine_has_text`, `wrap_width_in`, `wrap_height_in`, the bleed and the three panels in points; `export.cover` returns an `Out` with `preset` `cover/<preset>` and `format` `pdf`. Both need `page_thickness_in`; a screen should ask for the printer's figure and say where it comes from.
- **A PDF viewer screen** must show the file the export returned. The application's own pages have a strict CSP and only the attachment manager allows a frame for a PDF; a proof screen needs the same narrow allowance for a `blob:` URL of the response, or a download link, and must not load the PDF through a third-party viewer.
- **A printer's proof checklist** for that screen: the page size is the trim size; the warnings above are read; the page count is even and in the printer's range; the inside margin suits the page count; every picture is 300 dpi or more at its size; fonts are embedded (the validator says so); the cover's spine is from the printer's own paper figure; and a physical proof is ordered before the print run.

### Not done

Images inside table cells (left out with `table_images`); the book-mode "Notes" section is in neither the contents nor the outline; footnotes referenced from a dedication, epigraph or copyright page (`note_not_set`); hyphenation; PDF/A and PDF/X; tagged PDF and structure for screen readers (the text is searchable and in reading order, the outline and labels exist, nothing more); complex scripts, right-to-left text, ligatures, small caps and old-style figures; an ISBN field, imprint metadata and a barcode (a white box is drawn on request); per-chapter and ranged export; a hardcover wrap; CMYK and ICC; running heads that follow the chapter title; keep-with-next for a table's last row; a re-download of a past export; the screens; the e2e script for paged HTML in `scripts/` (the Chromium check was run by hand, see above).

### Phase 6 print: review fixes

Each has a regression test (named in the commit's report). What changed, and what was concluded.

- **The embedded fonts were not in the repository.** `.gitignore`'s `data/` rule (written for the runtime data directory) also hid `internal/publish/font/data/`, so a clean clone could not build. The rule now has a negation for that directory, the files are tracked, and `internal/repocheck.TestEmbeddedFilesAreTracked` fails when any file a `//go:embed` pattern names is not in the git index (it skips outside a git checkout). Proven on a clean clone: see the report. No other ignored-but-needed file was found (`git status --ignored` lists only `node_modules`).
- **Load.** Compiles (previews, exports, covers) run through a gate: `SCRIOBH_COMPILE_CONCURRENCY` slots (default 2); a request that cannot get one in two seconds is answered 429 "busy, try again" with a retry hint. A cancelled request stops the layout (it looks between sections and every 256 boxes, and between pages when writing). A PDF compile or preview is refused above `SCRIOBH_COMPILE_PDF_MAX_WORDS` (default 600,000; the 2,000,000-word cap stays for Markdown and DOCX), because a 2,000,000-word PDF needs about 2 GB. Animated GIFs are decoded from the first frame only.
- **A cut book is refused, not written.** If the layout had to leave text out (past 20,000 pages, the line or table-cell limits, or the footnote text limit), `export.compile` fails with a plain message and the preview warns (`layout_limit`, "the export will be refused"). The earlier behaviour wrote the file with a silent hole and counted every note as unset.
- **Footnotes split across pages.** A note that does not fit with its reference is divided (at least two lines on each side, none stranded) and its rest continues at the foot of the next page without a rule; a box placed after the split has its notes follow. What must stay with the box (the next line of a paragraph, a heading's text) keeps its room. A chapter opener is no longer emptied by a long note, and a page no longer ends with a hole of many lines before a note-bearing paragraph. Notes in table cells are set; a reference where no note can be set (a dedication page) is reported (`note_not_set`).
- **Code keeps its indentation:** each leading and inner space of a code line is a glyph of the monospace face inside one unbreakable piece, cut only at the measure.
- **The contact name is not an author name in print.** A PDF, a paged HTML, a cover and a book's metadata take the byline option or the project's author and nothing else; with neither, the `no_author` warning is given and no name is used. A DOCX manuscript keeps the contact-name fallback unless `contact: false`, which is honoured everywhere (also for the header's surname). The contact block is in the input hash only when it can reach the file.
- **JPEG:** EXIF/XMP (APP1), IPTC (APP13), JFIF, comments and other application segments are removed; Adobe and ICC markers are kept; bytes after the end-of-image marker are dropped and only a JPEG that ends before it is refused. The EXIF orientation is honoured by the matrix that places the picture (the data is never re-encoded), the layout uses the upright size, and the preview reads the head of the file for the orientation so that it lays the page out the same. DOCX and EPUB pictures are unchanged: they still carry their metadata.
- **Colour:** a PNG with a gamma or colour profile and a JPEG with an ICC profile are embedded as their numbers say, with the warning `image_colour_profile` ("not applied"). No conversion is attempted.
- **PNG pass-through** has the decode path's pixel cap (`publish.MaxImagePixels`, 16 million) and is read a row at a time with the filter byte of each row checked.
- **Paged HTML has no live link:** a link is its label, with the address in parentheses under `link_urls`. The only anchors left are in-page.
- **Layout robustness:** the indent is held to a third of the measure, a drop cap that would take more than a third is skipped, and `Validate` refuses (on options that name a print page) a text block under 2 inches wide or 3 inches tall, or an indent over a third of the measure, in words. Title-page, cover and chapter-title words are never broken: the type shrinks until the widest word fits.
- **RTL:** the Latin-script `ku` is left to right; `ckb`, `ug`, `ar`, `fa`, `he`, `iw`, `ur`, `yi`, `ps`, `sd`, `dv` are right to left, and so is any tag with an explicit right-to-left script subtag (`ku-Arab`).
- **Preview and export keep the same pictures** when their total is over the 192 MB cap: the preview now counts the stored sizes as the export does. `outline[].page` is the physical page (counted over every page, blank ones too) and `outline[].label` the printed number.
- **The input hash names the engine.** `compile/3` and `publish.EngineVersion` (typesetter, fonts, PDF writer, DOCX and EPUB writers) are in it, so the same input hash is the same bytes across upgrades. A change that can alter the bytes must change the string; `TestPDFGolden` refuses to rewrite its pinned hashes unless it has.
- **Font notices.** Each subset keeps the font's copyright notice, licence description and licence address (name ids 0, 13, 14) in a `name` table, which is a few hundred bytes (more for the Vera-licence text of the fallback faces). Conclusion, not legal advice: the OFL says its licence requirements do not apply to a document made with the fonts and permits embedding; the Bitstream Vera / DejaVu licence asks that its notice be in copies of the font software and allows embedding in documents, and keeping the notice in the embedded subset is the conservative reading of that. The Liberation fonts' reserved name is not used for a modified font: the subset keeps the original PostScript name with a subset tag, as PDF subsets do.



## Phase 6 import decisions

Import from Scrivener, WordPress and Ghost. The backend only: `import.preview`, `import.apply`, `import.append_preview` and `import.append` took `layout: scrivener | wordpress | ghost`, six options and new fields in their results; `scriobh import` reads the sources. **No screen is built, and no op was added.** The rows are in [FEATURES § AK](FEATURES.md#ak-implementation-status-phase-6-imports); the code is in `internal/imports` (`convert.go`, `mdsink.go`, `rtf.go`, `rtfmd.go`, `scrivener.go`, `xdom.go`, `htmlmd.go`, `mdbound.go`, `wordpress.go`, `ghost.go`, `convin.go`, `convsrc.go`, `research.go`) and `internal/domain/research/importer.go`.

### What was checked, and what was not

- **Checked:** hand-written fixtures (`internal/imports/testdata`: a Scrivener 3 project and a Scrivener 2 project, a WXR 1.2 file, a Ghost 4 export with mobiledoc and a Ghost 5 export with lexical), each through the preview and the apply into the database, from the command line, as a zip, as a listing, and appended to a project that exists; golden files of everything each converter makes (`testdata/golden`, regenerate with `-update` and read the diff); determinism (four previews of one source are the same bytes and token); the mirror (an imported project's research reaches the mirror tree with its tags); the editor's round trip (the committed bundle reads and writes the converter's private-note forms back unchanged; the source-level check was skipped because `web/editor/node_modules` is not installed here); hostile suites (below); 6,000 random mutations of the fixtures per converter, none a panic.
- **Not checked, and only these can confirm it:** that a **real Scrivener project** (2 or 3), a **real WordPress export** or a **real Ghost export** imports as the fixtures do. No real file was available and nothing was copied from one: every shape is implemented from the format's documentation and from what the author knew of what those programs write, and the fixtures prove only that model. In particular **Scrivener's own markers are recalled, not verified**: inline annotations and comments are recognised as a group that holds `\annotation`, `\comment`, `\Scrv_annot*` or `\Scrv_cmt*` (the underscore is read although RTF does not allow it in a word); footnotes as `\footnote`; links as `\field` with `HYPERLINK "scrivlnk://<id>"` (and `scrivcmt://` as a comment link, kept as text); the binder as `BinderItem` (`UUID` or `ID`, `Type`, `Title`, `MetaData` with `IncludeInCompile`, `LabelID`, `StatusID`, `Keywords`), `Children`, and the settings lists `LabelSettings`, `StatusSettings`, `Keywords`; files as `Files/Data/<UUID>/content.rtf`, `synopsis.txt`, `notes.rtf` and `Files/Docs/<id>.rtf`, `<id>_synopsis.txt`, `<id>_notes.rtf`. Comments Scrivener keeps in a separate per-document file are **not** read (the report lists unread files by type and says so when `.comments` files are present). WordPress: `item` with `wp:post_id`, `wp:post_type`, `wp:status`, `wp:post_name`, `wp:post_date`, `content:encoded`, `excerpt:encoded`, `category` with `domain`, `wp:postmeta`, `wp:comment`, `wp:attachment_url`, the channel's `title`, `description`, `language` and `wp:author`. Ghost: `db[0].data`, bare `data` or bare `posts`; posts with `title`, `slug`, `type` or `page`, `status`, `visibility`, `html`, `lexical`, `mobiledoc`, `markdown`, `custom_excerpt`, `published_at` and `created_at` (text or milliseconds), `tags` with `posts_tags`, `users` with `posts_authors` (names only), `settings` (the `title`).
- **Measured** (Apple silicon, a quiet machine, ordinary prose with formatting, an annotation, a footnote and a link in each scene; the registry, database included): 150,000 words in 330 Scrivener documents with 100 research notes: preview 38 ms, apply 90 ms; WordPress 187 posts, preview 42 ms, apply 71 ms; Ghost, 34 and 58 ms. The converters alone, for 100,000 / 150,000 / 400,000 words: Scrivener 25 / 36 / 93 ms and 66 / 98 / 258 MB allocated, WordPress 26 / 39 / 104 ms, Ghost 20 / 30 / 80 ms: **four times the words take 3.8 to 4.0 times as long and 3.9 to 4.0 times the memory** (asserted: at most six, where a quadratic converter would show sixteen). 40,000 binder items are 4.4 times 10,000. The budgets asserted are generous (the race detector is ten times slower) and catch a slip to quadratic work, not a regression of tens of percent.

### Shape of the work

- **One path.** A converter reads the foreign project into `cdoc` (a document with kind, title, status, synopsis, story date, source path, compile flag, Markdown, children) and `rnote` (a research note) and `toFiles` lays the tree out as the **Scríobh layout** (`manuscript/NN-title/00-title.md`) with a `scriobh.json` that **the converter writes**, never one read from the source: a foreign file cannot name a path, a kind or an entity. So titles (never taken from a heading in the text), kinds, statuses, `include_in_compile`, synopses, story dates and source paths reach the plan through the importer's own manifest reader and its limits, and the preview, token, edits, reordering, caps and provenance are the folder import's. A folder over 9 levels (the importer's path limit) is flattened into the deepest folder allowed, in order, and says so, instead of losing documents as "nested too deeply"; more than 2,000 documents are left out and said so.
- **The token** covers the Markdown, the manifest **and** the research notes (a hash of them is folded in), so an apply cannot differ from its preview in a note the file listing does not show.
