The compared files no longer include `files/projects/<id>/covers/` (the backup leaves it out, by design: `internal/backup`); the rehearsal requires that three derived pictures existed in the source, that none is in the restored directory, that every table (including `cover_layouts`, `attachments`, `attachment_refs`) matches row for row, that the layout's API answer is identical (one answer per project among the 46 compared, up from 44), that the front, the thumbnail and the back are served by the restored server as JPEGs with the same sha-256 as before the backup, that the covers folder holds three derived pictures again afterwards, that the two cover pictures download, and (as before) that the regenerated mirror, which now carries the cover, equals the source's byte for byte. The restore with a different key and the two other rehearsals the script runs (`TestRestoreRehearsalEveryFeature`, the step-order test) passed unchanged.
- **`go vet ./...`**: clean. **`go test -p 2 -count=1 ./cmd/scriobh`** (the package both rehearsals live in, run once after the edits, which changed the shared `capture` helper): passed, 144 s. The rest of `go test ./...` and the other items of the AGENTS.md verify list were not run in this gate.
- **`make bench`** (150k and 500k words, `bench/baseline.json`), run last. Machine `darwin/arm64/16cpu`, so the regression check ran; the report said nothing about a missing entry or a generator mismatch. **The machine was not silent**: the one-minute load average was 2.5 (5 minutes 3.9, 15 minutes 4.2) just before the first run, after a 15-minute wait for it to fall from 7 (the gate's own rehearsals, and builds of other sessions); the machine's ambient level with only the desktop programs running (a video call, a chat client, browsers, the window server) was 2.3 at the start of the session. It was 3.9 to 4.7 during and between the runs (the runs themselves count). No other test run or browser automation was going on that could be seen, but the desktop programs were. The three runs, each `ok` (33 s, 98 s for the best-of-three, 32 s), p50 in ms, with the baseline's in brackets:

  | | 150k, first run | 150k, best of three | 500k, first run | 500k, best of three |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `doc.update` | 2.43 [2.28] | 2.35 | 3.60 [3.06] | 3.29 |
  | `http.save` | 2.49 [2.39] | 2.53 | 3.76 [3.17] | 3.29 |
  | `page.chapter` | 6.49 [5.61] | 6.11 | 18.37 [17.09] | 18.32 |
  | `cover.derive` | 325.4 | 326.9 | 328.1 | 327.6 |

  The save path (`doc.update`, `http.save`) is within 7% of the baseline at 150k and, in the first 500k run, 18% to 19% over it, which is inside the 20% tolerance and below the 5 ms noise floor; in the best of three it is 8% and 4% over. The p95s are 3.3 to 4.7 ms against budgets of 120 ms (`doc.update`) and 400 ms (`http.save`). Nothing is a regression and the existing numbers are close to the baseline; the first 500k run was taken while the load average was rising from 2.5 to 3.9, and the machine was not quiet enough to say whether the 0.5 ms is the load or the code. `cover.derive` (make the front of a 300-dpi 6x9 wrap, 3825 by 2775 px, from nothing kept) is 0.33 s at p50 and at most 0.39 s at p95 in every run, against the budget of 1.5 s.
- **The baseline.** `cover.derive` had no entry. It was recorded for `darwin/arm64/16cpu` from the best of three runs at 150k and 500k (3 timings each, as the metric is timed `max(3, runs/10)` times): 326.9 ms p50 and 341.5 ms p95 at 150k, 327.6 and 331.2 at 500k. This was **not** done with `--write-baseline`, which replaces every metric of the sizes it runs (and the entry's `recorded` and `go` fields): the candidate of `scriobh bench --best-of 3 --candidate` was written beside the baseline and only the two `cover.derive` objects were put into `bench/baseline.json`, as the studio metrics were added in `d61a948d`. `git diff --numstat bench/baseline.json` is 12 insertions and 0 deletions; no other entry moved (none was refreshed, stale or not), and the entry's `recorded` date is still 2026-11-15. `make bench` after the change loads the entries and judges `cover.derive` against them: `ok`, 32 s, `cover.derive` 326.2 ms at 150k and 329.1 ms at 500k.

What the gate found and fixed:

- **Nothing in the product.** The one failure was in the rehearsal's own data: the first version of the picture step wrote the picture (a `document.updated`) after the webhook existed, so the old release's webhook answer was captured with one delivery pending (`pending: 1, last_status: 0`) and read `pending: 0, last_status: 204` after the upgrade (and after the rollback), which the rehearsal compares. The rehearsal on the unmodified tree passed twice and then failed on the new step. The picture is now written before the webhook is created, so the comparison is as it was. Why the old release leaves a delivery pending for several seconds with a 50 ms debounce was not looked into: it is how every older rehearsal already behaved when a document changed after a webhook exists, and a delivery does happen once the new build starts.


What the pre-release review found, and what was fixed before the tag (branch `pre-2.2-fixes`, each with a test that fails on the old code):

- **The cover form's idle limit counted the server's own work.** A front-and-back save on a slow machine could fail with every byte sent; the read deadline is now set at the start of every read.
- **A trickle could hold an upload slot for 15 minutes.** After 30 s waiting, a body averaging under 32 KiB/s is dropped (a 25 MiB picture at that rate finishes in 13 minutes, inside the ceiling).
- **A rotated JPEG kept a gray or CMYK colour profile on RGB pixels** (every attachment, not only covers). Only an RGB profile now crosses the rotation.
- **The publish leak gate took `.mp4`, `.webp` and `.webm` on their extension.** It now reads inside them and refuses compressed or unknown boxes and chunks (a `cmov` MP4 and a zlib WebP chunk were shown to pass before).
- **The GitHub release left an old-named Linux package beside a new one on a re-run**, and **the AUR recipe's file name broke for a prerelease tag**. Both fixed.

What the print cover is in v2.2.0: the paperback cover PDF (`export.cover`) still uses the generated cover. The writer's own cover reaches screens, the EPUB, the HTML export and the site; print (phase C2 of [COVERS.md](COVERS.md)) is not built. The Compile page says so.

Not verified:

- **The 1M-word bench**, and `cover.derive` at 1m (the baseline's `1m` entries have none; the metric is only timed at the sizes the Makefile builds). `make soak`, `make ci`, the browser scripts, the contrast check, the CSP smoke test, the desktop smoke test, `go test ./...` as a whole and the race pass were not run. The AGENTS.md checks at 390 px, keyboard-only and with the network cut were not run.
- **A quiet bench machine** (above): the load average was 2.5 before the first run and about 4 during the runs, with the ambient desktop programs. The baseline was recorded on a quiet machine on 15 November. Each number above is one run on this machine at this load.
- **GitHub's `v2.1.1` as a tag.** It was not rehearsed; the migration set from v2.0 to `main` is exactly `0810` and `1837` (`git diff --name-status v2.0 main -- internal/db/migrations`), and a database from that release has the schema v2.0 had.
- **`internal/db/testdata` has no fixture for v1.1, v1.2 or v2.0** (`releaseFixtures` stops at v1.0, and the notes of Phase 8 say so). The rehearsal covers them; `go test ./internal/db` does not.
- **The cover tables with attachments from v0.1 to v0.4** (no rich dataset), **a public remote on v0.5 and v1.0** (no audience before v1.1), and **a cover pushed to a public remote** (the rehearsal's remotes are never given a layout: `1837` only sets the flag).
- **The restore of a backup taken while a cover was being made, and a cover with a wrap layout.** The rehearsal restores one layout of sides with two pictures; a wrap and the right-to-left key are covered by the unit tests only.

Decided:

- **v2.0 is in the default list** of the upgrade rehearsal (as v1.1 and v1.2 were added when they were tagged; v2.0 is the last forge release). `--all` already took every tag, so nothing else was needed for it.
- **The rehearsals check the cover migrations on real rows, not by adding another test for them.** The checks are in the rehearsal's own flow (`checkCoverTables` after the upgrade, `readCoverFacts` before it), so a later release that touches these tables is checked by every tag's run.
- **A private remote carries the cover (`include_cover` 1) and a public one does not (0), including after the upgrade**, as migration `1837` says; a remote from before the audience existed is private, so it has it on. The rehearsal asserts exactly that, in both directions, on the old release's own remotes.
- **The derived cover pictures are compared by what they are, not as backed-up files**: absent from the archive, rebuilt by the restored server with the same bytes. A comparison of the whole `files/` tree would have failed the moment a cover existed.
- **`cover.derive`'s baseline is the best of three and is added by hand-merging the candidate**, so the other entries stay as they were (see above). The next phase that adds a metric should do the same.

## Release notes for v2.2.1

A small release so the new landing page can go live. No migration, no change to stored data.

- **The landing page and README are rewritten** in plain words. AI being off until you add a key and switch it on, per project and per feature, is stated at the top. The page offers the download for the visitor's system (a small self-hosted script; without it, every download is listed), and the technical detail sits in one section at the end. Its download links are written in when the page deploys, from the latest GitHub Release, so a visitor's browser asks GitHub's API nothing.
- **A universal macOS disk image** (Apple silicon and Intel in one) is built on GitHub, and is the macOS default on the page.
- **The Writing Studio's streak sentences** read as sentences ("8 days in a row so far. Write 300 more today to keep it going.").
- **A sponsor link** at the top of the README.

Not verified: the universal macOS leg and the page's deploy-time download step have run only locally until this release's GitHub runs; real Safari, Firefox and phones against the page's platform detection.

## Desktop updates: decisions

The design is [UPDATES.md](UPDATES.md), written on 17 November 2026; phases U1 (the pipeline) and U2 (the shell) are built. The owner took these decisions on 17 November 2026 (the file has the reasons and what each led to). **None of the open items below is "still open" any more except the ones marked as later.**

1. **Consent (invariant 7): decided.** One first-run question, off until answered, plus an always-visible switch in the Help menu. Checks about daily; one plain GET with no identifiers. The documents now say: the app looks about once a day, only after you said yes. Invariant 7 in AGENTS.md carries the clause (item 13).
2. **Which builds can contact GitHub: decided.** Only GitHub release builds for a tag, with the endpoint and the public key injected at build time. The forge, source and development builds have no update source.
3. **macOS signing: decided for now.** Accept the keychain prompt on each update and make it gentle: the restart question and the docs say that macOS asks once for the login password and to choose Always Allow. A Developer ID may come later (phase U3 keeps the notes). Nothing is notarised.
4. **Windows signing: decided for now.** Unsigned. The release notes and the docs say that SmartScreen and Smart App Control may warn or block.
5. **The update key: made.** The owner approved it; it was made on 17 November 2026. The private key and its password are repository Actions secrets on the published repository (`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`, `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSWORD`), not in an Environment; the plan's Environment restriction (tags and `main`, the owner as reviewer) can be added later. The public key is `desktop/updater.pub`. The staging repository needs its own throwaway key (UPDATES.md, "The staging repository").
6. **Linux `.deb`: decided.** A notice now; the signed apt repository (phase U6) is later.
7. **When an update installs: decided.** Only when the person chooses Restart to Update.
8. **Where the notice shows: decided.** The menu first (the macOS application menu and the Help menu); the in-page line (U5) is later.
9. **Channels: decided.** Stable only; a prerelease is never offered.
10. **Flathub: decided.** Later.
11. **The bridge release: decided.** The first release that ships the updater, **v2.3.0** (the owner confirmed; the release workflow's `BRIDGE_TAG` is `v2.3.0`). Its notes tell people on v2.2.x or earlier to install it by hand once.
12. **A staging repository: decided against, for now** (the owner, after the first report). UPDATES.md, "The staging repository", says what one would need; the bridge release is the first real run.
13. **Invariant 7: amended by the owner** with the clause "except the desktop app's update check, which exists only after the person says yes, and only in GitHub release builds".
14. **Per-chip Mac updates: decided.** The universal `.dmg` bakes the update source in and asks the question; it updates to the package of its own architecture (it stops being universal; the app is the same).

## Release notes for v2.3.0

Two things are new. You can make the paperback cover PDF from your own cover layout. And the desktop app can update itself from GitHub, after you say yes. There is no migration and no change to stored data.

**Print covers.** The Compile page has a cover section. Pick a printer profile (KDP, IngramSpark or generic) and give the paper's thickness. Scríobh counts the pages from the book, works out the spine, draws the cover from your own pictures, and says in words what looks wrong: a picture that is too small, a spine that does not fit, text too close to the edge. You get three downloads. The first is the file for the printer, one page with the front, spine and back together. The second is the front alone. The third is a proof for you, with the folds, the safe margin and the barcode box drawn on it. The interior PDF can start with your cover page, and the EPUB can end with your back cover. Both are off until you switch them on. A book for a public Git remote still uses the generated cover everywhere, as before.

**Desktop updates.** The first time the app opens it asks one question. The safe answer is the first button, and closing the dialog counts as no. If you say yes, the app asks github.com about once a day whether a newer release exists. It sends nothing about you or your writing. A newer version is downloaded in the background, checked against the update key built into the app, and installed only when you choose Restart to Update. The Help menu always has a switch for it. Only full releases are offered, never a prerelease. Only the GitHub release builds can do any of this. The source and development builds have no update address.

Limits you should know about:

- **No printer has seen a cover from Scríobh.** No KDP or IngramSpark previewer was given a file and nothing was printed. The sizes come from the printers' published figures and are tested as arithmetic. Whether a printer accepts the file, and whether the barcode box and the spine type sit where it wants them, is only known by uploading one. There is no CMYK conversion. The file is RGB.
- **The desktop builds are not signed.** On macOS the app is signed ad hoc and not notarised, so Gatekeeper makes you open it by hand the first time. After each update macOS asks once for your login password so Scríobh can open its key again. Choose Always Allow. This is from Apple's documentation and the code, and nobody has seen the prompt yet.
- **On Windows, SmartScreen may warn and Smart App Control may block the installer.** The installer is unsigned. Nothing has run on Windows.
- **A `.deb` or Arch install is told that a newer version exists and nothing more.** Update it the way you installed it. A signed apt repository is planned for later.

**The first update is by hand.** People on v2.2.x or earlier install this release by hand, once, and later versions update themselves after they say yes. Older apps have no real update key built in (v2.2.0 has a placeholder), so they cannot check an update at all. The release workflow writes this paragraph into the notes of the Release whose tag is `BRIDGE_TAG` (`v2.3.0`), and [UPDATES.md](UPDATES.md), decision 11, quotes it. The words are the same in all three places (checked by comparing them, apart from the bold on the first sentence in the workflow):

> If you already have Scríobh 2.2.x or earlier, install this release by hand, once. Those versions cannot update themselves, so this one is the first that can. Download the file for your system below and open it the way you installed the first time: on macOS drag Scriobh to Applications and replace the old one; on Windows run the installer (it replaces the old version); on Linux install the `.deb` with your package manager or replace the AppImage. Your writing is kept: it lives in the data folder, not in the app. From here on the app can update itself, if you say yes when it asks.

If you change the tag the bridge sits on, change `BRIDGE_TAG` in `scripts/publish/release.yml` and the quotation in UPDATES.md together.

### The gate

The gate for the coming `v2.3.0` tag, run on branch `release-2.3` (a worktree at `/private/tmp/scriobh-rel23`; nothing is pushed or tagged). The branch is `main` at `4913b51c` (the v2.2.1 notes) plus 46 commits: print covers (phase C2 and its Compile page) and desktop auto-update (phases U1 and U2), merged, and then the gate's own commits: the rehearsal fix `1e8517ae`, and these notes with the README and roadmap lines. The run was on 17 November 2026 on darwin/arm64, 16 cpus, Go 1.27.4, Rust 1.100.0, Node 26.13.0, with the short `PATH` of the task. Everything ran with `GOMAXPROCS=2` and `TEST_PARALLEL=2`, and the Rust build had a private target folder that was deleted afterwards. This is a wider gate than the one for v2.2.0, but not all of `make ci`: the timing tests were off in the plain pass, and the 390 px, keyboard-only and network-cut walk was not run as one check of the new pages (see "Not verified").

What was run, with the time of each:

- **Static checks:** `make workflows-check actionlint fmt-check vet skill-check publish-check release-check updater-check` passed (76 s together). The release steps ran against a fake `gh`, and the manifest script's 33 tests passed. `PUBLISH_CHECK_BUILD=1 bash scripts/publish-check.sh` passed (64 s): the exported tree builds, vets, is gofmt-clean and matches its skill snapshot. `go test ./internal/repocheck` passed (1.7 s).
- **Plain pass, whole tree:** `make editor-deps`, then `SCRIOBH_TIMING=off GOMAXPROCS=2 TEST_PARALLEL=2 TEST_COUNT= make test-plain`: 79 packages ok, none cached, 488 s. The server package took 191 s and `cmd/scriobh` 142 s.
- **Race pass:** `make test-race-core` passed in 265 s: the thirteen core packages, the import limiter and the server's streams.
- **Desktop:** `desktop/scripts/test.sh` passed, 153 tests including the ones against the real server binary (77 s). One test was ignored, `keychain::tests::real_keychain_round_trip`, which needs the real keychain and is not run in the gate. Clippy with `-D warnings` was clean (32 s) and `cargo fmt --check` was clean. `make desktop-smoke` passed (16 s). `make desktop-shell-smoke` passed all 68 checks (26 s). It opens a real window against the stand-in server.
- **Browser:** `make e2e-fast` passed from the first script to the last, in 30 min 8 s, with no failure: 683 passing steps. Each of the 42 scripts that report findings said "no console, CSP or off-origin findings" (the cover scripts `e2e-covers` and `e2e-compile-print` among them, 19 and 9 steps). The accessibility audit walked 160 pages in both themes at 1280 and 390 px with 0 violations (17 excused, as listed in the audit). `scripts/csp-smoke.mjs` passed all 96 checks (138 s). `scripts/check-contrast.py` passed: 5 themes, 0 failing pairs.
- **Upgrade rehearsal:** `scripts/upgrade-rehearsal.sh --all` passed for all twelve tags (v0.1, v0.2, v0.3, v0.3.5, v0.4, v0.5, v1.0, v1.1, v1.2, v2.0, v2.2.0, v2.2.1), 48 s, on the clean commit. **This release adds no migration**: `git diff v2.2.1 HEAD -- internal/db/migrations` is empty and the database holds the same 87 migrations. So for v2.2.0 and v2.2.1 the rehearsal requires exactly that: none applied, no pre-migration copy, a second start that applies nothing, and the old binary starting on the upgraded database. For v2.0 and older it applies `0810` and `1837` as before. v2.2.0 and v2.2.1 are now in the default list (with v0.1, v0.5, v1.0, v1.1, v1.2 and v2.0).
- **Restore rehearsal:** `scripts/restore-rehearsal.sh` passed, 94 s.
- **Bench:** `make bench` (150k and 500k words, `bench/baseline.json`) passed, 32 s, run last. The one-minute load average was 1.5 before and 1.3 after, with only desktop programs running (a browser, a chat client). That is the quietest this machine was during the gate. Machine class `darwin/arm64/16cpu`, so the regression check ran. p50 in ms, with the baseline in brackets:

  | | 150k | 500k |
  |---|---|---|
  | `doc.update` | 2.17 [2.28] | 2.95 [3.06] |
  | `http.save` | 2.28 [2.39] | 3.02 [3.17] |
  | `page.chapter` | 5.71 [5.61] | 17.00 [17.09] |
  | `cover.derive` | 329.5 [326.9] | 330.1 [327.6] |

  Nothing is more than 2% over the baseline at p50, and the save path is 4% to 5% under it. I ran `make bench` twice more at the same load (1.4 to 1.9) and the p50s repeated within 1% (`page.chapter` at 500k: 17.00, 16.87, 17.05; `cover.derive` 323 to 330 ms). The p95s of the page metrics are higher than the baseline's and they repeat: `page.chapter` at 500k was 35.7, 32.6 and 33.5 ms against 17.9 in the baseline, at 150k 8.2 to 8.7 against 6.9, and `page.project` at 500k 31 to 33 ms. This is not this release. I ran the same bench twice on `main` at `4913b51c` (v2.2.1, in a temporary worktree, removed afterwards), at the same load, and it gave 37.0 and 37.4 ms for `page.chapter` at 500k, 8.4 and 8.5 at 150k and 32.3 for `page.project`. The baseline's p95s were taken in a calmer run. What makes the tail is not looked into. The budget is 150 ms for `page.chapter` and 400 ms for `page.project`, and the check passes. **No new metric was added**, so the baseline is untouched. The print cover (`export.cover`) is not in the bench. Its time was not measured by this gate. If it is added, do it the way `cover.derive` was: hand-merge the candidate of `scriobh bench --best-of 3 --candidate`, so no other entry moves.

What the gate found and fixed:

- **The upgrade rehearsal failed for v2.2.0 and v2.2.1, and the code was fine.** Every other check in the run passed (the migrations, the schema, the 69 answers, the second start), and then the attachment check failed with a message that printed the same row twice. The check read an attachment as `id|sha256|cover_clean`. For a release from before migration `0810` there is no `cover_clean` column, so the old row ends in a bar, and the check expected the new row to start with that row plus a bar. A release that has the column (v2.2.0 is the first) has the value already, so its row is identical after the upgrade and never starts with itself plus a bar. The check now requires the row to be kept exactly when the old release has the column, and to gain the value when it does not (`cmd/scriobh/upgrade_rich_test.go`). No product code changed. The tags that passed before (v0.5 to v2.0) pass as they did.

Nothing else failed. The desktop smoke test, which the branch's history says used to fail about half of its runs, ran once here and passed; that is one run and not a measurement.

Not verified:

- **Any update, on any OS, from a real release.** No update has been fetched from GitHub, downloaded, verified in the running app or installed. The schedule, the consent, the install-or-notify decision, the cache and the order of a restart are tested as functions and against stand-in downloads. The plugin's own calls are not. v2.3.0 is the first real run of all of it, and the first update after it is the real test.
- **The macOS keychain prompt**: its wording, whether Always Allow behaves as described, whether an ad hoc app replacing itself in `/Applications` triggers macOS's App Management prompt, and that a downloaded update carries no quarantine mark (and no Mark of the Web on Windows). All of these are read from code and documentation, not seen.
- **Windows, as to running anything**: the installer replacing the running app and restarting it, WebView2's absence, Smart App Control's behaviour with this installer. **Linux**: the AppImage updating under `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN`, `app.restart()` starting the new AppImage, and the `.deb` signature files being where the scripts look. The universal macOS build, and the split into a build with no key and a bundle step with it, ran for arm64 on this Mac only. Intel was not seen at all.
- **The GitHub side, as to running:** the draft Release and its publish step, `latest.json` last, the repository secrets, and the redirect GitHub gives to its storage host. They run against a fake `gh` here. No staging repository was made (the owner decided against one).
- **The menu and the two dialogs in a real window**: the checked item, the changing text, the first question's button order and its wait for a page where nobody is typing. Save-now before a restart was run against the real server and in Chromium, and not in WKWebView or WebView2.
- **The print cover at a printer.** No previewer, no printed proof, no CMYK. The proof's small type was read at 110 dpi on a screen, not on paper. The drawing's generated type was compared by eye with the file's, not measured. The EPUB's back cover was not opened in a reading system and EPUBCheck was not run (it is not installed here). The interior PDF's cover page is for a proof or a copy you print yourself, and is not meant for a printer as the interior.
- **Screen readers.** The Compile and cover pages were not used with one. The accessibility audit is automatic and keyboard-driven, not a person.
- **The timing tests did not run in the plain pass.** `SCRIOBH_TIMING=off` makes every test that calls `testutil.Timing` skip itself, as the push gate does. They run in the nightly workflow with it unset. This gate's own timing evidence is the bench, and the race pass (which is not timing-gated).
- **A cover layout made in v2.2.x and printed by v2.3.0 after the upgrade.** The rehearsal gives the old release no layout (which is why "no layout" holds for those tags), so it does not carry a layout across. The tables did not change, and the unit tests read layouts, but a real layout from v2.2.x through this build was not tried.
- **What this gate did not run:** `make e2e-extra`, `make soak` and the 1M-word bench, and AGENTS.md's 390 px, keyboard-only and network-cut walk as one check of the new pages (the Compile page and its cover section are not served offline). The race detector ran on the core packages only, not the whole tree.
- **Bench machine:** a quiet laptop by this machine's standard, not a silent one. The numbers are one run each.

Decided:

- **The attachment check compares by what the old release has**: kept exactly if it has `cover_clean`, extended if not. A later release that touches this table is checked the same way for every tag.
- **v2.2.0 and v2.2.1 are in the rehearsal's default list**, as v2.0 was when it was tagged.
- **Left for the owner: the app's version number in the source.** `desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` and `desktop/package.json` still say 2.2.0, and so does "Version 2.2.0" in [DESKTOP.md](DESKTOP.md). The release workflow stamps the tag's version into every package, as it did for v2.2.1 (whose files also said 2.2.0), so the packages say 2.3.0 whatever those files hold. The runbook (`docs/p8-notes/release-runbook.md`, "Version") says to set all three and commit before the tag, and the AUR recipe's `pkgver` follows `tauri.conf.json`. Bumping them touches the AUR files and `Cargo.lock`, which this gate did not do unasked.
- **Left for the owner: the runbook's "Before announcing" line.** It asks for the staging repository's round trip once per system, and says it has not been done. The owner decided against a staging repository (decision 12 above), so v2.3.0 is the first real run, and the line is out of date once the owner accepts that.
- **No bench metric for the print cover yet.** It was not added by the cover work and the gate does not add one.
